# Decisions

Each entry: date · decision · alternatives · why. Newest entries go at the bottom of their section. Business-rule changes are marked **[rule change]**; UX changes **[ux]**; improvements **[improvement]**; dependency additions outside the approved list **[dep]**.

## Process

- **2026-09-29 · Superpowers followed by hand.** The plugin install was not possible in this environment (no `/plugin` command, the org catalog has no match). The skills were cloned from `github.com/obra/superpowers` into the session scratchpad and followed manually: brainstorming → spec → plans → subagent-driven TDD execution → verification → review. Alternative: skip the workflow. Why: the user asked for that workflow.
- **2026-09-29 · Review cadence.** Tasks are sized as cohesive slices (a package or a feature). Each task gets an implementer subagent plus a task reviewer subagent, and a whole-phase review runs between phases. Alternative: one reviewer per micro-step. Why: roughly 100 micro-tasks × 2 reviews would cost more than it catches; phase reviews keep the quality gate.
- **2026-09-29 · Brainstorming questions answered from the prompt.** Every open question was resolved from the user's brief; where the brief was silent the choice below was made.

## Toolchain and versions

- **2026-09-29 · Newest stable version of every package** (user request). A deviation is only allowed for a concrete incompatibility and is listed here.
- **2026-09-29 · TypeScript 7.x** (native `tsc`), per the user's request. TS 7 drops the classic JS compiler API. If a tool needs that API, that tool alone gets a scoped `typescript6` alias (`npm:typescript@6`); none is known yet.
- **2026-09-29 · Keep knip 6** for unused files, exports and dependencies (it uses oxc and does not depend on TypeScript, so it is compatible with TS 7). Dependency direction is enforced by a small Bun script, `scripts/check-boundaries.ts`, against the graph in `@smog/config`. Alternatives: drop knip (loses unused export/file/dependency detection), or dependency-cruiser (covers direction only and parses with the TS API when present). Why: no other tool covers workspace-wide unused code; the script needs no new dependency.
- **2026-09-29 · Vitest 4.x for the Workers pool.** `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers@0.22` requires `vitest ^4.1`; Vitest 5 is used nowhere else (pure packages use `bun test`). Revisit when the pool supports 5.
- **2026-09-29 · NativeWind 4.2.7 (stable) with Tailwind 3.4.19 on Expo SDK 57.** Verified in phase 1: `expo export` for iOS and Android compiles the classes (the bundle's injected stylesheet has `text-brand` → `#00805f`, `dark:` as a `prefers-color-scheme` media rule) and the JSX goes through `react-native-css-interop`'s runtime, with Reanimated 4.5.1 / Worklets 0.10.1 and the `babel-preset-expo` + `nativewind/babel` setup. Tailwind 3 lives in the mobile app only; Bun's hoisted linker nests Tailwind 4 under `@tailwindcss/*` for the site. Alternative: NativeWind 5.0.0-rc.0 + `react-native-css` 3.1.0-rc.0 + Tailwind 4 (targets exactly Expo 57, but still a release candidate). Revisit when 5.0 is stable (one Tailwind major across the repo). Not verified here: rendering on a device or simulator (none available in this environment).
- **2026-09-29 · Bun stays at 1.3.11 for now (deviation).** `bun upgrade` to 1.4.2 failed in this environment (the GitHub release download is blocked by the egress proxy, HTTP 403), so `packageManager` pins the installed `bun@1.3.11`. Alternative: pin 1.4.2 without being able to run it (CI would then test a version nobody ran locally). Revisit: bump `packageManager` to the newest 1.x once the toolchain can be upgraded.
- **2026-09-29 · TypeScript 7 `tsc -b` works with shared bases.** Library bases are `composite` and emit declarations only, into `${configDir}/node_modules/.cache/tsc` (TS 7 supports `${configDir}`), so nothing lands next to sources or in `dist/`. `worker.json` adds `${configDir}/worker-configuration.d.ts` (the `wrangler types` output) to `files` instead of depending on `@cloudflare/workers-types`. Alternatives: `--noEmit` per package (breaks project references) or a per-package `outDir` (repeated in every package).
- **2026-09-29 · Root Bun scripts are checked by root Turborepo tasks.** `//#test:scripts` (`bun test scripts`) and `//#check-types:scripts` (`tsc -p scripts`) run beside the package tasks in `bun run test` / `bun run check-types`. They are uncached because root tasks do not hash other workspaces' files (the scripts import `@smog/config`). Package `check-types`/`test` depend on a `transit` node, as the installed Turborepo docs recommend, so they run in parallel but still miss the cache when a dependency changes.
- **2026-09-29 · Workers tests use `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` (the renamed pool).** `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` stopped at 0.22.0 (2026-08-18) and pins wrangler 4.124; Cloudflare's current docs and releases use `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` 1.x (same `cloudflareTest()` API, `import { exports } from "cloudflare:workers"` instead of the deprecated `SELF`), which pins the same wrangler as `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` 1.62 requires (4.143). It still needs Vitest ^4.1, so the Vitest 4.x exception stands. Alternative: the old package name (stale, a second wrangler/miniflare copy).
- **2026-09-29 · Site `worker-configuration.d.ts` is generated, not committed.** `check-types` runs `wrangler types && tsc -b` (offline; it only reads `wrangler.jsonc`), the file is gitignored, and `apps/site/turbo.json` lists it as the `check-types` output (its inputs are the default package files, which include `wrangler.jsonc`). It covers all envs, so each binding is optional on `Env` because the top level has none. `test` and `build` do not need it. Run `bun -F @smog/site cf-typegen` for editor types after a fresh clone. `src/routeTree.gen.ts` (small, stable, TanStack Router) stays committed. Why: the 600 KB file changes whenever a binding is added (every phase), which means noisy diffs and merge conflicts. Alternative: commit it and run `wrangler types --check` in CI.

- **2026-09-29 · React 19.2.3 across the repo (deviation from 19.3.0).** Expo SDK 57 pins `react` 19.2.3 (React Native 0.86.3 ships the 19.2.3 renderer, `jest-expo` pins `react-test-renderer` 19.2.3), and `react-dom` must equal `react` exactly. Two React versions in one hoisted monorepo would give shared packages (feature `./client` hooks, UI kits) a second copy of React in the mobile bundle. The catalog therefore pins `react`/`react-dom` 19.2.3 and `@types/react` 19.2.18 (`@types/react-dom` 19.2.7 in the site). Alternative: 19.3.0 for the site only, with Metro/Jest resolver pins in the app (two Reacts to keep apart forever). Revisit with the next Expo SDK.
- **2026-09-29 · React Native 0.86.3, not 0.87.** Expo SDK 57 (`expo` 57.0.26) bundles RN 0.86.3; RN 0.87.1 is on npm but belongs to the next SDK. The app uses the versions `expo install --check` selects.
- **2026-09-29 · Jest 29 for the mobile app (deviation from Jest 30).** `jest-expo` 57 depends on the Jest 29 packages (`babel-jest`, `@jest/globals`, `jest-environment-jsdom` ^29). Tests import from `@jest/globals` instead of `@types/jest` globals. Revisit when `jest-expo` moves to 30.
- **2026-09-29 · TypeScript 7 in the mobile app; `typescript` is excluded from Expo's dependency-version check.** `tsc --noEmit` passes with `expo/tsconfig.base` + `@smog/config/tsconfig/react-native.json` (composite/declaration turned off, since the app is not referenced by other projects), and `expo/types` is listed in `types` so CSS imports type-check on a fresh clone without the generated `expo-env.d.ts`. expo-doctor *does* check the TypeScript version online: SDK 57's api.expo.dev list pins `typescript ~6.0.3`, and the first CI run failed on it (the earlier claim that doctor has no TypeScript check came from an offline run, where that list is not fetched). `apps/mobile/package.json` sets `expo.install.exclude: ["typescript"]`, the mechanism doctor's advice names ("To ignore specific packages, add them to expo.install.exclude"; `@expo/cli` `validateDependenciesVersions` filters it). Metro, Babel (`babel-preset-expo` strips types) and Jest do not use the TypeScript JS API, so `expo export` and the Jest suite pass on TS 7. Alternative: a `typescript6` alias for the app only (two compilers, and the repo standard is TS 7).
- **2026-09-29 · No `EXPO_USE_PRECOMPILED_MODULES=0` in `eas.json`.** Precompiled Expo modules are on by default for iOS since SDK 56 and Android since SDK 53; source builds are only needed for patched native code or custom Reanimated/Worklets feature flags, and the app has neither. Alternative: keep the old app's opt-out (slower builds, no known reason).
- **2026-09-29 · Kebab-case file names in the mobile app** (`src/app-config.test.ts`, `plugins/with-screen-capture-permissions.js`), because the Ultracite `useFilenamingConvention` rule applies everywhere except Expo Router's route files. `global.css` gets a Biome override that allows the `@tailwind` at-rule (Tailwind 3 syntax), and the placeholder `apps/mobile/assets` are excluded from Biome.
- **2026-09-29 · `bun audit --production` ignores three moderate advisories (`bun run audit`).** Bun 1.3.11's `--production` does not drop dev dependencies from the report, and all three have no fix we can take: GHSA-3wwx-pv8p-q78v (undici 7.29.0 WebSocket deflate DoS) is pinned exactly by wrangler / miniflare / `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` 4.143 (newest), is local dev tooling, and is not in the Worker bundle; an `undici` override would force srvx (8.x) and RN dev-middleware (6.x) onto one version. GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq (uuid 7.0.3 via `expo`/`expo-splash-screen`, only when a caller passes `buf`) and GHSA-vcc3-ghjq-m6fr (decode-uri-component 0.2.2 via `expo-router`) come from Expo SDK 57; the fixed versions are majors (uuid 11, decode-uri-component 0.5 is ESM-only) that the CJS consumers may not load. Alternatives: overrides (risk breaking Expo), or not gating on audit. Remove each `--ignore` when wrangler or the next Expo SDK moves.
- **2026-09-29 · root `overrides` pins `undici` to 7.29.1.** undici 7.29.0 (pinned exactly by miniflare, hence wrangler, `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` and `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`) gained nine advisories (two high) fixed in 7.29.1, and `bun audit` failed on them. No stable wrangler release takes the fix (wrangler 4.144.0 only moves miniflare to another 5.x alpha, the same alpha line already in use), so a bump was rejected in favor of an override to the stable patched undici. The lockfile holds a single undici (7.x), so the override touches no other major (srvx and React Native dev-middleware do not resolve their own copy). It applies to dev tooling only; the Worker bundle does not include undici. Remove the override when miniflare ships undici >= 7.29.1. The GHSA-3wwx-pv8p-q78v ignore above is now redundant but left until wrangler moves.
- **2026-09-29 · `bun run audit` also ignores GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99 (esbuild ≤ 0.24.2, moderate).** It comes only from `drizzle-kit` 0.31.11 (newest stable) → `@esbuild-kit/esm-loader` → `@esbuild-kit/core-utils` (deprecated) → `esbuild` 0.18.20. The advisory is about `esbuild serve` answering cross-origin requests; drizzle-kit uses esbuild to load `drizzle.config.ts` and the schema and never starts a server, and it is a devDependency of `@smog/db` only (not in any bundle). Alternatives: a root `esbuild` override (would also move the exact esbuild versions wrangler and Vite pin), or drizzle-kit 1.0 rc (not stable). Remove the ignore when drizzle-kit drops `@esbuild-kit`.
- **2026-10-02 · `bun run audit` also ignores GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv (node-forge ≤ 1.4.0, high).** RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signature verification accepts extra nested DigestAlgorithm elements. node-forge 1.4.0 is the newest release (no fixed version exists). It reaches the lockfile only through Expo SDK 57 tooling: `@expo/cli` (dev-server certificates) and `expo-updates` → `@expo/code-signing-certificates` (signing updates at build/publish time). It is not in the exported iOS/Android bundles (checked: no node-forge code in `apps/mobile/dist`) and not in the Worker; the app does not verify update signatures with it at runtime. Alternatives: an override (no fixed version to override to), or not gating on audit. Remove the ignore when node-forge ships a fix or Expo drops it.
- **2026-10-03 · `bun run audit` also ignores GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm (braces ≤ 3.0.3, high).** Stack exhaustion on deeply nested brace patterns, published 2026-10-03; braces 3.0.3 is the newest release (no fixed version exists). It reaches the lockfile only through build and test tooling that expands glob patterns we write ourselves: micromatch/fast-glob (ultracite, Tailwind's chokidar, Jest, Expo, the TanStack Start build) and react-email's tooling. No runtime code passes user input to it; it is not in the Worker or the app bundles (checked: not in `apps/mobile/dist`). Alternatives: an override (nothing to override to), or not gating on audit. Remove the ignore when braces ships a fix.
- **2026-09-29 · `SMOG_OFFLINE=1` for expo-doctor's network checks (local only, not equivalent to CI).** expo-doctor 1.20 has no flag to skip a check; its documented env vars are `EXPO_DOCTOR_ENABLE_DIRECTORY_CHECK` (README) and `EXPO_DOCTOR_WARN_ON_NETWORK_ERRORS` (`build/utils/env.d.ts`, only for `fetch failed`/`ENOTFOUND`). Behind an egress proxy the schema fetch gets a 403 text body, which crashes the check with a JSON error that doctor does not count as a network error. Offline, `scripts/mobile-release-check.ts` sets both env vars, and three checks are degraded, which the script prints on every offline run: (1) the config schema check is tolerated only when doctor reports it as an "Unexpected error" (a crash, not schema issues) and the failure count matches; (2) "Check that packages match versions required by installed Expo SDK" (`expo install --check`) falls back to the bundled native-module list, so the api.expo.dev pins (react, react-native, typescript, jest-expo, …) are not checked and the check passes; (3) the React Native Directory check is off. Every other failure fails. CI does not set it (`release-config-check` asserts that), so CI is the authority. Alternative: an `expo.doctor` config in package.json, which only covers the directory check.
- **2026-09-29 · Deploy workflow: gated on the release check, migrations guarded by `packages/db/migrations`.** `deploy.yml` calls `ci.yml` as a reusable workflow (`workflow_call`) in a `release-check` job, and `deploy` `needs` it, so a red commit never migrates or deploys. Alternative: `workflow_run` on CI success (no duplicate run on develop/master pushes, but it needs `workflow_run.head_branch` for the env mapping and a checkout of `head_sha`); the cost of the chosen way is that a push to develop/master runs the release check twice (CI + Deploy). The migrations step `bunx wrangler d1 migrations apply DB --env <env> --remote` runs before `bun -F @smog/site deploy` and is guarded by `hashFiles('packages/db/migrations/*.sql') != ''`, the spec's location (phase 2 sets `migrations_dir: "../../packages/db/migrations"`). `release-config-check` derives the directory from `wrangler.jsonc` `env.<staging|production>.d1_databases[DB].migrations_dir` (default `packages/db/migrations` while there is no D1) and fails if the guard points elsewhere. The deploy itself is the guarded site script (`CLOUDFLARE_ENV` from the branch, asserted equal to `environment`), never a bare `wrangler deploy`.

- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `sharp` 0.35.5, a devDependency of `@smog/brand` only.** It rasterises the logo artwork into the committed PNG icons (`bun -F @smog/brand generate`); Bun has no image API and the ICO container is written by hand (`src/ico.ts`). Builds never run the generator. Alternatives: `@resvg/resvg-js` (SVG only, no PNG palette/flatten control), or committing hand-exported PNGs (drift nobody notices).
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `radix-ui` 1.6.7 (the unified package) for the web kit's Dialog, Sheet, AlertDialog, Menu, Select, Tabs, Tooltip, Toast, Switch, Checkbox, RadioGroup, ToggleGroup (SegmentedControl), Avatar, Progress and Slot.** These are accessible primitives the platform does not provide (focus trapping, roving focus, typeahead, dismissal layers, portals). One package keeps every primitive on one release. Alternatives: the separate `@radix-ui/react-*` packages (fifteen versions to keep aligned), React Aria (a second a11y model next to the native kit), or native `<dialog>`/`popover` (no menu/select/toast semantics). Toasts are our own on Radix Toast, not sonner (brief).
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `lucide-react` 1.48.0 for web icons** (root catalog: the kit and the site's `/dev/ui`). Tree-shaken per icon, `currentColor`, and `lucide-react-native` shares the icon names for the native kit. Alternative: hand-copied SVGs (drift, no names).
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `class-variance-authority` 0.7.1, `clsx` 2.1.1, `tailwind-merge` 3.7.0 in `@smog/ui-web`** (`lib/variants.ts`, `lib/cn.ts`, as briefed). tailwind-merge is extended from `@smog/styles/tokens` (text sizes, radii, elevations, spacing keys, font weights, named durations), because the theme replaces Tailwind's scales and the default config would treat `text-body` as a colour and drop it next to `text-primary`.
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] Kit tests on `bun test` with `@happy-dom/global-registrator` 20.14.5 and Testing Library (`@testing-library/react` 16.3.3, `dom` 10.4.2, `user-event` 14.6.7).** `packages/ui-web/bunfig.toml` preloads `src/test/setup.ts`, which registers happy-dom before Testing Library loads and runs `cleanup` after each test. Radix Select, Menu, Tooltip and Toast render and open under happy-dom without shims. Alternatives: Vitest + jsdom (a second runner for a pure package).
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `@playwright/test` 1.63.0 and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 for `apps/site` e2e (`bun -F @smog/site test:e2e`).** The browsers are preinstalled (Chromium 141, revision 1194, at `/opt/pw-browsers`); Playwright 1.63 expects a newer revision, so `playwright.config.ts` launches `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` by path when it exists (`PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_PATH` overrides; elsewhere Playwright's own browser is used). `playwright install` is never run here. The suite is not part of `release:check`/CI yet: CI has no browser step. `webServer` runs `bun run dev` (local bindings). Alternative: pin an older Playwright that matches revision 1194 (breaks "newest stable").
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `@gorhom/bottom-sheet` 5.2.14 for the native Sheet (and the Select picker and Menu), with `react-native-gesture-handler` ~2.32.0.** It is JavaScript on Reanimated + Gesture Handler (no native module of its own), supports Reanimated 4 (`overrideReduceMotion` follows the kit's `useReducedMotion`), sizes to its content, and the old app ran 5.2.10 on Reanimated 4 / New Architecture. Gesture Handler is Expo SDK 57's pin (`bundledNativeModules.json`: ~2.32.0; npm's newest is 3.3.0, a deviation from "newest stable" that expo-doctor requires). The kit re-applies `themeVars` at the sheet root, turns off gorhom's grouped accessibility container (`accessible={false}`, otherwise VoiceOver reads the whole sheet as one element) and draws its own decorative handle (gorhom's default handle has an English label). Alternatives: Expo Router `formSheet` presentation (a route, so a Select or Menu inside a screen cannot use it), a `Modal`-based sheet (no drag to dismiss, no content sizing). Not verified on a device or simulator (none here); `expo export` bundles it.
- **2026-09-29 · Native Menu is a kit action sheet, not `@react-native-menu/menu`.** `MenuContent` renders the items in the kit's bottom sheet (`menu` role, 44 pt `menuitem` rows), as `packages/ui-web/API.md` describes ("Native: an action sheet"). `@react-native-menu/menu` 2.0.0 (UIMenu / PopupMenu, used by the old app) was not adopted: it is a native module last published in 2025-09 whose build against RN 0.86 cannot be checked here (no native build or simulator), it cannot be opened programmatically on iOS (no controlled `open`), it takes SF Symbol names instead of the kit's icons, and its trigger must not be a Pressable (the kit's triggers wrap Buttons). Revisit for header overflow menus once a device build is available.
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `lucide-react-native` 1.48.0 (root catalog) with `react-native-svg` 15.15.4, and `expo-haptics` ~57.0.3.** Lucide keeps the web kit's icon set and names. Icons are imported per file (`lucide-react-native/icons/check`): Metro does not tree-shake, and the barrel import made the iOS bundle 6.5 MB instead of 4.4 MB. `react-native-svg` also draws `@smog/brand/svg` (Logo, the EmptyState hands); 15.15.4 is Expo SDK 57's pin (npm has 15.15.5). `expo-haptics` gives the toggle and danger haptics (spec §16). Alternatives: `@expo/vector-icons` (another icon set than web), PNG logos (`@smog/brand` has the SVGs).
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] Native kit tests: Jest + jest-expo with `@testing-library/react-native` 14.0.1 and `test-renderer` 1.2.0 (root catalog), styles compiled by the real Tailwind 3.** RNTL 14 needs `test-renderer`; 1.3.0 depends on `react-reconciler` 0.34, which requires React ^19.3, and React is pinned to 19.2.3, so 1.2.0 (reconciler 0.33, React 19.2) is used. css-interop's own test helper is only used for `registerCSS` and `setupAllComponents`: `jest.setup.ts` runs the kit's sources through Tailwind 3.4.19 + the SMOG preset (postcss 8.5.28) once per file and registers the CSS, so tests assert real styles (`min-h-touch` is `minHeight: 44`) and theme resolution (a dialog title is the dark `foreground` in dark mode). `jest.setup-env.ts` holds the native-module mocks (Reanimated with a switchable `useReducedMotion`, Worklets, Gesture Handler, safe area, haptics, and gorhom's mock extended so `dismiss()` reports `onDismiss`); it is exported as `@smog/ui-native/jest-setup` and the mobile app uses it too. Container roles (`radiogroup`, `tablist`, `menu`) stay non-accessible views so their children remain focusable, and RNTL's role queries only see accessibility elements, so tests find those containers by label or test id. Alternatives: RNTL 13.3 + `react-test-renderer` (deprecated in React 19), or rendering without CSS and asserting props only (no check of the 44 pt sizes or the overlay theme).

- **2026-09-30 · Parallel CI release lanes, one gate per deploy-branch push.** The same `release:check:core`, `release:check:tests` and `release:check:mobile` scripts run sequentially under local `release:check` and in a three-runner CI matrix with `fail-fast: false`. Deploy still waits for the whole reusable workflow. CI's direct push trigger excludes develop/master because Deploy already calls it; pull-request checks remain. This supersedes the duplicate-run tradeoff above. A successful CI run measured ~4m25s tests, ~2m40s mobile validation and ~1 minute other checks; independent runners reduce elapsed time without dropping checks or caching online audit/doctor results. Alternatives: sharing a runner (CPU contention), caching test results across runs (requires a broader cache-input audit). Both mobile exports retain cache clearing and gallery checks. The release-config guard asserts all three lanes exist.

## Architecture

- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/rpc` split from `@smog/api`.** Features need the oRPC base (context, middleware, errors) and `api` composes the features; putting both in one package would create a cycle. `rpc` = base, `api` = composition + client. Alternative: features define their own base (duplicated) or live inside `api` (not modular).
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/local-store` package** for the on-device guest store with web/native adapters (the brief asked for "one shared local-store abstraction"). Features' `./client` hooks use it when signed out.
- **2026-09-29 · Contract-first oRPC** (`@orpc/contract`), so the mobile bundle only ships contracts and schemas, never server code.
- **2026-09-29 · Jobs package owns message types, producers and schedules; handlers are wired in the site Worker.** This avoids a jobs ↔ features cycle.
- **2026-09-29 · No locale prefix in site URLs.** The language comes from a cookie, then `Accept-Language`, then `nl`. Why: old URLs (`/gestures/…`, `/lists/<token>`) and app links stay valid, and gesture content (the sign videos) is language-neutral. Alternative: `/{nl,en,fr}/…` as in the reference branch (better hreflang, but breaks every old link and deep link).
- **2026-09-29 · Gesture URLs use slugs** (`/gestures/<slug>`); old Convex ids resolve through `gesture.legacy_id` (301 on web, transparent lookup in the app).
- **2026-09-29 · Boundaries graph encodes subpaths.** In `@smog/config/boundaries`, a plain entry (`@smog/db`) allows the package and all subpaths, a subpath entry (`@smog/render/contract`) allows the dependency but only that subpath in imports, and `@smog/feature:*` (optionally `/schema`, `/client`) stands for every package in `FEATURE_PACKAGES`. Every package from spec §4.1 is listed now, before it exists, and a workspace missing from the graph is a violation. `scripts/check-boundaries.ts` checks declared `@smog/*` dependencies and every `@smog/*` import in a workspace's source files (forbidden subpaths and undeclared imports). `@smog/config` may be a devDependency of any package (for the tsconfig bases, spec §4.1 leaves it out of `ui-web`/`ui-native`); as a runtime dependency or import it is still checked. Alternative: Turborepo's experimental `boundaries` (tags only, no subpath rules).
- **2026-09-29 · The boundaries check also covers `optionalDependencies` and mock specifiers.** `vi.mock`/`vi.doMock`/`vi.importActual` and `jest.mock`/`jest.requireActual` (and friends) with an `@smog/*` string count as imports, so a test cannot mock a package its workspace may not use. Closes the phase 1 known gap.
- **2026-09-29 · Site Cloudflare env is chosen at build time with `CLOUDFLARE_ENV`, and deploys go through a guard.** With the Cloudflare Vite plugin, `vite build` flattens one wrangler env into `dist/server/wrangler.json` (`targetEnvironment`) and `wrangler deploy` uses that redirected config, so `--env` does not select bindings: a bare `wrangler deploy` ships whatever env `dist/` was last built for. `vite.config.ts` defaults `CLOUDFLARE_ENV` to `dev` (local dev, test builds, `vite preview`). Deploys only run through `bun -F @smog/site deploy` (`vite build && bun scripts/deploy-guard.ts && wrangler deploy --env $CLOUDFLARE_ENV`). The guard (`apps/site/scripts/deploy-guard.ts`, tested) requires `CLOUDFLARE_ENV` to be `staging` or `production` and the build's `targetEnvironment` to match, so a missing env, `dev`, or a stale build for another env fails before wrangler runs, and wrangler itself refuses an `--env` that differs from the build. `deploy:dry` = `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=staging bun run deploy --dry-run`. `apps/site/turbo.json` hashes `CLOUDFLARE_ENV` for `build` (and caches `.wrangler/deploy/**`, the redirect file), so a dev build is never replayed for staging. CI (`deploy.yml`) must call the `deploy` script, never `wrangler deploy` directly. Alternatives: build the top-level config by default (a deploy then ships without bindings), or `.env.<mode>` files (need `.gitignore` exceptions, same effect).
- **2026-09-29 · `parseWorkerVars` takes `object`.** The generated `Env` is an interface without an index signature, so `Record<string, unknown>` rejected `env` from `cloudflare:workers`; zod validates the shape anyway.
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] oRPC 1.15.4** (`@orpc/contract`, `server`, `client`, `openapi`, `zod`, `tanstack-query`, newest stable, in the root catalog). `@smog/rpc` has four subpaths: `.` (Worker: builders, middleware), `./contract` (`baseContract = oc.errors(ERRORS)`, `ERRORS`, `roleSchema`; only `@orpc/contract` + zod, so contracts in the app bundles never pull `@orpc/server`), `./react` and `./testing` (`makeRpcContext`, `makeSession` for feature tests). `roleSchema` is written out (`["user", "admin"]`) because `@smog/db` (`ROLES`) must not reach the app bundles; a type test keeps it equal to `@smog/auth`'s `Role` and the db enum.
- **2026-09-29 · rpc context shape.** `RpcContext.env` is `WorkerEnv` (`workerEnvSchema` / `parseWorkerEnv` in `@smog/config/env/worker`: vars + secrets; outside `dev` it requires `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`, so `requireTurnstile` cannot fail open in staging/production; Cloudflare's always-pass test secret is fine for a staging without a widget) plus the four rate-limit bindings, typed by a structural `RateLimiter` interface (the Workers `RateLimit` type is global only where `wrangler types` ran). The context has no `user`; `requireUser` / `requireAdmin` add it, so `context.user` is non-null exactly where a guard ran. The site builds the context without I/O (`createRpcContext(request, siteEnv(), { waitUntil })`: `ip` from `cf-connecting-ip`, `unknown` locally; `locale` from the `locale` cookie parsed from the request with `cookie-es` 3.1.1, the parser TanStack Start already uses, then Accept-Language) with `session: null`; the `loadSession` handler interceptor reads the session once with `getSession`, after the `RL_API` limit passed, so a rate-limited request costs no D1 read. Both transports get this order from `rpcHandlerOptions` (`@smog/rpc`).
- **2026-09-29 · rpc errors and middleware.** `ERRORS` carries statuses only (no messages; clients show `@smog/i18n` copy for the code): 409 for `CONFLICT`/`INVALID_STATE`/`GESTURE_UNAVAILABLE`/`PAYMENT_MISMATCH`, 410 `TOKEN_EXPIRED`, 400 `TOKEN_INVALID`, 403 `TURNSTILE_FAILED`/`FORBIDDEN`, 401, 404, 429, and `VALIDATION` 422 with `z.flattenError` data. `mapValidationErrors` (a handler `clientInterceptor`) turns oRPC's `BAD_REQUEST` + `ValidationError` into `VALIDATION`, so input errors are typed. Middleware throws `new ORPCError(code, { status })` instead of `errors.X()`, so it works on any contract; oRPC marks it `defined` when the procedure's contract declares the code (every `baseContract` procedure does). `logErrors` sits on `publicProcedure` and on every procedure of `implementRpc(contract)` (the contract-first implementer features use); ORPC errors below 500 pass silently, anything else is logged as `[rpc:<path>]` and replaced by a bare `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`. Turnstile verification reads `x-turnstile-token` and posts `secret`/`response`/`remoteip` to siteverify. A siteverify network error or non-2xx answer counts as an invalid token (`TURNSTILE_FAILED`, logged; the client retries with a fresh token) instead of a 500. The token travels per call through the typed client context `RpcClientContext` (`{ turnstileToken? }`, `@smog/rpc/contract`), which `ApiClient` and the slice clients of `@smog/rpc/react` carry; `createApiClient`'s link maps it to the `x-turnstile-token` header of that call. Better Auth's captcha plugin reads `x-captcha-response` on `/api/auth/*` instead.
- **2026-09-29 · Rate limits: the binding only has 10 s and 60 s periods.** The Workers Rate Limiting `ratelimits` config accepts `period: 10 | 60` only (docs and wrangler's schema), so the old 20/3600 s (sponsor) and 30/900 s (auth) windows cannot be expressed. In staging and production `RL_SPONSOR` and `RL_AUTH` are 5/60 s: the old count spread over one minute rounds to 1 and 2, which would block a normal multi-step flow (send code + verify + a typo; logo upload + checkout), so the floor is 5 per minute. Long-run this is looser (auth 75/15 min instead of 30; sponsor 300/h instead of 20). Better Auth's own limiter is only a per-isolate memory counter outside dev (see Auth), not a real per-IP limit. `RL_API` 300/60 s and `RL_ANALYTICS` 120/60 s are exact. `dev` allows 1000/60 s on all four: miniflare enforces the bindings and every local request shares the `unknown` IP, so the old values would lock out a developer or the e2e suite. The site tests override `RL_AUTH` to 5/60 s (`miniflare.ratelimits` in `vitest.config.ts`) to exercise the 429. Namespace ids are unique per binding and env (dev 1001–1004, staging 2001–2004, production 3001–3004; tests 9003). Where each applies: `RL_API` per IP on every request of both transports (`limitRequests("RL_API", "api")`, a handler interceptor that runs before the session lookup and throws `RATE_LIMITED` with `defined: true`, like the old `/rpc/*` limit); `RL_AUTH` per IP on `POST /api/auth/*` except `/sign-out` and `/get-session`, which only act on the caller's own cookie (429 `{ "code": "RATE_LIMITED" }`, `retry-after: 60`); `rateLimit(binding)` per procedure keyed `<ip>:<path>` for `RL_SPONSOR` (and `RL_ANALYTICS`) in later phases. Alternatives: a KV/D1 counter for the long windows (writes our own limiter, KV is not atomic), or 1–2/60 s (breaks flows). Revisit if abuse shows up.
- **2026-09-29 · Feature hooks get typed utils for their own contract slice.** `RpcProvider` holds the one app client and its `createTanstackQueryUtils` utils; `useRpcClient<{ gestures: GesturesContract }>()` / `useRpcQuery<…>()` cast to the slice's client/utils type. A feature therefore never imports `@smog/api` (boundaries). Safe because the app client has every slice under the same key; `@smog/api` fails to type-check if a feature router is not mounted under its contract key. Pattern in `packages/rpc/README.md`.
- **2026-09-29 · OpenAPI transport with oRPC's `OpenAPIReferencePlugin`.** `/api/openapi/*` is an `OpenAPIHandler` with the plugin (Zod 4 converter from `@orpc/zod/zod4`), which serves the generated document at `/api/openapi/spec.json` and the Scalar reference UI at `/api/openapi` (script from jsdelivr; [Phase 4 fix wave A] now our page, pinned with SRI and its own CSP). It is gated by its own `apiDocsEnabled` (dev and staging, spec §7), not by `devToolsEnabled` (dev only since the Task 2 review, because `/dev/mail` holds sign-in links). `apps/site/src/server/openapi.ts` is loaded with a dynamic `import()` after the gate, so production never evaluates the OpenAPI handler, converter or plugin. Procedures have no `.route()` yet, so paths are the procedure paths (`POST /system/health`).
- **2026-09-29 · Mobile api client takes the app's auth client as a parameter.** `apps/mobile/src/lib/api.ts` exports `createMobileApiClient(authClient: Pick<ExpoAuthClient, "getCookie">)`: `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL`, the `cookie` header from `await authClient.getCookie()` (async in `@better-auth/expo` 1.7.6) and `credentials: "omit"`. Now that `@smog/auth/expo` is client-safe (Task 2 fix, `src/fields.ts`) the type comes from it, but the auth client itself is still created once by the app root with the providers (Task 9): one auth client per app, which both the session provider and the api client use, and the Jest test needs no SecureStore or Better Auth ESM. Jest transforms `.mjs` and `@orpc/*` (oRPC is ESM only).
- **2026-09-29 · One convention for user-scoped query keys, one purge.** `@smog/rpc/react` owns `userScopedKey(key, userId)` (appends `{ user: id | null }` to the oRPC key, so `rpc.key()` still matches), `isOtherUsersKey`, `purgeOtherUsers(queryClient, userId)` and `usePurgeOtherUsers()` / `<PurgeOtherUsers />`, which removes every feature's queries scoped to another user once the session settles. Both apps mount `<PurgeOtherUsers />` inside `AuthStateProvider`, so a sign-out or account switch drops the previous user's lists and favorites even when no list screen is mounted (required before phase 3 task 7 persists the query cache on mobile); the favorites and lists hooks call the hook too, so they are correct on their own. Replaces lists' `forUser` (`{ user }`, never purged) and favorites' inline `{ userId }` plus `isOtherUsers`. `@smog/rpc` gains `@tanstack/react-query` as a peer. Alternative: a purge in `AuthStateProvider` (couples auth to TanStack Query).

## Design system

- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/brand` may depend on `@smog/styles` (boundaries change).** Spec §4.1 lists brand → config only, but the icon generator must take the brand green and white from the tokens (nothing written twice). `styles` is a leaf (→ nothing), so no cycle is possible. `@smog/config/boundaries` now allows `@smog/brand` → `@smog/styles`, `@smog/config`. Alternative: repeat the hex values in brand (two sources of truth).
- **2026-09-29 · [ux] Light `focus-ring` is `#C3780F` instead of `#EE971C` (spec §16 deviation, measured).** A focus indicator is non-text UI and needs 3:1 against what surrounds it (WCAG 1.4.11). The brand orange `#EE971C` measures 2.19:1 on `background` `#F7F9F7`, 2.31:1 on `surface` `#FFFFFF`, 2.06:1 on `surface-sunken` and 2.00:1 on `primary-subtle`. `#C3780F` is the same hue and saturation (HSL lightness lowered until every pair passes): 3.30:1 on `background`, 3.49:1 on `surface`, 3.11:1 on `surface-sunken`, 3.02:1 on `primary-subtle`. `accent` stays `#EE971C`, the dark theme is unchanged (`#F5AB45`, 9.69:1 on `background`), and the light `primary` stays `#00805F`. Alternative: keep the orange ring and add a second, dark ring (two tokens for one job).
- **2026-09-29 · Contrast pairs and status text roles (`CONTRAST_PAIRS`) [ux].** Spec §16 lists roles, not pairs, so the pairs the kits may use are fixed in `src/contrast.ts` and tested in both themes, and a test fails when a role is in neither a pair nor `NON_TEXT_ROLES` (`border`, `border-subtle`: decorative; functional edges use `foreground-muted`). The §16 status hues cannot carry small text in light mode (on `background`: `success` 4.14, `warning` 3.44; `primary` is 4.27 on `primary-subtle`; nothing passes on `accent` in both themes), yet Badge/StatusBadge/Field text needs them. So new roles were added, the §16 roles stay unchanged: `*-subtle` tints (light/dark) `success-subtle` `#E3F3EA`/`#123021`, `warning-subtle` `#FBF0DA`/`#33260B`, `danger-subtle` `#FBE9E9`/`#3A1717`; `*-strong` text shades `primary-strong` `#00694E`/`#2BB38A` (5.81:1 on `primary-subtle`, 5.97 on `surface-sunken`), `success-strong` `#166B3A`/`#4CC27E` (5.71 on its tint, 6.57 on `surface`), `warning-strong` `#7A4D06`/`#E9B24A` (6.42, 7.26), `danger-strong` `#A61F1F`/`#F16B6B` (6.32, 7.40; dark 5.37 on its tint); and `accent-foreground` `#17211A`/`#04140E` (7.15 / 9.71 on `accent`). Naming: `*-foreground` is text on the filled role (like `primary-foreground`), `*-strong` is the hue as text on its `*-subtle` tint and on page backgrounds. Body pairs (4.5:1): `foreground`/`foreground-muted` on page backgrounds, `foreground` on every tint, `primary-foreground` on `primary`/`danger`, `accent-foreground` on `accent`, each `*-strong` on its tint and on page backgrounds, `danger` on page backgrounds and `primary-subtle`. UI pairs (3:1): `primary` on page backgrounds and `primary-subtle`, `success`/`warning` on page backgrounds (icons only), `focus-ring` everywhere. Not allowed: `success`/`warning` as fills under `primary-foreground` (4.38 / 3.64:1). Alternatives: darken the §16 status hues themselves (light `warning` would drop to about `#8A5A12`, no longer orange), or lighten `primary-subtle` to `#EDF7F3` (1.03:1 from the page, no visible tint).
- **2026-09-29 · Generated themes replace Tailwind's default scales.** The web theme resets `--color-*`, `--spacing-*`, `--radius-*`, `--text-*`, `--font-weight-*`, `--shadow-*`, `--ease-*` `--breakpoint-*` and `--container-*` before declaring the tokens, and the NativeWind preset sets `theme.colors/spacing/borderRadius/fontSize/fontWeight/screens` (not `extend`), so `bg-red-500`, `p-7` or `text-sm` do not exist and off-token values cannot creep in. Spacing keys follow Tailwind's `key × 4 = px` convention (`p-4` = 16 on both platforms), plus `touch` (44, the minimum tap target). Web lengths are rem (user font scaling), native px. The `display` size grows to 56/64 from `md` through a media query on its variable (one class, `text-display`). Named durations are `@utility duration-fast|normal|slow` (Tailwind's `duration-*` only takes numbers), and `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` sets the duration variables (and the default transition duration) to 0 ms. Dark elevation 1 is `none` (spec: border + `surface-raised` instead). Outputs are `packages/styles/generated/theme.css` and `tailwind-preset.cjs`, committed, Biome-ignored and drift-tested (`bun -F @smog/styles generate` after editing tokens). `--container-*` is reset too (only `max-w-content` and `max-w-reading` exist); `--tracking-*`, `--leading-*`, `--blur-*` and `--animate-*` deliberately keep Tailwind's defaults (no §16 tokens for them). The native preset also replaces `lineHeight` with the type scale's line heights. `src/tailwind.test.ts` compiles the theme with the real Tailwind v4 compiler and asserts token utilities exist, default ones do not, and `transition-*` reads the default duration that reduced motion zeroes.
- **2026-09-29 · NativeWind colours are CSS variables; one class set on both platforms (replaces the `*-dark` colour keys).** The preset maps each role to `rgb(var(--color-<role>) / <alpha-value>)` (opacity modifiers like `bg-primary/10` keep working), and `@smog/styles/native` exports `themeVars.light` / `themeVars.dark`, NativeWind `vars()` styles whose values are space-separated RGB channels generated from the tokens. The mobile root (`apps/mobile/src/theme-root.tsx`) applies the one for `useColorScheme()` and puts the page background one level in, so kits write `bg-surface text-foreground` exactly as on web, with no `dark:` doubling. Verified: Tailwind 3 compiles `bg-primary` to `rgb(var(--color-primary) / var(--tw-bg-opacity, 1))`, css-interop turns that into a runtime `rgb(var(...))` descriptor, and its `rgb` resolver splits `"0 128 95"` into channels; a Jest test asserts the root applies `themeVars.dark` in dark mode; `expo export` passes. `darkMode: "class"` stays so the app can override the system scheme (`colorScheme.set`). Caveat for the kits: variables reach only descendants, so views that mount outside the root (native modals/portals) re-apply `themeVars` at their own root. `nativewind` moved to the root catalog (mobile + styles, a peer and dev dependency of styles). Alternative: `dark:*-dark` classes (the first cut; every colour class twice, native only).
- **2026-09-29 · Brand outputs.** The generator (ported from the reference branch, `packages/brand/scripts/generate.ts`) writes the site icons and `brand/*.svg` into `apps/site/public` and the store icon, `smog.icon`, Android adaptive foreground/monochrome, splash icon and `logo-{white,green}{,@2x,@3x}.png` into `apps/mobile/assets`, plus `packages/brand/src/svg.gen.ts` (raw `currentColor` SVG strings, the `./svg` export, so kits render the logo inline without a bundler SVG loader). The reference's Expo web `favicon.png` is dropped (the app has no web target). Tests run on `bun test` instead of Vitest (pure package). `app.config.ts` reads the adaptive-icon and splash background from `@smog/styles/tokens` (Expo's config loader resolves the workspace TypeScript under Node 22 and Jest). Drift: `src/compose.ts` holds the output plan (every file and the SVG it is rendered from); the generator writes from it and `src/drift.test.ts` compares every committed vector byte for byte and every PNG and favicon.ico entry as decoded RGBA pixels against a fresh render (per-channel tolerance 2 for cross-platform librsvg anti-aliasing; locally the difference is 0, and a 0.845 → 0.8 mark-width change fails 7 tests).
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/ui-web` conventions (the API `@smog/ui-native` mirrors, `packages/ui-web/API.md`).** Refs are React 19's `ref` prop, not `forwardRef` (deprecated direction in React 19; `ComponentProps<"button">` already carries `ref`). `className` is merged last with `cn()`. Files are kebab-case (`icon-button.tsx`, Ultracite's filename rule). Props are platform-neutral: `onClick` (native maps it to `onPress`), `variant`/`size`/`tone` names from spec §16; `asChild` exists on web only (links styled as a Button/ListItem). Interactive `md`/`lg` are ≥ 44 px; `sm` exists for dense admin UI and keeps a 44 × 44 hit area through a centred `after:` pseudo-element (`hitArea`), so inputs, which cannot have pseudo-elements, have `md`/`lg` only. Alternatives: `forwardRef` wrappers (more code, same behaviour), PascalCase files (lint rule).
- **2026-09-29 · [ux] Hover and press are a `foreground` state layer, not a darker fill.** A `before:` overlay at 8 % (hover) / 12 % (press) opacity darkens in light mode and lightens in dark mode. The obvious `hover:bg-primary-strong` has no effect in dark mode, where every `*-strong` equals its base hue (by design, for text contrast). The wash is at most 12 %, applied over the whole control including its text. Alternative: separate hover tokens per role (twelve new tokens for one effect).
- **2026-09-29 · Kit classes are checked against the theme (`packages/ui-web/src/classes.test.ts`).** The theme deletes Tailwind's default scales, so an off-token class (`h-24`, `max-w-sm`, `min-w-48`) compiles to nothing, silently. The test scans the kit with Tailwind's own scanner (`@tailwindcss/oxide`), compiles the candidates that use themed roots with the real theme and the kit CSS (`@tailwindcss/node`), and fails on any class that produced no rule. During the build `h-24` (twice), `min-w-48` and `max-w-screen-2xl` had slipped in and rendered as nothing; the test now fails on such a class. `packages/ui-web/src/styles.css` registers `./components` and `./lib` as Tailwind sources (the shared fragments in `lib/variants.ts` were missing from the site's CSS until `./lib` was added) and excludes tests.
- **2026-09-29 · Overlays portal into `PortalContainerProvider`'s element** (default `document.body`). The `/dev/ui` dark column is a `.dark` subtree inside a light page; overlays opened there portal inside it and keep its variables (the e2e asserts the dialog is inside the dark column). On native the same idea covers modals that mount outside the theme root (see the NativeWind entry above). Alternative: toggle the page theme to preview overlays (cannot show both at once).
- **2026-09-29 · Overlay motion uses the motion tokens only.** `styles.css` defines `animate-fade-*`, `pop-*` (dialogs, `scale` so it composes with Tailwind's `translate`), `slide-*` (sheets, toasts) from `--duration-*` and `--ease-standard`, and every overlay adds `motion-reduce:animate-none`; the tokens are 0 ms under reduced motion anyway. The indeterminate ProgressBar sweep is `4 × --duration-slow`. Toasts stay 5000 ms (Radix's default; a reading time, not a motion value).
- **2026-09-29 · DataTable sorts in the component; no table library.** Columns declare `sortValue`; sorting uses `Intl.Collator` (numeric) and cycles none → ascending → descending, uncontrolled or controlled (`sort`/`onSortChange`, for URL-synced admin filters). Rows with `onRowClick` take focus and open on Enter/Space. Alternative: `@tanstack/react-table` 9 (headless, but a large API for three admin tables; add it when admin needs filtering/column visibility).
- **2026-09-29 · `/dev/ui` is compiled out of production builds and gated at runtime.** `vite.config.ts` defines `__SMOG_DEV_TOOLS__` as `CLOUDFLARE_ENV !== "production"`; the route lazy-imports the gallery only behind it, so a production build has no gallery or kit chunk (the route component chunk is 0.4 kB and renders nothing), while dev and staging keep the page. `scripts/deploy-guard.ts` (run by every `deploy`) scans `dist/**/*.js` for the gallery marker `data-theme-column` and fails a production build that contains it, or a staging build that lacks it (tested). Alternatives: a TanStack Router ignore pattern per env (the committed `routeTree.gen.ts` would be rewritten by every production build), or a runtime check only (the 640 kB chunk would ship). At runtime, as defence in depth, `getDevToolsEnabled` (`src/server/dev-tools.functions.ts`) returns `devToolsEnabled(ENVIRONMENT)`; `false` throws `notFound()`, which Start answers with HTTP 404 (verified on a `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=production` build under `vite preview`: `/dev/ui` 404, `/api/health` 200). The page language comes from `?lang=nl|en|fr` (default `nl`), because the site has no locale plumbing yet. Section titles are the components' code names (identifiers, not copy); every other string comes from `@smog/i18n`. The e2e saves one element screenshot per theme column at 390 and 1280 px (`e2e/__screenshots__/dev-ui-{390,1280}-{light,dark}.png`, gitignored).
- **2026-09-29 · Native classes are checked against the preset (`packages/ui-native/src/classes.test.ts`).** Tailwind's scanner (`@tailwindcss/oxide` 4.3.3, the one ui-web's check uses) collects the candidates from `packages/ui-native/src` and `apps/mobile/{app,src}`; every theme-root utility must compile with Tailwind 3 and the generated preset (as NativeWind does), because an off-token class (`h-24`, `p-7`) produces no style on native and nothing to inspect. The test also fails on hex colours and arbitrary `-[…]` values in those sources, and proves itself on `h-24`/`p-7`. The helper stays local: ui-web's runs Tailwind 4 under Bun, and the boundaries graph has no shared test-utility package for the two kits. Alternative: review by hand (how it was checked before).
- **2026-09-29 · Toasts draw above native overlays.** A native `Modal` is its own window and the gorhom sheet host renders after the app's tree, so the provider's toasts were hidden under an open Dialog or Sheet. Each open overlay (Dialog/AlertDialog card, Sheet/Select/Menu panel) mounts a `ToastViewport`; the provider keeps one list of toasts, their timers and the iOS announcement, and only the most recently mounted viewport draws, so a toast shows once, on the topmost surface. The Dialog card no longer sets `accessibilityViewIsModal` (the Modal window already keeps focus inside, and the flag hid the toasts next to the card). Alternative: document that toasts wait for the overlay to close.
- **2026-09-29 · The native preset turns off Tailwind 3's opacity variables (`corePlugins`).** With them, `bg-primary` compiles to `--tw-bg-opacity: 1; background-color: rgb(var(--color-primary) / var(--tw-bg-opacity))`, so a class that adds a colour after the first render (a tab that becomes selected: `border-transparent` → `border-primary`) makes the view start *setting* a CSS variable; css-interop then remounts it (its "set a variable during the initial render" upgrade), and under the test renderer the remount loops until the heap runs out. `backgroundOpacity`, `borderOpacity`, `divideOpacity`, `placeholderOpacity`, `ringOpacity` and `textOpacity` are off in `generate-native.ts`, so colours compile to `rgb(var(--color-primary) / 1)`; opacity modifiers (`bg-foreground/40`) still work through `<alpha-value>`. `packages/styles/src/generate.test.ts` checks the setting and `packages/ui-native/src/gate.test.tsx` renders a view that gains a colour class (it hung before the change). This amends the "NativeWind colours are CSS variables" entry, whose compiled form is now `/ 1`. Alternative: keep every state's colour class on the first render (every kit author would have to know the trap).
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/ui-native` conventions (`packages/ui-native/API.md` lists every divergence from the web API).** Web `onClick` is `onPress`; `asChild` triggers become wrappers of one pressable child (`DialogTrigger`, `SheetTrigger`, `MenuTrigger`, AlertDialog `children`). Icons are lucide elements that receive the label's colour and a size (no `currentColor`); props that need a colour value read it from `useColor(role)` (tokens of the active scheme). Sizes in code come from `tokens.spacing` / `tokens.touchTarget`; small targets reach 44 pt through `hitSlop`; overlays (native `Modal` for Dialog/AlertDialog, the gorhom sheet for Sheet/Select/Menu) re-apply `themeVars` at their root and drop animation under `useReducedMotion`. The app root adds `KitProvider` (gesture root + sheet host) inside `ThemeRoot`. `OfflineBanner` requires `online` on native (the app passes NetInfo). Alternatives: `forwardRef`/web prop names on native (`onClick` would fight RN's conventions).
- **2026-09-29 · The mobile component gallery is guarded and compiled out of production bundles.** `app/settings/developer-tools/{index,gallery}.tsx` sit in a minimal `app/settings/_layout.tsx` stack (Task 9 adds the settings screens and the 5-tap unlock) behind `Stack.Protected guard={devToolsAvailable()}`, which is false when `EXPO_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT` is `production`. The route file itself must exist (Expo Router bundles every route), so `gallery.tsx` only `require`s `src/dev/component-gallery.tsx` behind `process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT === "production" ? null : require(...)`: babel-preset-expo inlines the variable and Metro's constant folding (release builds) drops the dead `require` before collecting dependencies. Metro's transform cache does not key on `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` values (a cached dev transform kept the gallery in a production export), so the app's `export` script and the release check's production export run with `--clear`. `scripts/mobile-release-check.ts` asserts the gallery's testID marker (`smog-dev-component-gallery`) is in the dev export and absent from an `EXPO_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT=production` export (`dist/production`), like the site's deploy guard. EAS production builds set the variable in `eas.json`. knip lists `apps/mobile/src/dev/*.tsx` as entries, since it does not follow the conditional `require`. Alternatives: a Metro `blockList` per environment (breaks the routes `_layout` names), or the guard only (the gallery would ship).
- **2026-09-29 · Domain components take structural prop types, not `@smog/gestures/schema`.** The boundaries graph (spec §4.1) gives `ui-web`/`ui-native` no feature dependency, so each kit declares `GestureCardData` (`id`, `slug`, `name`, `playbackId`, `categories: { name, slug }[]`) and `CategoryRef` in `src/domain/types.ts`. `GestureSummary` and `SearchResult` are assignable to it, so screens pass them unchanged. The kits stay feature-agnostic. Alternative: add `@smog/feature:*/schema` to the kits' allowed dependencies. That would couple the UI kits to Zod schemas for five fields.
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `@mux/mux-player-react` 3.13.4 in `@smog/ui-web`, loaded on the client only.** VideoPlayer imports the player with `import.meta.env.SSR ? null : lazy(() => import("@mux/mux-player-react"))`. Vite replaces `import.meta.env.SSR` per environment, so the Worker build drops the import and emits no player chunk. The first version used the package's `/lazy` entry, which still put a 1.95 MB chunk in `dist/server`. The server and the first client render (`useSyncExternalStore`: false on the server and during hydration) show the Mux poster inside the aspect-locked frame, so there is no layout shift. `apps/site/scripts/deploy-guard.ts` fails a build whose `dist/server` contains Mux Player (`MUX_PLAYER_MARKERS`). It refused the old build and passes the new one. Mux Data is off (`disableTracking`, `disableCookies`) until analytics consent covers it (phase 4). `autoPlay` is muted on both platforms. Web `onEnded` never fires while `loop` is on, because a looping media element does not end; `onNearEnd` does not depend on it. Native uses `expo-video` (~57.0.5, the SDK 57 version the app already has, now a peer dependency of the kit) on `https://stream.mux.com/{id}.m3u8`, with native controls. The `onNearEnd` rule is `createNearEndTracker` in `@smog/utils` (`media.ts`, with `muxStreamUrl` and `muxThumbnailUrl`), so both kits share it: it fires once per playthrough at ≤ 5 s left, and at the first update for a video of 5 s or less. It re-arms when time jumps back (the next loop or a seek back) or on `ended`. Native adds `isFocused`: the video pauses on blur and an autoplaying one resumes on focus.
- **2026-09-29 · [ux] CourseBanner marks the linked phrase in the copy (`<course>…</course>`) instead of matching phrases.** The old banner searched each message for "Klik hier", "klik dan hier", "Click here", "Cliquez ici" and so on. Now `gesture.videoComplete.1..7` wrap that phrase in a `<course>` tag in every locale, and the kits render it with react-i18next's `Trans` (web: an `<a>` in a new tab; native: a nested `Text` with the `link` role that calls `Linking.openURL`). Each language links its own words, and a translation cannot silently lose the link: a kit test checks that messages 1, 2, 3 and 6 link in nl, en and fr and that 4, 5 and 7 do not. The tag is not `<link>`: react-i18next's HTML parser treats `link` as a void element and drops the phrase out of it. Copy edits while porting: nl 6 and 7 got a question mark ("… het ontstaan? Klik hier.", "… over SMOG? Stuur een mail …"). The banner is a polite live region (web `role="status"`). The caller chooses `messageIndex` (`VIDEO_COMPLETE_COUNT` is in `@smog/config`, which the kits cannot import; `COURSE_MESSAGE_COUNT` = 7 in the kits and a test pin it).
- **2026-09-29 · Web gesture cards and rows use a stretched link and take the router's link as `linkComponent`.** The name is the only link. Its `after:` pseudo-element covers the card or row and carries the focus ring, so there is one tab stop and a full-size target. The heart and the row slots (drag handle, trailing menu) are siblings of the link, above it, never nested inside it. A plain `<a href>` would reload the page under TanStack Router, so the site passes a component that renders its `Link` from `href`. `linkComponent` is web only. Native uses `onPress` on a `Pressable` (the brief's `href` / `onPress` split), and the heart is also a sibling there (a nested pressable would be hidden from VoiceOver inside an accessible button). Thumbnails are `image.mux.com` stills with `alt=""` (the name is the text alternative), lazy-loaded with width and height set.
- **2026-09-29 · [ux] FavoriteButton is a toggle with a constant name.** Its name is always `a11y.favorite`, and the state is `aria-pressed` (web) or `togglebutton` + `checked` (native), so screen readers say "Favorite, toggle, on/off" instead of switching labels. The heart is filled `primary` when on (not red, because `danger` means destructive in the kit). Switching it on pops the icon once to `tokens.motion.popScale` (1.25, `--motion-pop-scale` on web): web uses the `animate-favorite-pop` keyframes, native uses Reanimated `withSequence`. There is no pop when switching off or under reduced motion. Native plays the selection haptic. The component is controlled, and the caller updates optimistically.
- **2026-09-29 · `ShareLink` takes `access` (`view` | `edit`), not `role`.** The brief called it `role`, but on a component that name reads as an ARIA role and trips Biome's `useValidAriaRole`. The values are `@smog/lists` `SHARE_ROLES`. ShareLink is presentational: `onCopy` is the caller's (clipboard and toast), and the caller must surface a failed copy itself. The button confirms with `kit.copied` for 2 s. Web announces it through an sr-only `role="status"` sibling (a live region on the button is not read reliably); native calls `AccessibilityInfo.announceForAccessibility`. Revoke asks through the kit's AlertDialog first.
- **2026-09-29 · Native GestureGrid is a FlatList; SearchResults is full-bleed on native.** The grid is virtualised (`numColumns`, 2 by default) and pads the last row with empty cells, so an odd card keeps the column width. Other FlatList props pass through (`onEndReached` for load-more, `scrollEnabled={false}` inside a ScrollView, which also silences the nested-list warning). Native `SearchResults` pads its count, grid and skeletons with the screen gutter (`px-4`), so a screen renders it edge to edge. The count is a polite live region and is announced on iOS. Web `GestureGrid` is a CSS grid `ul`: 2 columns on phones, then 3 / 4 / 5 at `sm` / `lg` / `xl`.
- **2026-09-29 · `/dev/ui` e2e stubs Mux thumbnails and waits for hydration.** The domain sections load `image.mux.com` stills, which the sandbox (and CI) cannot reach, and a failed image load is a console error. `e2e/helpers.ts` answers them with a local SVG still, so screenshots are stable offline. It also waits until React has hydrated the dark column, because a screenshot taken during a late hydration changed the DOM (Playwright's caret hiding) and caused a hydration-mismatch error. `e2e/dev-ui-domain.spec.ts` saves one screenshot per domain section (`dev-ui-domain-{width}-{theme}-{n}.png`). The gallery's VideoPlayer mounts only when you press "Play video", so the page makes no stream requests. Native `renderKit` now passes its providers as the `wrapper`, so `rerender` keeps them.
- **2026-09-29 · `/dev/ui` and `/dev/mail` have separate runtime gates.** `devUiEnabled(env) = env !== "production"` gates `/dev/ui` (staging keeps the gallery, as the build flag and the deploy guard expect); `devToolsEnabled(env) = env === "dev"` gates only `/dev/mail` and `/dev/mail.json` (the mailbox holds sign-in links). The phase 2 task 2 tightening had applied the mail gate to both, so staging shipped the gallery chunk and answered 404.

## Data model

- **2026-09-29 · Favorites are one `favorite` table.** The old "default favorites list" and legacy `user_favorites` are merged into it by the migration. Lists are only user collections. Alternative: favorites as a special list (the old approach, which caused the duplication).
- **2026-09-29 · Sponsored video is not written into `gesture.playback_id`.** Public queries join the live sponsorship's video, so expiry no longer has to "restore" anything.
- **2026-09-29 · Sponsorship statuses redesigned [rule change: names/states].**
  - New statuses: `awaiting_payment → rendering → in_review → live → expiring → expired`, plus `render_failed`, `changes_requested`, `rejected`, `cancelled`. `expiring` is set when the renewal reminder goes out.
  - Business rules kept: payment is required first, every sponsorship needs admin approval before going live, it lasts 1 year from approval, there is one reminder 30 days before the end, and unpaid checkouts are cancelled after 24 h.
- **2026-09-29 · Consent log stores no IP address or user agent.** The privacy policy does not require them (data minimisation). The old schema had optional fields for them.
- **2026-09-29 · Tokens stored hashed** (sponsorship re-edit/renewal). List share tokens are stored in plain text because the owner must be able to copy the link again. They are 256-bit random capabilities and can be revoked.
- **2026-09-29 · Drizzle ORM 0.45.3 + drizzle-kit 0.31.11** (the `latest` dist-tags). 1.0 is still `rc`/`beta`. `drizzle-orm` is in the root catalog (features will use it); `drizzle-kit` is a devDependency of `@smog/db` only. drizzle-kit only generates (`db:generate`); wrangler applies (`wrangler d1 migrations apply DB`, locally and in `deploy.yml`), so `drizzle.config.ts` has no driver or credentials. Revisit at 1.0 stable.
- **2026-09-29 · Timestamps are `Date` in TS, integer ms in SQL** (`integer({ mode: "timestamp_ms" })`). Better Auth's Drizzle adapter reads and writes `Date` for its date fields, and its CLI generates exactly this mode for SQLite, so one convention covers auth and app tables. `created_at`/`updated_at` get TS-side defaults (`$defaultFn(() => new Date())`, `$onUpdate` for `updated_at`) and no SQL defaults; the seed and the migration script write explicit ms values. Pure helpers (`addDays`, `DAY_MS`) work on ms numbers; services convert with `getTime()` / `new Date(ms)`. Alternative: `number` columns (`integer()` without mode): no conversions, but Better Auth would need custom field mapping.
- **2026-09-29 · FTS5 `gesture_fts` details.** `tokenize = 'unicode61 remove_diacritics 2'` works in the local workerd D1 (schema test (g): `cafe` and `CAFÉ` match `Café`), so no fallback to `1` was needed; D1 in production runs a newer SQLite than 3.27, where `2` was added. `prefix = '2 3'` adds prefix indexes so `hond*` queries stay cheap. It is a standalone FTS table (not `content=` external content) with no triggers: `reindexGesture` in the gestures service rewrites the row in the same D1 batch as each write (spec §5.2), because the indexed `keywords`/`categories` columns aggregate other tables. The migration is hand-written but created with `drizzle-kit generate --custom`, so it is in drizzle's journal and the next generated migration gets number 0002.
- **2026-09-29 · CHECK constraints and partial-index predicates use bare column names.** Drizzle renders `${table.column}` as `"table"."column"`; in SQLite, drizzle-kit changes a CHECK by rebuilding the table as `__new_<table>`, where a qualified name no longer resolves. `packages/db/src/schema/columns.ts` renders `sql.identifier(column.name)` instead (`col()`, `inValues()`, `lengthBetween()`).
- **2026-09-29 · Extra database constraints beyond spec §5's list.** The spec's length limits are also `CHECK (length(…) BETWEEN …)` (display name ≤ `DISPLAY_NAME_MAX`, list name 1..80, …), amounts are `≥ 0`, `payment.currency = 'EUR'`, and every FK column has an index (`list_item.gesture_id`, `list_share.list_id`, `audit_log.actor_id`, `payment_item.sponsorship_id`, …) so cascades and RESTRICT checks do not scan. `sponsorship` also has a plain `(gesture_id, status)` index for the public "live sponsorship of this gesture" join, since SQLite only uses the partial unique index when the query repeats its `IN (…)` predicate. Alternative: Zod only (a bad write from a script or the migration would get in).
- **2026-09-29 · Enum values not named by the spec.** `SPONSORSHIP_EVENT_TYPES` has one type per state-machine event plus `reminder_sent`, `token_issued`, `refund_needed` and `legacy` (migrated rows). `AUDIT_ACTIONS` are dotted `<target>.<verb>` names covering the old `adminLogs` actions (create/update/delete/toggle gestures and categories, approve/reject/force-expire, mark paid, re-edit link, cancel pending payment) plus user management (role, ban, unban, delete, impersonate), `category.reorder`, the maintenance toggle (`maintenance.enable`/`maintenance.disable`, target type `setting`, target id = the KV key) and the sponsorship CSV export (`export.sponsorships_csv`, target type `system`, `target_id` NULL, which is why `audit_log.target_id` is nullable), and `legacy` for unknown migrated shapes (spec §15). The old `restore_original_video` action has no successor (the sponsored video is joined, not copied). Adding a value later is a table rebuild migration.
- **2026-09-29 · Better Auth tables hand-written in phase 2 Task 1.** They follow the CLI's SQLite output (snake_case columns, `timestamp_ms`, `session_user_id_idx`-style indexes) with the admin plugin's `role`/`banned`/`ban_reason`/`ban_expires`/`impersonated_by` and the passkey table. Deviations: `role` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'user'` with a CHECK (`user|admin`, spec §5.1; the plugin stores roles as free text and sets `defaultRole` on sign-up), `locale` and `legacy_id` are ours, and `account` has an extra `(provider_id, account_id)` index for Better Auth's account lookup. Task 2 compares them with the CLI output and adds a migration if they differ.
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/db` test setup.** Vitest runs in `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` with a test-only `packages/db/test/wrangler.jsonc` (just the `DB` D1 binding). `vitest.config.ts` passes `readD1Migrations("migrations")` as the `TEST_MIGRATIONS` binding and the seed SQL as `SEED_SQL`; `@smog/db/testing/apply-migrations` (a `setupFiles` entry other packages can reuse) calls `applyD1Migrations(env.DB, env.TEST_MIGRATIONS)`. `check-types` runs `wrangler types --config test/wrangler.jsonc` first (gitignored output, like the site) so `D1Database` and `Cloudflare.Env` come from wrangler, not `@cloudflare/workers-types`.
- **2026-09-29 · The dev seed is generated and committed.** `packages/db/scripts/seed.ts` builds `seed/dev.sql` (fixed UUID-shaped ids `5eed000<kind>-…`, fixed 2026-01-01 timestamps, `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE`/`DO NOTHING`, and delete-then-insert for the FTS rows; not `INSERT OR REPLACE`, which deletes the old row first and so can run into the `ON DELETE` rules of its children). A Bun test fails if the committed file differs from the generator, and a Vitest test applies it twice to D1. The admin user `admin@smog.test` has no password; it signs in with an email code or magic link (dev email sender). Alternative: generate the file only at `seed:dev` time (nothing to review, no test of the SQL itself).
- **2026-09-29 · Schema columns keep their definition order.** drizzle-kit emits columns in object-key order, and Biome's `useSortedKeys` (Ultracite) would sort them alphabetically (so `id` would not come first, and the Better Auth tables would stop matching the CLI's output line by line). `biome.json` turns `useSortedKeys` off for `packages/db/src/schema/**` only. Alternative: accept alphabetical SQL columns.
- **2026-09-29 · `render_job.workflow_instance_id` is unique and equals the job id.** Workflow instance ids cannot be reused, so a render retry inserts a new `render_job` row (`attempt` + 1) instead of restarting the old one. Knowing the id at insert time is what makes the NOT NULL column possible.
- **2026-09-29 · Drizzle `relations()` for every foreign key** (`src/schema/relations.ts`), for the relational query API and Better Auth's adapter joins. Alternative: plain joins only (every feature would repeat them).

## Sponsorship flow

- **2026-09-29 · [improvement][ux] Live Remotion Player preview in the wizard; the final render happens once, after payment.** Before, a full server render ran per gesture before payment: it took up to 2 min, created orphan Mux assets for abandoned checkouts, and could 400 on names over 35 chars. The admin still reviews the real rendered video before approval.
- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] One display-name field (≤ 35 chars) shown in the video.** The old UI had the sponsor name (≤ 40, UI said 40, enforced 35) and a separate overlay text (≤ 100 on re-edit), but the renderer only accepted 35. The contact name is a separate field.
- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] Max 10 gestures per checkout** (`MAX_GESTURES_PER_SPONSORSHIP = 10` from the old config; the old API allowed 20, the UI enforced none).
- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] Admin "new sponsorship" email is sent after payment**, not at creation. Unpaid attempts no longer notify admins.
- **2026-09-29 · [improvement][rule change] Renewal flow.** The reminder email links to `/sponsor/renew?token=…`. Paying renews for another year at the same price (€50 per gesture, +€10 if the sponsorship has a logo); `ends_at += 365 days`, with no re-render and no re-approval. The old system had no renewal (the link went to the homepage).
- **2026-09-29 · Late paid webhook for a cancelled sponsorship:** revive it if the gesture is still free, otherwise mark the payment `refund_needed` and email admins; always return 200. The old system returned 500 forever.
- **2026-09-29 · Stale-payment cancellation measures from payment creation** (the old code used `updatedAt`, which reset the clock) and also cancels the Mollie payment when it can.
- **2026-09-29 · Overlay layout stays fixed** (the brand-approved layout: logo 22 % box at y 76 %, green text at y 87 %, "Met de warme steun van:"). "Overlay configuration" in the wizard means the display name and optional logo with a live preview. Why: sponsors should not be able to break the brand layout.

## Auth

- **2026-09-29 · Better Auth plugins:** emailOTP, magicLink, passkey, admin (roles/bans/user list), expo, captcha (Turnstile). The user management UI uses the admin plugin's server API through oRPC.
- **2026-09-29 · Passkeys on mobile: web first.** WebAuthn is not available in React Native; a native passkey module is evaluated in phase 4 and recorded here.
- **2026-09-29 · Better Auth 1.7.6 storage: D1 only, no secondary storage, no cookie cache** (revised in the Task 2 review). Every session read (`getSession`, `requireUser`, `requireAdminUser`) queries D1, so a ban, a revoked session, a role change or a deleted user takes effect on the next request (tests in `packages/auth/test/revocation.test.ts`). Verification values are in D1 too (single-use, consumed there). The first version cached sessions in KV (`secondaryStorage` + `storeSessionInDatabase`); a KV hit was trusted without D1, and revocation deleted KV tokens through a read-modify-write `active-sessions-<userId>` list, which KV's eventual consistency and one-write-per-second-per-key limit can lose, so a revoked or banned session could survive until it expired. **Deviation from spec §6** ("secondary storage in KV for rate-limit counters and verification caches") and from the brief's `createAuth({ kv })`: nothing in auth needs KV now, so the option is gone. Alternatives: a KV adapter that ignores session keys (still no atomic operations for rate limits), or `session.cookieCache` (≤ 60 s, so bans would take up to a minute; not needed, a D1 read by primary key is cheap). Revisit a cookie cache only if session reads show up in latency metrics.
- **2026-09-29 · Password hashing: Better Auth's default scrypt, measured on workerd.** `@better-auth/utils/password` resolves the `workerd` export condition to `node:crypto` `scrypt` (native in workerd with `nodejs_compat`), not the pure-JS `@noble/hashes` fallback. Measured in `packages/auth/test/auth.test.ts` (h): one hash ≈ 86 ms, a whole sign-up request ≈ 100 ms in local workerd. That fits Workers Paid (30 s CPU default; the Email Service needs Paid anyway) but not the Free plan's 10 ms. Alternative: PBKDF2 through Web Crypto via `emailAndPassword.password.hash/verify` (a custom hash format for every future credential). Revisit only if production CPU metrics say so.
- **2026-09-29 · Better Auth schema reconciled with its CLI; no migration.** `@better-auth/cli` is deprecated; the CLI is now the `auth` package (`bunx auth@1.7.6 generate --config <file>`). Its Drizzle SQLite output for our config (admin, passkey, emailOTP, magicLink, expo, captcha, `locale`/`legacyId` fields) has exactly our columns, types and FKs. The only differences are the deliberate ones: `role NOT NULL DEFAULT 'user'` + CHECK (the CLI makes it nullable; `admin({ defaultRole: "user" })` always writes it, asserted by a sign-up test), `legacy_id` unique, TS-side instead of SQL `created_at`/`updated_at` defaults, snake_case index names (`session_user_id_idx` vs the CLI's `session_userId_idx`) and our extra indexes.
- **2026-09-29 · Auth config details.** Cookie prefix `smog` (`smog.session_token`, `__Secure-smog.session_token` outside dev, where `useSecureCookies` is on); the Expo client uses the same prefix. Email codes and magic-link tokens are stored hashed (`storeOTP` / `storeToken: "hashed"`). `revokeSessionsOnPasswordReset: true`. `locale` is a client-settable additional field validated as `nl|en|fr`; `legacyId` (and the admin plugin's `role`) are `input: false`, so a sign-up that sends them gets a 400. Apple adds `https://appleid.apple.com` to `trustedOrigins` when configured (its form_post callback). The Expo client omits the passkey plugin (web first, see above). Auth emails are rendered in the user's `locale`, else the request's `Accept-Language`, else `nl`. `appName` and the passkey `rpName` are `APP_NAME` (`common.appName` of the default locale, exported by `@smog/email`, since `@smog/auth` may not import `@smog/i18n`). The site builds one Better Auth instance per isolate (`getAuth()`; it holds no per-request state) and hands it `waitUntil` from `cloudflare:workers`, so emails go out after the response (no timing signal) until the email queue lands in phase 6. `parseAuthEnv` refuses the public dev `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` from `.dev.vars.example` outside `dev` (a site test keeps the two equal).
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/auth` client entries are free of server types.** `./web`, `./expo` and `./react` import only `src/fields.ts` (the `Role` type and `USER_ADDITIONAL_FIELDS`, the one definition of our `user` columns, used by the server's `user.additionalFields` and by the clients' `inferAdditionalFields({ user: … })`) and `src/cookie.ts`. They never import `server.ts`, `env.ts` or `session.ts`, so the apps can typecheck and bundle them without Workers types. `inferAdditionalFields<Auth>()` pulled `D1Database`/`KVNamespace`/`SendEmail` into the mobile program. Two guards: `tsconfig.client.json` (in `check-types`) compiles the entries with the DOM lib and no Workers types, plus `guard/client-types.ts`, which asserts the clients still infer `locale`/`legacyId`; and `guard/client-entries.test.ts` (Bun) walks the entries' import graph and fails on a server file, `@smog/db`, `@smog/email`, `drizzle-orm`, Better Auth server plugins or `cloudflare:*`. Alternative: a `bun build --target=browser` of the entries (it would bundle react-native for `./expo`).
- **2026-09-29 · Captcha endpoints: sign-up/sign-in and every unauthenticated email sender.** With `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` set, Turnstile guards `/sign-up/email`, `/sign-in/email`, `/request-password-reset`, `/send-verification-email`, `/email-otp/send-verification-otp`, `/email-otp/request-password-reset`, `/forget-password/email-otp` and `/sign-in/magic-link` (`CAPTCHA_ENDPOINTS`), with `allowedHostnames` = the `SITE_URL` host. `packages/auth/test/captcha.test.ts` calls every POST endpoint of the configured instance and fails if one that sends an email is missing from the list, so a Better Auth upgrade that adds a sender is caught. Endpoints that need a session (change email, delete user) are not included; they are off in our config. Known gap: once the key is set, the Expo app needs a Turnstile token (a WebView widget) for these calls; phase 4 plans that.
- **2026-09-29 · Better Auth's own rate limiter: memory storage, never on `/get-session`.** It is a coarse per-isolate backstop (`rateLimit: { enabled: ENVIRONMENT !== "dev", storage: "memory", customRules: { "/get-session": false } }`). Memory storage never extends a window on a blocked request, so a school behind one NAT cannot be locked out, and session reads (which `useSession` repeats on focus) are never counted. The real per-IP limit for `/api/auth/*` is the `RL_AUTH` Workers Rate Limiting binding around the route, owned by phase 2 Task 3. Alternatives: KV counters (not atomic, and re-putting the TTL made the window slide forever), D1 (a write per auth request).
- **2026-09-29 · Account linking keeps the spec's trusted providers.** `trustedProviders: ["google", "apple", "email-password"]` (spec §6) skips the provider's own `email_verified` check for Google and Apple; both report verified emails for their accounts, so the practical risk is low, and linking still needs a verified *local* email (`requireLocalEmailVerified: true`, pinned explicitly although it is Better Auth's default). `"email-password"` has no effect in 1.7.6 (the credential provider id is `credential`); it stays because the spec lists it. Alternative: drop Google/Apple from the list so their `email_verified` claim is checked too (stricter, no cost to real users; a reasonable later change).
- **2026-09-29 · The verify email never shows the sign-up name.** It goes to an address nobody has proven to own, and the name is attacker-chosen ("Hello Visit evil.example …"), so it greets with "Hello,". The reset email, sent only to the account's owner, keeps the name.
- **2026-09-29 · `AuthState` keeps the last known state on a fetch error.** `useAuthState()` returns `{ status, user?, error?: true, refetch }`: a failed session fetch (offline) keeps the previous `signedIn`/`signedOut` and sets `error`, so a signed-in user is not treated as a guest; with nothing known it is `signedOut` + `error`.
- **2026-09-29 · The dev seed gives `admin@smog.test` a dev-only password (`smog-dev-admin`).** `scripts/seed.ts` hashes it with Better Auth's scrypt format (`<hex salt>:<hex key>`, N=16384, r=16, p=1, 64-byte key, NFKC) and a fixed salt, so `seed/dev.sql` stays deterministic.
