- **2026-09-29 · Not in this task.** The CourseBanner on the web detail, the sponsor call-to-action (phase 5 has no `/sponsor` yet) and the app banner on home.
  - [fix wave, superseded] The counter did not go into local-store preferences. The web detail now has the CourseBanner, from the one rule in `@smog/gestures/client` (see "One course banner rule" under the phase 3 fix wave). The sponsor call-to-action (inventory L-17) goes into the phase 6 plan, since it needs `sponsorships.availability`; the app banner (L-16) and "open in app" (L-15) are phase 4 task 4.

## Mobile learning screens (phase 3 task 7)

- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `react-native-reorderable-list` 0.18.1 for list drag reorder.** It is maintained (0.18.1, July 2026), is built on Reanimated shared values and Gesture Handler, and its peers (`react-native-reanimated >=3.12`, `react-native-gesture-handler >=2.12`, any React Native) cover the app's Reanimated 4.5 / RN 0.86. The alternative, `react-native-draggable-flatlist` 4.0.3 (the old app's), has had no release since May 2025 and predates Reanimated 4. The handle starts the drag on press-in (`useReorderableDrag`); the same `onReorder` path serves the row menu's Move up / Move down and the handle's screen-reader actions (`moveUp` / `moveDown`), so reordering never needs a drag. Jest has no UI thread, so `jest.setup.ts` swaps the list for a plain FlatList (the library's own `reorderItems` stays real); drags are checked on a device.
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] Offline cache and device modules in the app.** `@tanstack/react-query-persist-client` and `@tanstack/query-async-storage-persister` 5.104.0 (the same release as `@tanstack/react-query`), `@react-native-community/netinfo` 12.0.1, `expo-screen-capture` ~57.0.3 and `expo-clipboard` ~57.0.2 (the Expo SDK 57 pins from `bundledNativeModules.json`). They are only in `apps/mobile`, so they are pinned there, not in the catalog. The app also depends on `@smog/gestures`, `@smog/favorites` (client hooks) and `@orpc/client` (`isDefinedError` for NOT_FOUND screens).
- **2026-09-29 · What the offline cache keeps (spec §10).** `PersistQueryClientProvider` writes the query cache to AsyncStorage (`smog:query-cache:v1`, throttled to 1 s) and restores it for 24 h; the buster is the app version (`expo-constants`), so another build never restores it. It keeps only successful public catalogue reads (`gestures.list`, `categories`, `bySlug`, `byIds`, `related`; `byIds`/`related` make favorites, lists and the gesture screen work offline from the same public data) and the signed-in user's `favorites.ids`, and only while that key is scoped to a user (`userScopedKey`). Searches (one entry per query), lists, share links and favorite pages are never written. Another user's data is never restored: after the restore `PurgeRestoredQueries` runs `purgeOtherUsers` for the current session (next to `<PurgeOtherUsers />`, which reacts to auth changes that may settle first), and every later write drops what the purge removed. Persisted procedures get `gcTime` = 24 h (`setQueryDefaults`), or a restored entry nobody is showing would be collected after 5 minutes. JSON turns the first page's `undefined` cursor into `null`, which the contract rejects on refetch, so the deserializer puts `undefined` back. A full storage drops the oldest query and retries (`removeOldestQuery`). TanStack's `onlineManager` follows NetInfo (unknown counts as online) and `focusManager` follows AppState; the kit's OfflineBanner shows on every learning screen while NetInfo reports no connection.
- **2026-09-29 · The course banner counts on the device, outside `@smog/local-store`.** [fix wave: the rule is now shared and counts once per visit; the storage choice below stands, see "One course banner rule" under the phase 3 fix wave.] The brief put the counter in the local store's preferences; it lives in its own AsyncStorage key (`smog:course-progress:v1`) instead. It is device UI state rather than guest data (nothing to import, clear or export), and a preferences field would mean a local-store version 3 migration in a package this task does not own. Every `VIDEO_COMPLETE_COUNT` (7) completed playthroughs (the player's `onNearEnd`, ≤ 5 s left) the banner shows with a random one of the 7 course messages, until dismissed, and the count starts again. The old app showed it on every playthrough; every seventh keeps the notice without nagging.
- **2026-09-29 · Search, Favorites and Lists are stacks inside their tabs.** A native header is what gives large titles, the iOS header search bar (`headerSearchBarOptions`; Android gets the kit SearchField in the screen) and header buttons (filter, new list, share, menu), so those tabs are folders (`(tabs)/search/_layout.tsx` + `index.tsx`, same for favorites and lists; the brief named `search.tsx` / `favorites.tsx`). Home stays headerless: logo, tagline, search field (submit opens the search tab with `?q=`), category chips (open it with `?category=`) and the first 8 gestures, as the site's home. The gesture screen and the shared list are cards on the root stack over the tabs; `unstable_settings.initialRouteName` is `(tabs)` at the root and `index` in the lists stack, so back from a deep-linked screen lands on the tabs or the overview.
- **2026-09-29 · Deep links (`app/+native-intent.tsx`).** It accepts `https://<host>/…`, `smog://…` (the host is the first segment), `exp://…/--/…` and bare paths; `/gestures/<slug|legacyId>` opens the gesture (the screen resolves legacy ids with `bySlug`), `/lists/<token>` the shared list, `/gestures` and `/search` the search tab with `q` (≤ 100 characters) and `category` carried over, the app's own top-level routes open as themselves, and everything else (deeper paths, dot segments, malformed escapes, other schemes, non-strings) goes to `/`. The whole function is in a `try`, so it cannot throw at launch. An app list is never opened from a link: `/lists/<x>` is a share token by spec. Better Auth's Expo callback (`smog:///`) maps to `/`.
- **2026-09-29 · [superseded 2026-09-30 by "`lists.containing` and one `useListMembership`" below] "Save to list" on the gesture screen reads each list through `useList`.** There is no procedure for "the lists that contain this gesture", so while the ListPicker is open the screen mounts one `useList(id)` per list (the device's for guests, `lists.get` signed in) to fill `contains` and to add or remove. That is up to `LISTS_MAX` cached reads, only while the sheet is open. A new list gets the gesture as soon as its read settles. Alternative, for later: a `lists.containing({ gestureId })` procedure in `@smog/lists`.
- **2026-09-29 · Error states retry by oRPC key.** The feature hooks do not expose `refetch`, so a screen's ErrorState calls `useRetry((rpc) => [rpc.lists.key(), …])` (`src/lib/retry.ts`), which refetches those procedures through the app contract's query utils.
- **2026-09-29 · Mobile screen tests run the real routes over a fake API.** `src/test/harness.tsx` renders the app's route files with `renderRouter`, `AppProviders` with an oRPC client whose `fetch` answers from fixtures per procedure path (errors as the RPC handler sends them), a memory local store, and a guest or signed-in session. `expo-router/testing-library` mocks Reanimated again on import without the kit's `useReducedMotion`; `jest.setup-after-env.ts` loads it once and puts the hook back on the mock.

## Analytics (`@smog/analytics`)

- **2026-09-29 · Package shape.** Subpaths: `.` (the consent gate `createAnalytics`, `NOOP_ANALYTICS`), `./schema` (the taxonomy), `./web` (relay transport, `createWebAnalytics`), `./native` (OpenPanel transport, `createNativeAnalytics`), `./server` (`handleAnalyticsRelay`), `./react` (`AnalyticsProvider`, `useAnalytics`, `useScreenTracking`, `useAnalyticsIdentity`, `useSignInCompleted`, `useMarkSignInStarted`, `createConsentSource`, `useSyncConsent`) and `./testing` (`createRecordingAnalytics`). It depends on `@smog/config` only.
  - *Alternatives:* one entry point; the apps building the gate themselves.
  - *Why:* each runtime loads only its transport, and the apps stay thin (`createWebAnalytics` / `createNativeAnalytics` are one call).
- **2026-09-29 · Boundaries: features and mobile may import `@smog/analytics/react` and `./schema`** (features also `./testing`). Spec §4.1 listed only `./server` for features and `./native` for mobile, but the tracking points are client hooks (`useGestureSearch`, `useFavorites`, `useList`, `useSharedList`, `useGuestImport`), so they call `useAnalytics()`, which is a no-op without a provider. Spec §4.1 is updated.
  - *Alternatives:* an injected callback per hook; a feature-local context.
  - *Why:* one provider serves every hook, and nothing is written twice.
- **2026-09-29 · Taxonomy is strict.** Every properties object is a `z.strictObject`; ids match `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`; counts are non-negative integers; `source`, `action`, `collection` and `method` are enums from the old taxonomy. `sign_in_completed.method` uses `@smog/auth`'s `AuthMethod` values. The relay accepts only `track` (the taxonomy, plus `platform` and an optional `profileId`) and `identify` (`profileId` plus `auth_mode: "authenticated"` and `platform`); `increment`, `alias`, `group` and `replay` get 400. A body over 4 KB gets 413 and is never buffered: a `content-length` over the cap is refused before reading (a non-numeric one gets 400), and a chunked body is read with a running byte count and cancelled past 4096 bytes. Without `cf-connecting-ip` (never on Cloudflare) the relay warns, uses one `analytics:unknown` bucket and forwards no `x-client-ip`.
- **2026-09-29 · Screen views send the route template, not the URL.** The web sends TanStack Router's `fullPath` (`/lists/$token`); native sends Expo Router's segments without groups (`/lists/[token]`). The schema forbids `?`, `#` and spaces.
  - *Alternatives:* the old `location.href` (full URL).
  - *Why:* share links carry capability tokens (an edit link grants editing), and search params can hold free text. Neither may reach OpenPanel.
- **2026-09-29 · The gate remembers the identity and the current screen in memory while consent is missing.** When consent turns `true`, it sends `identify` and the open screen once. Withdrawing calls `transport.reset()` (web: drop the `profileId`; native: `OpenPanel.clear()`). A throwing consent reader counts as no consent.
  - *Why:* on the web the local store loads after the first render. Without this, the page someone allows on is never counted.
- **2026-09-29 · `handleAnalyticsRelay(request, { env, isForeign, limit, fetch?, waitUntil? })`** takes the origin check and the rate limit as functions: `@smog/analytics` may not import `@smog/rpc`. The site route passes `isForeignRequest` and `checkRateLimit(RL_ANALYTICS, "analytics:<ip>")`. The brief's signature was `(request, env)`. The forward runs in `waitUntil`. A 5xx from OpenPanel, a network error and a failing rate-limit binding all still answer 202 (logged `[analytics]`). Missing credentials warn once per isolate. Headers: `openpanel-client-id`, `openpanel-client-secret`, `openpanel-sdk-name: smog-relay`, `openpanel-sdk-version: 3.0.0`, `x-client-ip` (from `cf-connecting-ip`) and `user-agent`. A `screen_view`'s `path` is renamed to OpenPanel's `__path` when forwarded.
- **2026-09-29 · Native: `@openpanel/react-native` 1.4.1 (newest) with a `bun patch`** that loosens its peer ranges (`expo-application` `5 - 7` → `>=5`, `expo-constants` `14 - 18` → `>=14`), as the old repo did for SDK 55. SDK 57 works. The mobile app now depends on `expo-application` (~57.0.3, a peer of the SDK). The module is imported lazily, on the first consented event, so tests and the web never load it. No `storage` is passed, so no offline queue outlives the process or a withdraw. The SDK's `filter` asks the consent again before every send. `EXPO_PUBLIC_OPENPANEL_CLIENT_ID` / `_SECRET` are optional (analytics is off with one warning); `EXPO_PUBLIC_OPENPANEL_API_URL` defaults to `https://analytics.zias.be/api`.
- **2026-09-29 · Collection and search sources come from hook options.** `useFavorites({ source })`, `useList(id, { source })` and `useSharedList(token, { source })` default to `gesture_list`; `useGestureSearch({ source })` defaults to `filter_change`. Only changes that went through are tracked: a rejected add, a failed server flip or a failed import sends nothing. `search_performed` fires once per settled search (text and categories) when its results arrive, with `result_count = total`. The browse list (no text, no category) is not a search. `guest_data_imported.lists` is `listsCreated + listsMerged`.
- **2026-09-29 · `sign_in_completed`.** The auth screens call `useMarkSignInStarted()(method)` on the method choice. `useSignInCompleted(userId)` tracks the event when a user id appears with a mark younger than 15 minutes. The mark always lives in memory. With consent it is also written to `sessionStorage` (`smog:analytics:sign-in`), so it survives the Google, Apple or same-tab magic-link redirect; without consent nothing is written (see the 2026-09-30 entry). A magic link opened in another tab is not counted.
- **2026-09-30 · Consent source: `useConsent` (`@smog/account/client`).** Signed in, the account's consent log is the source; guests use the device. The apps feed it into the gate through `createConsentSource()` + `useSyncConsent(feed, useConsent().analytics)` (`@smog/analytics/react`), so the analytics package still knows nothing about the account package. While `useConsent` is loading, or a yes is under an older policy, it reports `null`, which counts as no consent. (Before this, the local store was read directly.)
- **2026-09-30 · `null` pauses the gate; only `false` closes it.** A signed-in user's consent query is keyed per user, so every sign-in passes through `null` while it loads.
  - During the pause nothing is sent and nothing is reset. The identity, the current screen and one `sign_in_completed` (the event taken from the mark) are held in memory.
  - On `true` the gate sends only what the transport does not have yet: no second identify, and no repeat of a screen already sent. Then it sends the held sign-in.
  - On `false` the held sign-in is dropped, and the transport is reset if it was used since the last close (web: drop the `profileId`; native: `clear()`).
  - A different user id while identified resets the transport before the new identify.
  - Other events during a pause are dropped, as before consent.
  - *Why:* the first round reset on every sign-in, lost `sign_in_completed` and repeated the screen view.
- **2026-09-30 · `gesture_viewed` and `video_playback_completed` on the mobile gesture screen.**
  - `useOpenGesture(from)` adds a `from` route param: `search_results`, `favorites` and `related_gestures`. A deep link has none, so it counts as `direct`, as do Home and list screens. An unknown value also falls back to `direct`. A deep link may carry `from` too (`smog://gestures/x?from=favorites`); the value is enum-checked, so the worst case is a mislabelled source, which is accepted.
  - The screen tracks `gesture_viewed` once per gesture shown, when its data arrives, and `video_playback_completed` once per visit, since the video loops.
  - The heart and "Save to list" on the gesture screen report `gesture_detail`; search cards report `search_results`; other cards report `gesture_list`.
  - The site has no gesture page yet. Its phase 3 page task wires both (`useAnalytics().track`). `sponsorship_checkout_started` waits for the sponsorship flow.
  - [fix wave A1, corrects the line above] The phase 3 page task did not wire them. The fix wave does, through shared hooks both apps now use (`useGestureViewed`, `useVideoCompleted`, `gestureViewSource` in `@smog/gestures/client`); see "Site analytics sources" under the phase 3 fix wave.
  - Mobile may import `@smog/analytics/schema` (the source enums), so the values are not written twice.
- **2026-09-30 · `search_performed` counts once per settled text, categories and source.** The mobile search tab sets the source from what changed: typing or a filter (`filter_change`), a submit or a `?q=` link from Home (`submit`), or a recent search (`recent_search`). A submit of the already-settled text therefore adds a `submit` event. A new source while the text is still debouncing waits for that text, so a source is never pinned on the previous query.
- **2026-09-30 · The sign-in mark writes `sessionStorage` only with consent.** The auth screens call `useMarkSignInStarted()`, which asks `analytics.isAllowed()`; without consent the mark stays in memory, so an in-page sign-in still counts after a later Allow. `Analytics.isAllowed()` is new on the interface.
- **2026-09-30 · Accepted: a client-supplied `profileId` is not tied to a session.** Anyone can post taxonomy events under any user id, but the payload is enums, counts and ids, so the impact is metric pollution. Verifying the session per event would cost a D1 read on every analytics call. Revisit if the metrics are polluted.
  - *Also accepted:* `@smog/analytics/testing` is allowed for all feature source, not only tests. It is tree-shaken, and knip flags it if prod code imports it without a test.

## Phase 3 fix wave, group A (site analytics, gestures client, docs)

- **2026-09-30 · One course banner rule, in `@smog/gestures/client` (inventory L-13, bug 21).** `useCourseBanner({ gestureId, messageCount, storage })` and `createCourseProgress(storage)` replace the mobile app's own copy; both apps render their kit's `CourseBanner` from it.
  - A visit counts one video: the hook ignores repeated `onNearEnd` calls until the gesture id changes or the screen mounts again. Before, mobile counted every loop, so one clip left playing reached 7 in about 35 s.
  - Every 7th video (`VIDEO_COMPLETE_COUNT`) shows a random one of the kit's messages (`COURSE_MESSAGE_COUNT` is passed in, since a feature may not import a kit) until dismissed. The banner belongs to the gesture it was earned on: another gesture hides it.
  - Storage stays a dedicated device key, `smog:course-progress:v1` (the mobile key, so existing counts carry over), through a small `CourseStorage` interface (`getItem`/`setItem`). The site passes `webAdapter` (`@smog/local-store/web`), mobile passes AsyncStorage. Not a local-store field: it is device UI state, not guest data (nothing to import, clear or export), and a field would need a local-store v3 migration for no gain.
  - *Alternatives:* `preferences.videosCompleted` with a v3 migration; keeping the rule per app.
  - Mobile passes AsyncStorage itself, not `nativeAdapter`: the adapter's lazy `import()` does not run under Jest. The AsyncStorage Jest mock was `undefined` (the setup took `.default` of a CommonJS module), so storage calls threw inside the tests unnoticed; it is now the module itself.
- **2026-09-30 · Site analytics sources, as mobile reports them (I3).**
  - `gesture_viewed { gesture_id, source }` once per gesture shown. On `/gestures/$slug` the source is a validated `?from=` search param (`validateGestureSearch`: a known source is kept, anything else dropped, so it counts as `direct`). Browse cards set `search_results`, favorites `favorites` and related gestures `related_gestures`; home, lists and typed or shared URLs are `direct`. The browse page's detail column is `search_results`. `gestureHref(slug, from)` builds the link, and the kit link component turns its query string into router search params.
    - *Alternatives:* router history state (keeps URLs clean, but is lost when a card is opened in a new tab). Accepted: `?from=` shows in the address bar, so a URL copied from it keeps its source; the canonical link has none, and the share and QR URLs are built from the slug.
  - `video_playback_completed` once per visit from `onNearEnd`, never `onEnded` (a looping video does not fire `ended`).
  - Hearts: `useHearts(source)`. `gesture_detail` on a gesture (page and browse panel, related cards included), `search_results` on the browse page, `gesture_list` elsewhere.
  - `search_performed`: `SearchBox` reports how a search started (`onSubmit(query, "submit" | "recent_search")`). The browse page sets `submit` for Enter and for a `?q=` landing (Home's form, a link, back and forward), `recent_search` for a pick and `filter_change` for typing and chips. A recent search picked on Home lands as `?q=`, so it counts as `submit`.
  - The shared-list "add gestures" sheet searches with `useGestureSearch({ track: false })`: it is not a catalogue search (M10).
  - SaveToList's source on the site came with group B's `useListMembership` (`gesture_detail`). Mobile's shared-list add sheet (group B) also searches with `track: false`.
- **2026-09-30 · Site component tests run on Bun with happy-dom** (`bun test --preload ./src/test/dom-setup.ts src`, part of `@smog/site` `test`). `src/test/render.tsx` renders a screen in a memory router with the app's providers (guest session, memory local store, recording analytics, an API client that is never reached). mux-player is mocked, as in the kit's tests. The Worker tests stay in Vitest (`test/`). [dep] `@happy-dom/global-registrator` 20.14.5, `@testing-library/react` 16.3.3 and `@testing-library/dom` 10.4.2, the versions the kits use.
- **2026-09-30 · QR PNG download (inventory L-14, M1).** The QR dialog shows a `QRCodeSVG` at 256 px (level M, margin 2, black on white). "QR-code downloaden" draws that SVG onto a fixed 1024 × 1024 canvas, white behind it, at click time (`qrPngDataUrl`, `src/lib/qr-png.ts`), and saves it. [fix round 1, M3] It is not a `QRCodeCanvas`, which scales its backing store with `devicePixelRatio`: a 3072 px PNG and about 37 MB of canvas on a 3× phone. The PNG is saved as `smog-<slugify(slug) || "gesture">-qr.png`, like the old dialog (which slugged the name). i18n `gesture.qr.download` in nl, en and fr. The admin QR (L-14's admin half) comes with the admin gestures tab in phase 5.
- **2026-09-30 · One featured-gestures hook (M9).** `useFeaturedGestures(limit = FEATURED_LIMIT)` (8) in `@smog/gestures/client` is one `gestures.list` page (`gesturesPageOptions`), which the site's home loader prefetches with the same key. Mobile Home used a 50-gesture browse page sliced to 8. Mobile's persisted catalogue now holds that page for Home.
- **2026-09-30 · Search SSR in 3 D1 reads (M5, M8).** Worst case before: the session, the FTS candidates, a cold projection, the typo hydration and the categories (5).
  - The catalog snapshot (`getCatalogSnapshot`) is the typo projection plus the published categories with counts, loaded in one D1 batch per isolate and catalog version. `gestures.categories` serves from it, and falls back to a direct D1 read when KV or the snapshot fails.
  - `gestures.search` computes the typo matches from the snapshot first, then reads the FTS candidates and the first `limit` typo matches' summaries in one batch. The first `limit` are enough: the page needs `limit - direct` typo matches, and at most `direct` of the first `limit` are direct matches themselves. The typo tier still only applies below 5 direct results, and the typo rows are still re-read as published, so a stale snapshot never shows an unpublished gesture. That makes at most 2 reads (1 when the snapshot is warm).
  - `prefetchBrowse` runs the search, then the categories (from the same snapshot), so the page is at most 3 reads with the session. Browse without a query and home stay parallel: page + categories + session.
  - [fix round 1, M1] `catalog:version` is read with `cacheTtl: 30`: KV's minimum since 2026-01-30 (60 s, the first choice, is only the default). A bump reaches other isolates within the propagation delay plus 30 s, and category counts and names can lag by that much. A D1 write without a bump (a manual `wrangler d1 execute`, a data import) stays invisible until the isolate is recycled; a deploy recycles them all. So the phase 5 admin screens read categories from D1, and the phase 8 import bumps the version (PROGRESS carries).
  - [fix round 1, I1] The typo scoring is gated. `typoCandidates` (ranking) first counts the snapshot's direct matches on name, keywords and categories, stopping at 5. The FTS candidates are a superset of those (the same tokens, prefix-matched, far under the 200 cap). With 5 or more, the tier cannot apply, so there is no Damerau–Levenshtein pass and no typo rows. Otherwise the pass runs over the snapshot's already-normalised values (`normalized: true`, which skips `normalizeQuery` per value). So only queries with at most 4 in-memory hits pay the typo cost, which is the old behaviour. A stale snapshot can at worst skip the tier for a query whose matches just changed.
    - Measured (bun on this container, synthetic catalogues, median of 9), for a query matching every gesture's category:

      | Catalogue | Ungated typo pass | Gated path |
      |---|---|---|
      | 2,000 gestures | 83 ms | 0.1 ms |
      | 5,000 gestures | 162 ms | under 0.1 ms |

    - A typo query with no direct hits costs 17 ms at 2,000 and 33 ms at 5,000 with the normalised fast path (27 and 71 ms without it).
    - `src/typo-gate.test.ts` checks this against a baseline, not an absolute time: the gated path must be under a fifth of the ungated pass on 5,000 gestures, and the fast path must beat the full one.
    - [2026-09-30, phase 4 task 6 fix round 1] That timing comparison timed out (bun's 5 s per-test default) when turbo ran every suite in parallel, so it is now an opt-in benchmark: `bun -F @smog/gestures bench` (`SMOG_PERF=1`), skipped in `test`. `test` instead checks the gate deterministically: over 5,000 gestures whose fields record each read, the gated path reads exactly 5 gestures (it stops at the 5th direct match, so no Damerau–Levenshtein pass runs), and the ungated pass reads all 5,000. The measured numbers above still stand.
  - [fix round 1, M4] The rows-read cost: a cold categories read (Home, the browse page) loads the whole published projection in the same batch (every gesture's name, keywords and categories), once per isolate and catalog version. That is one read, which is fine at today's size (about 0.3 MB of JSON per 2,000 gestures). Revisit if the catalogue grows past about 10,000 or if isolates are recycled often.
  - *Alternatives:* summaries in the projection with no hydration (rejected: the running sponsorship's video and publish state change without a catalog bump); categories in the search response (a contract change); documenting 5.
  - Phase 5 carry, unchanged: category writes must bump too, since the snapshot holds the categories.
- **2026-09-30 · Offline search: none on the device (M4, spec §7.1/§10).** The spec said the ranking "runs on the device for the mobile offline cache". Nothing did, and the persisted catalogue (summaries without keywords or descriptions) could not rank like the server anyway. Spec §7.1 and §10 now say what the code does: search needs a connection. Offline, the Search tab shows the offline banner and the search waits for the connection (TanStack Query pauses it while `onlineManager` is offline). Home, gesture pages, favorites and lists work from the cache. `rankGestures` stays exported from `@smog/gestures/client` for a later on-device fallback.
  - *Alternative:* rank the cached `gestures.list` pages when offline. Rejected for now: it would give a second, weaker ranking (names and categories only) behind the same field.

- **2026-09-30 · [fix round 1] `?from=` leaves the URL once it is tracked (M6).** The gesture page reads `?from=`, and the detail tracks `gesture_viewed` with it (the child's effect runs first, in the same commit). Then the page strips it with the router: `navigate({ search: without from, replace: true, resetScroll: false })`, never `history.replaceState`, which would desync TanStack Router. A reload or a copied address therefore counts as `direct`. The root passes unvalidated search params through (`useSearch` still showed `from: "bogus"` after `validateSearch` dropped it), so the page checks `from` again with `gestureViewSource`. The new route test found this.
- **2026-09-30 · [fix round 1] One `onNearEnd` for both apps (M7).** `useVideoEnd({ gestureId, messageCount, storage })` in `@smog/gestures/client` pairs `useVideoCompleted` with the course banner's count and returns `{ banner, onNearEnd }`. The site detail and the mobile gesture screen call it instead of composing the two themselves.
- **2026-09-30 · [fix round 1] One happy-dom preload (M8).** `@smog/config/testing/happy-dom` now registers happy-dom with the dev origin (`http://localhost:5173/`, so `localStorage` works), and after each test runs Testing Library's cleanup and clears `localStorage`. The site's component tests use it, and its own `dom-setup.ts` is gone. `@testing-library/react` 16.3.3 is a devDependency of `@smog/config`. `src/test/render.tsx` now also mounts the app's own file routes (`route.update`, as `routeTree.gen.ts` does) over a fake RPC fetch, so route-level wiring is tested: `?from=`, the browse page's search sources and the panel's source (M5).

## Rule changes recorded from the feature inventory (2026-09-29)

- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] Guests are device-only (user brief §3.2).** No guest rows exist, so the old monthly guest-cleanup cron, guest consent rows, `migrateGuestToUser` and the "12 months inactivity" guest retention go away. The privacy page says guest data never leaves the device.
- **2026-09-29 · [improvement] Web guests get favorites and lists** (stored on the device); the old web required sign-in. Sharing still requires an account.
- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] Auth provider: WorkOS → Better Auth** (user brief). New methods: email + password, email code, magic link, Google, Apple, passkeys. Migrated users sign in with a code, a magic link or social login, or set a password through "forgot password".
- **2026-09-29 · [ux] Route names:** `/sign-in`, `/sign-up`, `/forgot-password` … replace `/login` and `/callback`; the old paths redirect (301).
- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] Category filter URLs use slugs** (`/gestures?category=begroetingen`) instead of names. Old `?category=<Name>` values are mapped to slugs by `normalizeText` + `slugify` on read.
- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] Sponsorship CSV export columns follow the new model:** sponsor, invoice request, payment (Mollie id, status, paid at), items (amount, logo), sponsorship (status, starts, ends). The old columns are all still present, under the same headers where the meaning is the same; added columns are appended.
- **2026-09-29 · Rate limits use Workers Rate Limiting bindings**, with the old numbers kept: API 300/60 s, sponsor actions 20/3600 s, auth 30/900 s, analytics 120/60 s. (Superseded for the windows: the binding has 10 s/60 s periods only, see "Rate limits: the binding only has 10 s and 60 s periods" under Architecture.)
- **2026-09-29 · [improvement] Mux webhooks** (`video.asset.ready`, `video.asset.master.ready`, `video.upload.asset_created`, `video.asset.errored`) replace polling, with polling kept as a fallback in the render Workflow.
- **2026-09-29 · Search typo tolerance** is implemented in TypeScript (spec §7.1): Damerau–Levenshtein similarity ≥ 0.6 as the successor of Fuse.js threshold 0.4, with the same field weights and a 150 multiplier.
- **2026-09-29 · [ux] Mobile gets a Favorites tab** (Home · Search · Favorites · Lists); before, favorites were a default list inside Lists.
- **2026-09-29 · [improvement] Legal pages are localized** (nl canonical; en/fr translations marked as translations). The old pages were Dutch only.
- **2026-09-29 · [improvement] Turnstile** on sign-up, sign-in, password reset, code and magic-link requests, sponsor checkout and re-edit submission (bot protection; skipped when no key is configured).
- **2026-09-29 · Cutover compatibility:** unexpired re-edit tokens are migrated (hashed), and `POST /webhooks/mollie` stays as an alias for 30 days (spec §15).
- **2026-09-29 · No sponsor email on rejection or change request (parity).** Admins contact the sponsor manually, as before. The admin screen shows the re-edit link to copy.
- **2026-09-29 · Subjects of new emails** (`admin_render_failed`, `admin_refund_needed`, auth emails, `we_moved`) are copy decisions made in `@smog/i18n` (nl canonical); they are reviewed in the phase 6 email pass.

## Phase 3 fix wave B: lists, favorites persistence, import, mobile (2026-09-30)

- **2026-09-30 · The mobile offline cache keeps signed-in favorites, busts per bundle, and is capped.** This refines "What the offline cache keeps" above. `favorites.list` joins `favorites.ids` as a user procedure: it is persisted only while scoped to a user, and it gets the 24 h `gcTime`. A signed-in user's favorites tab then renders offline like a guest's (inventory L-21). The buster is `<app version>:<expo-updates updateId | runtimeVersion | "embedded">` (`appCacheBuster()` in `providers.tsx`). An EAS Update ships new JS under the same app version, so a cache written by the previous bundle, in a possibly older output shape, is never restored into it. The serializer refuses a cache above `QUERY_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` (1 MB of UTF-8). [Review fix M1] The retry (`trimToFit`) then trims in one pass: it sizes each query once and drops the oldest until the rest fits, so an oversized cache costs about two full serializations per write, not one per dropped query. A full storage still drops one oldest query per retry (`removeOldestQuery`). This keeps the one AsyncStorage row well under Android's roughly 2 MB CursorWindow read limit, since a failed read would lose the whole cache. Alternatives: trimming inside `dehydrateOptions`, which has no size in hand, and a per-query size filter, which would still let many small entries add up.
- **2026-09-30 · A guest's favorites pages are guest-scoped, one variant is kept, and a sign-in drops them.** `useFavorites` keys the guest's `gestures.byIds` pages as `userScopedKey([...byIds, { ids: order }], undefined)`, and removes every other guest-scoped `byIds` variant once the current one has loaded. Each heart tap used to leave one more variant, in memory and on disk, for 24 h. `isOtherUsersKey` now also counts the guest scope (`{ user: null }`) as "another user" while someone is signed in. So `purgeOtherUsers`, which the site root, the mobile root, `PurgeRestoredQueries` and the feature hooks run, drops a guest's pages at sign-in, and they leave the next cache write. Guest queries stay while signed out. Alternatives: dropping the 24 h `gcTime` for `byIds`, which would break a guest's offline favorites 5 minutes after launch, and keying each 100-id page separately, which would need `useQueries` in place of the infinite query.
- **2026-09-30 · `lists.containing` and one `useListMembership` for "Save to list".** This supersedes "Save to list on the gesture screen reads each list through `useList`" above. The new `lists.containing({ gestureId })` procedure (`requireUser`) returns the owner's list ids holding the gesture. It is one query, bounded by `LISTS_MAX`, and its query-plan test checks that nothing is scanned. `useListMembership(gestureId, { enabled, source })` in `@smog/lists/client` returns `{ lists: { id, name, contains, pending }[], toggle, createWith, status }`. Guests read and write the device lists. Signed in, the hook reads `containing` only while `enabled` (the picker is open) and lays each toggle over it at once. `pending` blocks a second tap until the call settles, a failure rolls back and rejects, and the lists are refetched afterwards. `toggle` resolves to `{ action, name }` for the toast, or `null` when it ignored the tap. [Review fix M2] While signed in and `containing` has not answered, every entry is `pending`, and the kit `ListPicker` (a new optional `pending` on `ListPickerList`, in both kits) disables its checkbox. The picker never shows a guessed "unchecked". Both apps' `SaveToList` are now the kit `ListPicker` plus toasts, with source `gesture_detail`. The old mobile component looped ("maximum update depth") when signed in with lists. No new kit component: the kits never import feature hooks, and what is left per app is about 15 lines of toast wording.
- **2026-09-30 · List positions are renumbered in the write.** `removeItem` and `reorder` end with one statement that renumbers the list's rows as they exist when it runs. It is `UPDATE … FROM` a `row_number()` ranking: payload order first, then any other row by its current position. So a remove that lands between a reorder's read and its write leaves no gap, and an add in the same window goes after the rest. A correlated scalar subquery per row does not work here: SQLite re-evaluates it per row and sees rows it has already renumbered, which gave two rows the same position in a test. A gesture delete (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) can still leave a gap until the list's next write. Positions order the items and readers sort on them. Nothing may index by position, and that includes the phase 8 data migration.
- **2026-09-30 · Concurrent guest imports: a guard row, not a name key.** The import's write batch now begins with `INSERT INTO guest_import (user_id, seq)`, without `ON CONFLICT`, where `seq` is one more than what its read batch saw (migration `0004_guest_import_guard`, primary key `(user_id, seq)`). When two imports are planned from the same read, the second batch fails on the key and D1 rolls it back whole. It then re-reads and plans again, so it merges into the lists the first import created (up to 3 tries). Only this guard's `UNIQUE constraint failed: guest_import` counts as a conflict; any other failure is thrown at once. [Review fix I1] Every row is kept until the account is deleted, about one per sign-in with guest data. A cleanup that deleted the older rows let a third import reopen the race: B and A read `seq` 0, A writes 1, C reads 1 and writes 2 and deletes 1, and then B's stale insert of 1 succeeded. A test runs two imports between one import's read and its write. The table holds nothing but the user id and a counter; it cascades on account deletion and is not part of the export. Alternatives: a normalised `name_key` column with `WHERE NOT EXISTS` (SQLite's `lower()` is ASCII-only while the import matches with `toLocaleLowerCase("und")`, so existing rows would need a backfill in JS), and a payload-hash row (it would refuse a legitimate repeat of the same import).
- **2026-09-30 · An authorization matrix over `appContract`.** `packages/api/test/authz.test.ts` classifies every procedure as public (`gestures.*`, `lists.shared.get`, `system.*`) or signed-in. A procedure in neither list fails the test, and so does a stale entry. Every signed-in procedure must answer `UNAUTHORIZED` to an anonymous call. Input validation runs before the guard, so each signed-in procedure carries a valid input; an anonymous call with no input answers `BAD_REQUEST` and proves nothing.
- **2026-09-30 · One i18n key per lists message.** `lists.added`, `lists.removed`, `lists.sharedBy` and `lists.sharedSignIn` are retired. The kept keys are `lists.addedTo`/`lists.removedFrom` (with the quoted wording) and `lists.sharedView.by`/`lists.sharedView.signInToEdit`. A catalogue test fails when two `lists.*` keys have the same text, apart from quotes and placeholder names.
- **2026-09-30 · Mobile edit links can add (U-21, parity with the site).** A signed-in user on an edit link gets an "Add gestures" header action. It opens a bottom sheet (`src/components/add-to-shared-list.tsx`) with the kit SearchField and `useGestureSearch` results, and each result adds through `useSharedList().addItem`. [Review fix M3] `addItem` now resolves `true` only when the list gained the gesture. A repeat tap while one add is in flight is ignored (`false`), and so is a gesture that was already in the list (`added: false`). Neither sends `gesture_collection_changed`, and mobile shows no toast for them. The site shows no success toast at all. Gestures already in the list show "In the list" instead. There is no `useSharedListAdder` hook: the body is the same two feature hooks and a `Set` on both apps, so a hook would only rename them. The sheet's search still sends `search_performed`. When A1 adds the opt-out to `useGestureSearch` (review M10), it goes here too.
- **2026-09-30 · Local e2e runs use `CI=1`.** Playwright's `reuseExistingServer` would otherwise attach to a dev server another worktree left on port 5173, and test that worktree's code. The four capability flows of review I10 are in `lists.spec.ts` (edit link by a second user, revoke then 404) and `favorites.spec.ts` (sign-out after the import, account switch). A revoked link shows the site's generic 404 page with HTTP 404.
- **2026-09-30 · Mobile Jest `testTimeout` is 10 s.** The first test of a suite that renders the real routes (`app-shell.test.tsx`, `screens.test.tsx`) pays the cold require of every route module. That takes under 1 s alone (the review measured every test under 0.9 s), but it passed the 5 s default when the whole turbo test run was in parallel on a loaded machine. 10 s matches the phase 4 task 3 branch. Waits inside the tests are unchanged, so a real hang still fails.

## Mobile Turnstile and magic link (phase 4 task 5)

- **2026-09-30 · The auth flow asks for the captcha; the app answers with a WebView sheet.** `useAuthFlow({ requiresCaptcha, requestCaptcha })` (`@smog/auth/react`): when the server requires a captcha (the app reads `system.authConfig`, `turnstileSiteKey` set), every captcha-guarded command (password sign-in and sign-up, code and magic-link requests and resends, verification resend, reset request) first awaits `requestCaptcha()` for a fresh token and hands it to the action (`AuthFlowActions` guarded calls take an optional `captchaToken`, which `createFlowActions` sends as `x-captcha-response` in preference to the widget's). The request stays `pending` during the challenge; `null` (the sheet was closed) sends nothing and clears `pending` without an error; a thrown challenge is `captchaFailed`. No token is kept or reused. Code sign-in, social and passkeys never ask. The web keeps its inline widget (it does not pass `requiresCaptcha`). Alternatives: an async `captchaToken` option in `createFlowActions` only (the flow could not keep the request pending or tell a closed sheet from a failure), or a captcha step in the reducer (more states for the same outcome).
- **2026-09-30 · `/turnstile-bridge` is a server route with its own CSP.** A plain HTML response (no app shell, no hydration) from `apps/site/src/server/turnstile-bridge.ts`: the widget with explicit rendering, the site key and texts in a JSON data block (`scriptJson`), and a script that runs only when `window.ReactNativeWebView` exists and posts `{ source: "smog-turnstile", type: "token" | "error" | "expired" }` to it and nowhere else (no `parent`/`opener`/`window.postMessage`); in a normal browser it loads no widget and shows "only in the app". Headers are local to the route, so phase 4 task 6's site-wide headers do not affect it: `default-src 'none'; script-src 'nonce-<per response>' https://challenges.cloudflare.com; style-src 'unsafe-inline'` (Turnstile sizes its frame inline)`; frame-src https://challenges.cloudflare.com; img-src 'self' data:; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, plus `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`. The nonce is Cloudflare's recommended CSP approach and is set on the dynamically added `api.js`. The page is not gated on the user agent: the app appends `SmogApp` to it (`applicationNameForUserAgent`), but a user agent proves nothing, and a token solved on our host is only useful to whoever solved it. Dev without `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` serves Cloudflare's always-pass test key (`1x00000000000000000000AA`), so the page can be exercised locally; the dev server checks no token. Without a key outside dev the route answers 404 (the app never opens it then).
- **2026-09-30 · The sheet trusts the site origin only.** `TurnstileSheet` (`apps/mobile/src/components/turnstile-sheet.tsx`) loads `<EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL origin>/turnstile-bridge?lang=<locale>` in `react-native-webview` with `originWhitelist: [site origin, https://challenges.cloudflare.com]`, `incognito`, and `onShouldStartLoadWithRequest` (`allowBridgeLoad`) allowing only the site as the top frame and the site or `https://challenges.cloudflare.com` in subframes. [Review fix C1] The library checks `originWhitelist` before our handler, for iframe navigations too on iOS (WKWebView), and opens anything outside it in Safari: with only the site listed, Turnstile's frame never rendered on iOS. The whitelist is a prefix match (`^https://site` also matches `https://site.evil.test`), so `allowBridgeLoad`'s exact origin check stays the real gate; a test drives the library's own `createOnShouldStartLoadWithRequest` with the sheet's props. `onMessage` accepts a message only when `nativeEvent.url`'s origin is the site origin and the JSON has our `source`, a known `type` and a 1–4096 character token (`parseBridgeMessage`). [Review M7] `nativeEvent.url` is the top frame's URL, not the sender's, and on Android the bridge object is injected into every frame, so this check cannot tell which frame posted; it holds only because the one possible subframe is Cloudflare's (the navigation lock and the page's CSP `frame-src`). Never rely on the origin check alone. A token resolves the one waiting request and closes the sheet; an error shows a retry that mounts a fresh WebView. The brief named `EXPO_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`; the app has no such variable, and `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` is the site origin (the API is the same Worker).
- **2026-09-30 · `react-native-webview` 13.16.1, not 14.0.1.** 14.0.1 is npm's latest, but Expo SDK 57's `bundledNativeModules.json` pins 13.16.1, which is what `expo install` picks and what expo-doctor checks. App-only, so not in the catalog. Revisit with the next SDK.
- **2026-09-30 · Magic links in the app: a universal link and an in-app exchange, never a cookie in a URL.** `@better-auth/expo` documents no deep-link session handoff for magic links. Its server hook does append the whole `Set-Cookie` (the 7-day session token) as `?cookie=` when `/magic-link/verify` redirects to `smog://`, but only its OAuth browser session reads it, and a custom scheme can be claimed by another app and lands in browser history, so we do not use it. Instead: when a magic-link request's `callbackURL` is not http(s) (`smog://`, `exp://` in dev; Better Auth's origin check already required it to be trusted), `sendMagicLink` mails `<SITE_URL>/magic-link/app?token=<token>` (`appMagicLinkURL`, `APP_MAGIC_LINK_PATH` in `@smog/auth`) with every callback dropped, so the link can redirect nowhere. The app claims that exact path (Android `path`, iOS AASA); `+native-intent` maps it to the app's `/magic-link?token=` with a well-formed token only, and `MagicLinkScreen` calls `magicLink.verify({ query: { token } })` without a callback: Better Auth then answers JSON and sets the cookie, which the Expo client stores in SecureStore. The token is the 5-minute (`MAGIC_LINK_TTL_SECONDS`, tested), single-use, hashed-at-rest magic-link token. Our code never logs it (a thrown error logs its name only). [Review M2] But Workers observability (`observability.enabled`) records each invocation as `<method> <URL>`, so `/magic-link/app?token=…` and `/api/auth/magic-link/verify?token=…` are in Workers logs, as with Better Auth's own link. Workers Logs has no setting to redact query strings (only `enabled`, `head_sampling_rate` and `invocation_logs: false`, which drops invocation logs altogether); we keep the logs, since the token is short-lived and single-use. Opened in a browser (no app, desktop, Expo Go), `/magic-link/app` explains the link is for the app and offers "Sign in in this browser", which is the verify endpoint with fixed callbacks (`/`, errors to `/magic-link`). Web requests keep Better Auth's own link. The app now offers the magic-link method. Supersedes point (2) of the "Mobile auth gaps, confirmed" entry for magic links, and point (1) for Turnstile; the verification and reset links still open the site. **Open:** the site's `apple-app-site-association` must list `/magic-link/app` (phase 4 task 4 owns that file); until then iOS opens the link in Safari, which shows the browser fallback.
- **2026-09-30 · [security fix] Search validators set every key they own.** The root route validates no search, so its raw params flow into every child's `search`, and a child `validateSearch` that omits a key leaves the raw value in place. `validateAuthSearch` omitted an unsafe `redirect`, so `/magic-link?redirect=https://evil.test` answered 307 to `https://evil.test` (an open redirect), and `/sign-in?redirect=…` carried the raw value into its links and callbacks. It now returns `{ error, redirect }` with `undefined` for an absent or unsafe value, and `/magic-link/app` returns `{ token: string | null }`. `test/magic-link-app.test.ts` follows the redirects and checks every hop stays on the site.
- **2026-09-30 · [Review fix I1] The app exchanges a magic link without asking only when it asked for it.** Anyone can request a link for their own account and get it opened in the app (a web page, a QR code, another app; custom schemes cannot be verified): a zero-click exchange would sign the app into the sender's account (login CSRF). The link carries only the token (no address: that would put personal data in Workers logs and the address bar). `MagicLinkScreen` decides:
  - Signed out, with a request from this app pending (`markMagicLinkRequested` in memory, within `MAGIC_LINK_TTL_SECONDS`): it exchanges at once, and the new session must be the address requested; otherwise the app signs out again and says the link is for another account.
  - Signed out otherwise (no request, expired, or the app restarted): "Sign in with the link from your email?", and a tap exchanges.
  - Signed in: "Switch account?" (naming the current account), and a tap signs the current session out first, then exchanges.
  - The success toast names the account of the new session (`auth.signedInAs`), so a swap is visible.
  - `+native-intent` maps only `/magic-link/app` (https, `smog://` and `exp://` in dev) to the screen; bare `/magic-link?token=` and `smog://magic-link?token=` go home. The AASA needs only the path, no query matching.
  Alternatives: the address in the link (personal data in logs and the address bar), or a server endpoint that reveals a token's address before use (couples to Better Auth's token hashing).
- **2026-09-30 · [Review fix M1] The captcha requirement fails safe in the app.** Until `system.authConfig` has loaded (or when it failed), `requiresCaptcha` is false. A guarded request then goes out without a token, and if the captcha plugin answers `MISSING_RESPONSE` (`toAuthResult` flags it as `captchaMissing`), the flow runs the challenge and retries once with the token. A rejected token (`VERIFICATION_FAILED`) is not retried. The web has no on-demand challenge, so it never retries.
- **2026-09-30 · [Review fix M3, M6] No token in the header's "Sign in" link; `/reset-password` sets its keys.** `signInReturnPath` (`apps/site/src/lib/redirect.ts`) drops any `token` query parameter from the return path and never returns to `/sign-*` or `/magic-link*`. `validateResetSearch` always sets `error` and `token` (strings only; the router JSON-parses `?token=123` to a number).

## Maintenance mode, security headers and CSP (phase 4 task 6)

- **2026-09-30 · The CSP carries a per-request nonce as well as the theme hash.**
  - TanStack Start streams its hydration data as inline `<script>`s whose content changes per request (`$_TSR`, Seroval records, the stream boundary), so no hash can allow them. The brief's `script-src 'self' 'sha256-<theme>' https://challenges.cloudflare.com` would stop the site from hydrating.
  - `src/worker.ts` makes a 128-bit nonce per request and passes it to Start as request context (`handler.fetch(request, { context: { nonce } })`). `src/router.tsx` sets `ssr.nonce` from `getGlobalStartContext()`, so Start puts it on every script it writes.
  - The theme pre-paint script keeps its hash. `build-defines.ts` hashes `SYSTEM_THEME_SCRIPT` at build time into the `__SMOG_THEME_SCRIPT_HASH__` define (vite.config.ts and vitest.config.ts), and a test hashes the served page's script again.
  - Alternatives: `'unsafe-inline'` (no protection), and `'strict-dynamic'`. Under `'strict-dynamic'` browsers ignore `'self'` and the host list, so it would need `https:` fallbacks and a Turnstile retest, and it would make this policy no safer.
  - [fix round 1, M5] **The nonce is readable in the DOM.** Start emits `<meta property="csp-nonce" content="…">` (its client reads it back for scripts it adds later), and browsers do not hide a meta's content the way they hide `script.nonce`. With `style-src 'unsafe-inline'`, injected CSS could select on that attribute. It could only exfiltrate through `img-src`/`font-src`, which list our own origin, Mux and Google's avatar host. This is Start's design and is accepted. Any later widening of `img-src` or `font-src` must weigh it.
- **2026-09-30 · The directives beyond the nonce.**
  - They are the brief's, with two changes made in fix round 1:
    - `img-src` adds exactly `https://lh3.googleusercontent.com`, so Google accounts show their profile picture (never a `*.googleusercontent.com` wildcard). Copying the picture to R2 at sign-in, so a page view does not send the viewer's IP to Google, is the better long-term option.
    - `font-src` drops `https://fonts.gstatic.com`: the site loads no web fonts.
  - `https://stream.mux.com` next to `https://*.mux.com` in `media-src` is redundant and kept as the brief wrote it.
  - Mux serves renditions and segments from several `*.mux.com` hosts, so the wildcard in `connect-src`/`media-src` is needed (the e2e fetches a rendition and a segment from `*.cfcdn.mux.com` stubs).
  - `inferred.litix.io` stays for Mux Data, although the player runs with `disableTracking`, so no beacon is sent today (the e2e cannot show it).
- **2026-09-30 · Header rules.**
  - One function (`respondSecurely`, `src/worker/headers.ts`) is the Worker's `fetch` body. It makes the nonce, runs the pipeline (the maintenance gate, the bypass endpoint, the legacy 301s, Start), and adds the headers with `withSecurityHeaders` to whatever comes back.
  - [fix round 1, M2] An exception that escapes the pipeline is logged (`[site] Failed to handle the request:`) and answered with a plain 500 (`no-store`) that has the headers. Without that, Cloudflare's error page would go out with none.
  - Every response gets `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy` and, outside dev, `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains` (the old Caddy value, no preload).
  - HTML responses and redirects also get the CSP, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Permissions-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`. `/api/*` JSON gets only the first set.
  - [fix round 1, M3] HTML also gets `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` when the route set none. The page carries its response's nonce, so no shared cache (a later Cache Rule, a proxy) may serve it to someone else. `no-cache` still allows the back/forward cache.
  - A header the route already set is never overwritten. A route that sends its own `Content-Security-Policy` or `-Report-Only` gets neither CSP header from the wrapper, because two policies would both apply. `/turnstile-bridge` relies on this (tested); it takes its nonce from the same `createNonce`.
  - Static assets are served before the Worker, so `public/_headers` gives them `nosniff` and the `Referrer-Policy`.
- **2026-09-30 · CSP modes: production and dev enforce, staging reports.**
  - Staging sends `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`, as the brief says. It has no report endpoint yet, so violations show only in the browser console. A same-origin `report-to`/`report-uri` endpoint is a phase 8 carry (docs/PROGRESS.md).
  - Dev enforces, so `bun dev`, the Vitest suite and the whole Playwright suite run under the production policy. Vite's dev client needs nothing more: its modules are same-origin, its HMR socket is `'self'`, and its styles are inline.
  - Alternatives: Report-Only in dev (the e2e would then prove nothing), or a separate CSP flag for tests (a second configuration to keep in step).
- **2026-09-30 · Maintenance: what the gate lets through, and how.**
  - The gate runs first in the Worker's pipeline, before the legacy redirects: their category lookup reads D1, which may be the thing under maintenance.
  - Exempt paths:
    - `/api/health`, `/api/webhooks/*`, `/.well-known/*`;
    - [fix round 1, I2] `/api/auth/*` (it has `RL_AUTH` per IP and the captcha) and the `/sign-in` page, so an admin can sign in during a window; [Phase 4 fix wave A] superseded: only `AUTH_SIGN_IN_ROUTES` pass, served by a sign-in-only auth (see "Phase 4 fix wave A");
    - `POST /api/maintenance/bypass`.
  - Matching is by exact path or whole segments on the normalised URL. `/api/healthz`, `/sign-in/x` and `/sign-up` are blocked. `..` and `%2e%2e` are resolved before the check (tested). Everything else, `/account` included, stays 503 for a signed-in admin until they hold the bypass cookie (tested). Start's JS and CSS are static assets, and the sign-in page needs nothing else from the Worker.
  - A blocked document gets the static page. A blocked `/api/*` call gets JSON `{ code: "MAINTENANCE", message?, until }`. Both are 503 with `Retry-After` (seconds until `until`, else 600) and `Cache-Control: no-store`. `HEAD` gets the same 503 with an empty body.
  - The page has no script, no font and no asset request: the logo and hands are inline SVG from `@smog/brand/svg`, and the colours come from the `@smog/styles` tokens. It follows the `locale` cookie, then `Accept-Language`, like the site, and the `theme` cookie (system by default). The operator's `message` is shown as escaped text under the standard copy; it is one string, not translated.
  - [review M8] An exempt prefix with no route (`/api/webhooks/*` until phase 6) renders Start's SSR 404, which may read the session. Once webhooks exist, consider answering unknown exempt paths with a plain 404 in the Worker.
- **2026-09-30 · Maintenance state: KV, cached 30 s, fails open.**
  - `readMaintenance` caches the parsed value per isolate for 30 s and reads KV with `cacheTtl: 30` (KV's minimum; the default is 60), so on/off reaches every isolate within about a minute.
  - [fix round 1, I2] The bypass cookie is signed, and checked, against a fresh KV read that skips the isolate cache and refreshes it. That costs one extra read per request carrying the cookie while the cache says "on". So a window's version is never taken from a stale isolate. [Phase 5 task 6, fix round 1] "Fresh" here means past the isolate cache only: KV's own 30 s edge cache and its propagation still apply (Task 6, "Propagation").
  - A malformed value, or a KV read that throws, is logged and treated as "off" (the last known state while cached): a KV hiccup must not take the site down.
  - The value is validated by hand, with no zod in the Worker's hot path: `enabled` boolean, `bypassVersion` integer, optional `message` string, and optional `until` parseable date.
- **2026-09-30 · Bypass cookie and endpoint.**
  - The cookie is `smog_mx=<exp>.<base64url HMAC-SHA-256(exp|bypassVersion, BETTER_AUTH_SECRET)>`, 12 h, `Path=/; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax`, plus `Secure` outside dev. [fix round 1, M1] Dev runs on http://localhost or a LAN address.
  - Verification uses `crypto.subtle.verify` (constant time) and checks the expiry, so a forged, tampered, expired or earlier-version cookie gets the 503 (tested). The gate reads no session: the signature is enough.
  - [review M7] The cookie is not bound to a user, so a demoted admin keeps it until it expires or the window ends (`off` voids it). The endpoint itself re-reads the role on every call (Better Auth has no cookie cache here), so a demoted admin gets no new cookie (tested).
  - `POST /api/maintenance/bypass` is handled in the Worker entry, not as a Start route (so `routeTree.gen.ts` is untouched). In order, it:
    - refuses foreign requests with `isForeignRequest` (403);
    - [fix round 1, I1] applies `RL_AUTH` per IP (`mx:<ip>`, 429 with `retry-after: 60`), so a caller cannot hammer D1 during a window;
    - requires an admin session through `getSession` + `requireAdminUser` (401/403);
    - answers `{ expiresAt }` with the cookie.
  - The endpoint is exempt from the gate, and it also works while maintenance is off.
  - Until the phase 5 settings screen, an admin signs in at `/sign-in` (before or during the window) and runs `fetch("/api/maintenance/bypass", { method: "POST" })` in the browser console. Phase 5's toggle should set the admin's cookie in the same response that turns maintenance on.
- **2026-09-30 · `bun run maintenance`.**
  - Usage: `scripts/maintenance.ts --env <env> on|off [--message …] [--until ISO] [--dry-run] [--yes]`.
  - It first reads the key (`wrangler kv key get maintenance --binding KV --env <env> --text`), then writes it with `wrangler kv key put maintenance <json> --binding KV --env <env>`, from `apps/site`. It uses `--local` in dev and `--remote` in staging and production (wrangler 4 defaults to local).
  - [fix round 1, I2] `on` keeps the current `bypassVersion` (the current Unix second when there is no key yet), so a cookie fetched before or during a window keeps working. `off` writes `max(now, current + 1)`, which voids the cookies of the window that just ended.
  - Production is refused without `--yes`, before anything runs (also with `--dry-run`). `--dry-run` prints both commands, with the version as a placeholder. `--until` must be a future ISO date and goes with `on` only.

## Phase 4 fix wave A

- **2026-09-30 · Maintenance: only an existing admin's sign-in passes `/api/auth` (review I1, ruled).**
  - The `/api/auth/` prefix exemption let sign-ups, resets, emails and account writes run through a window: a sign-up during the phase 8 import would clash with an imported user's email, and every mailed link lands on a 503.
  - Now `AUTH_SIGN_IN_ROUTES` (`worker/maintenance.ts`), matched by method and exact path (`:provider` is one lowercase segment): `POST sign-in/email`, `POST sign-in/social`, `GET|POST callback/:provider`, `POST email-otp/send-verification-otp`, `POST sign-in/email-otp`, `POST sign-in/magic-link`, `GET magic-link/verify`, `GET passkey/generate-authenticate-options`, `POST passkey/verify-authentication`, `GET|POST get-session`, `POST sign-out`. Every other `/api/auth` route, a wrong method or a longer path gets the JSON 503. A test lists each allowed and each blocked route, and another keeps the list and the test equal.
  - The gate answers those routes with `SIGN_IN_ONLY`, and the Worker serves them with a second Better Auth instance, `createAuth({ signInOnly: true })` (`RL_AUTH` still applies):
    - `disableSignUp` for email and password, email OTP, magic link and each social provider;
    - `databaseHooks.user.create.before` refuses every create (`SIGN_UP_DISABLED`), whatever the path: the backstop;
    - a magic link is mailed only to an address with an account (Better Auth sends before it checks); an unknown address gets no code either (`disableSignUp` in email OTP);
    - `/email-otp/send-verification-otp` sends `type: "sign-in"` only (a `hooks.before` 403 otherwise), so no reset or verification code goes out.
  - Why a second instance and not a per-request check in one: Better Auth reads `disableSignUp` from its options, not per request, and the auth package would otherwise need the maintenance KV read. The instance is built once per isolate, like the first.
  - Social sign-in through its callbacks can no longer create a user during a window (the review's alternative was to accept and document it). The app's social proxy (`/expo-authorization-proxy`) and `/magic-link/app` stay blocked: the app gets 503s from `/api/rpc` anyway.
  - `/api/analytics` is exempt (review M5): it only forwards consented events, and without it the sign-in page's screen views failed and logged through the window.
  - A valid bypass cookie still gets everything, all of `/api/auth` included.
- **2026-09-30 · One profile write path; the name bound at sign-up (review I2, ruled).**
  - `/update-user` is in `disabledPaths`: it took any `name` and `image` (`z.record(z.any())`). `auth.api` calls are unaffected. The site's language switch now calls `account.updateProfile({ locale })`, like the app (one rpc call, when signed in, beside the cookie).
  - `profileNameSchema` (trimmed, 1..80) moved to `@smog/auth/fields` (a new client-safe subpath); `@smog/account/schema` uses it and still exports `PROFILE_NAME_MAX`.
  - `databaseHooks.user.create.before` (`newUserProfile`), by endpoint path: `/sign-up/email` takes `profileNameSchema` (`INVALID_NAME` otherwise); other client paths (email code, magic link) may leave the name empty (shown by email, as before) but never over 80; provider paths (`/callback/*`, `/sign-in/social`) keep the photo and cut the name to 80, so a long Google display name never blocks a sign-in. A client-set `image` on any non-provider path is refused (`INVALID_IMAGE`). A create with no endpoint (the seed, the phase 8 migration) is trusted. The name is trimmed everywhere.
  - The phase 5 admin plugin's `/admin/create-user` runs through the same hook (a non-provider path): an image there is refused, a name over 80 too.
- **2026-09-30 · The OpenAPI reference UI: pinned Scalar with SRI, and its own CSP (review I3, ruled).**
  - The plugin's default page loaded an unpinned `@scalar/api-reference` from jsDelivr and an inline script; the site CSP (enforced in dev, report-only in staging) blocked both.
  - Same-origin was preferred but the standalone bundle is 4.3 MB (not "small"), so `/api/openapi` renders our page (`server/openapi-reference.ts`, `renderDocsHtml`): the bundle pinned to 1.72.2 with its `sha384` SRI hash (computed from the npm tarball's `dist/browser/standalone.js`), and two same-origin scripts, `/api/openapi/prelude.js` (sets Zod's `jitless` global, so Scalar's bundled Zod does not probe `Function("")`, an `eval` violation) and `/api/openapi/reference.js` (mounts Scalar on `/api/openapi/spec.json`, with `telemetry: false` and `withDefaultFonts: false`). No inline script, so no nonce.
  - The page sends its own `Content-Security-Policy` (enforced in dev and staging): `script-src 'self' <that exact bundle URL>`, `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` (Scalar injects its styles), `connect-src 'self'`, `default-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`. The site-wide headers keep a route's CSP, as for `/turnstile-bridge`. Still dev and staging only.
  - Upgrading Scalar means changing the version and the hash together (the module says how). `csp.spec.ts` proves SRI refuses a stand-in with no CSP violation; with `SCALAR_STANDALONE_JS` pointing at the real file (this machine cannot reach jsDelivr), it renders the spec with no violation (run for this change).
- **2026-09-30 · Consent log and export limits (review M1, M9).** `setConsent` appends only a change: a repeat of the current decision (same answer, current policy) returns the current state. A change first spends `RL_AUTH` per user (`user:<id>:account.consent.set`, 5/60 s in staging and production), so a script cannot grow the log (and the export) without bound. `account.export` spends `RL_AUTH` per user (`user:<id>:account.export`).
- **2026-09-30 · The site adopts `user.locale` (review M2).** The shell resolves the language from the `locale` cookie, then, signed in without one, the account's language, then `Accept-Language`. The cookie stays this browser's choice.
- **2026-09-30 · Small site fixes (review M3, M4, M6).** `sitemap.xml` lists `/privacy` and `/terms`. `<head>` has `theme-color` (the chosen theme's page background, or both by media query for `system`; rendered in the document, since `head()` keeps one meta per name) and the layout a `<noscript>` note (`a11y.noscript`). One `escapeHtml` in `@smog/utils` (escapes `& < > " '`, also valid XML) replaces the copies in the maintenance page, the Turnstile bridge and the sitemap.
- **2026-09-30 · Site tests: one mail helper (review I4 c).** `apps/site/test/helpers.ts` has `waitForMail` (a 15 s deadline poll that throws with the mailbox contents) and `signedUp`. It reads the dev mailbox from KV (what `/dev/mail.json` shows), so it also works while maintenance answers `/dev/*` with a 503. The auth, csrf, maintenance and shell tests use it. `packages/auth/test/auth.test.ts` (h) no longer asserts an absolute 1500 ms for a whole sign-up (review I4 b); the relative native-scrypt check stays.

## Admin foundation (phase 5 task 1)

- **2026-09-30 · Ruling 5 (audit writes) as implemented.** `auditStatement(context.db, entry)` (`@smog/admin/server`) returns the Drizzle `audit_log` insert for the change's own `db.batch([...])`, so a change never lands unaudited and nothing is audited that did not happen. It validates `data` with `AUDIT_DATA_SCHEMAS[action]` when the statement is built and throws `AuditDataError` before the batch runs; Zod drops unknown keys, so the schema decides what is stored. `writeAudit(context.db, entry)` is the standalone form for changes outside D1 (Better Auth `auth.api`, the KV maintenance flag): the change happens first, and a failed insert is logged (`[admin] Failed to write the audit entry <action> for <type>:<id>`) and rethrown. [Fix round 1, I1] `legacy` is read-only: `admin.audit.list` parses migrated rows with its `{ legacy: unknown }` schema, but `WritableAuditAction` excludes it (`READ_ONLY_AUDIT_ACTIONS`), the writer refuses it at runtime, and a procedure kind cannot map to it (type and coverage test), since writing it would bypass the per-action schemas and their data minimisation. The writer's core (`buildAuditStatement` / `writeAuditWith`) takes the schema map as a parameter, so its tests use a test-only schema. `src/schema/audit.ts` has one empty block per later task (catalogue, users, maintenance). A stored row that no longer matches its schema is logged and returned as stored, never dropped.
- **2026-09-30 · Ruling 10 (gate responses) as implemented.** `getAdminGate()` (`apps/site/src/server/admin-gate.functions.ts`) runs in `/admin`'s `beforeLoad`, so on every admin navigation, SSR and client. It reads the request's session (Better Auth reads it from D1 on each request, joined with the user row), so a demotion shows on the next navigation: a guest gets `redirect({ to: "/sign-in", search: { redirect } })`, a signed-in non-admin `notFound()` (HTTP 404, the site's 404 page), an admin `{ user }` in the route context. The return path is the location asked for, kept only when `safeRedirect` accepts it and it is under `/admin`, else `/admin`. Every procedure is built from one `adminProcedure = implementRpc(adminContract).use(requireAdmin)`: the `@smog/rpc` guard reads the role from that same D1-read session, so a demoted admin's next call is `FORBIDDEN` with the same cookie (tested against real Better Auth sessions).
- **2026-09-30 · The admin is split per area, so parallel tasks never share lines.** `@smog/admin` has one file per area in each layer: `src/contract/<area>.ts` exports `<area>Slice` (spread into `adminContract`) and its `ADMIN_PROCEDURES`; `src/server/<area>.ts` exports `<area>Routes(deps)` (spread into `createAdminRouter`); `test/inputs/<area>.ts` gives each procedure a valid input for the auth test. All eight phase 5 areas (`dashboard`, `audit`, `gestures`, `categories`, `mux`, `users`, `maintenance`, `emails`) exist now, the later ones empty, so Tasks 2, 3, 5 and 6 edit only their own files plus their block in `src/schema/audit.ts` and their `admin.<area>` i18n block. [Fix round 1, I5] The area schema files (`src/schema/{catalog,users,maintenance}.ts`) and client hook files (`use-admin-{gestures,categories,users}.ts`, `use-mux-{upload,assets}.ts`, `use-maintenance.ts`, `use-email-preview.ts`) exist empty and are re-exported whole (`export *`) by the entries, so no task edits `index.ts`. Each task writes its DECISIONS entries under its own pre-created heading below. `bun.lock` conflicts are resolved by taking either side and re-running `bun install`, never by hand (as `routeTree.gen.ts`). Every area's routes take `AdminDeps`, used or not, so `router.ts` never changes. Phase 6 adds `sponsorships` and `export` with one import, one spread and one `ADMIN_SLICES` entry. The site routes follow the same rule: placeholder files for `admin/{gestures/index,gestures/$id,categories,users,settings,emails}` that the tasks replace. `gestures/$id` and `gestures/new` are placeholders beyond the brief's list, so the audit Sheet's gesture link and the dashboard's "New gesture" are typed routes.
- **2026-09-30 · [Fix round 1, I2] Procedure kinds, enforced by `adminProcedure`.** This replaces the first version's source scan (a regex over the test files for `expectAudit("<path>"`, which a comment or a skipped test satisfied, with a row-count check for reads that an UPDATE or a KV write passed). Now:
  - Each slice exports `ADMIN_PROCEDURES`, an exhaustive `Record<ProcedurePath<slice>, "read" | { audit: WritableAuditAction } | { exempt: string }>` (`as const satisfies AdminProcedures<typeof slice>`), so `check-types` fails on an unclassified or a stale path. `ADMIN_PROCEDURE_KINDS` merges them.
  - `adminProcedure = implementRpc(adminContract).use(requireAdmin).use(adminGuard(ADMIN_PROCEDURE_KINDS))`. The guard looks up the call's `path` (with or without the `admin` mount; an unknown path fails closed) and gives each call its own Drizzle client over the same D1 binding. A read gets a D1 that only prepares `SELECT` / `WITH … SELECT` and a KV whose `put`/`delete` throw, in production as in tests, and may build no audit entry. A `{ audit: A }` mutation that succeeded must have built at least one entry, all with action `A` (`[admin] <path> finished without its audit entry`, then `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`). An `{ exempt }` mutation may build none. "Built" is recorded by the writer against the request's client (`trackAudits`), so handlers must use `context.db`; a separate client fails the check (the safe way round).
  - `test/auth.test.ts` calls every procedure of the contract as an admin with an input from `test/inputs/<area>.ts` (functions over a small seeded fixture set: an admin, a gesture, a category, a user) and requires success, so every mutation's audit write is proved by a real call. `test/guard.test.ts` covers every way to break the rules on a test contract. `expectAudit` stays for the exact stored `data` and one row per target.
  - Limits: the guard checks that the entry was built, not that the batch holding it ran; a handler that builds the entry and then forgets to run the batch passes the guard, and its own `expectAudit` test is what catches it.
- **2026-09-30 · The admin auth matrix lives in `@smog/admin`.** `test/auth.test.ts` enumerates every procedure of `adminContract`: a guest is `UNAUTHORIZED`, a user `FORBIDDEN`, and the admin's call succeeds, with real Better Auth sessions over the test D1. oRPC's implementer runs the middlewares before input validation, so the guest and user calls need no input. `@smog/api`'s `authz.test.ts` classifies `admin.*` as the third class and checks every mounted admin path as a guest and a user with no input ([Fix round 1, M2]).
- **2026-09-30 · `@smog/db/enums`, a client-safe subpath.** `@smog/admin/schema` is bundled into the site's client, and `@smog/db`'s main entry pulls in the Drizzle tables. `./enums` exports `src/enums.ts` alone (it imports only `@smog/config/constants`), so the audit enums are defined once. The account schema's copied consent enums predate it and are left as they are.
- **2026-09-30 · `admin.audit.actors`, a read the brief did not list.** The audit page's actor filter is a Select of every admin and every account with audit entries (a demoted or former admin), by name, at most 100, with `truncated` when there are more ([Fix round 1, M4]: `user WHERE role = 'admin' OR EXISTS (audit_log by actor)`, the `EXISTS` a seek on `audit_log_actor_created_idx`, instead of a `DISTINCT` over the whole log). A deleted actor's rows have `actor_id` NULL and are reachable through the other filters.
- **2026-09-30 · `admin.dashboard` is one D1 batch.** The gesture, category and user counts and the five newest audit entries go in one `db.batch` (one round trip, under the brief's "at most 2"), tested with a D1 wrapper that counts round trips. A ban counts while `ban_expires` is NULL or in the future; "new users" are accounts created in the last 30 days. Phase 6 adds the sponsorship stats. [Fix round 1, M12] Known cost: the user counts are one full scan of `user` per dashboard load (fine at SMOG's size, a few thousand rows); if the table grows, cache the counts in KV for about 30 s.
- **2026-09-30 · Audit list keyset and time filters.** Newest first by `(created_at DESC, id DESC)`, written as a range on the first key (`created_at <= c AND (created_at < c OR id < i)`) so SQLite seeks the index, like the gestures list. [Fix round 1, I3] Migration `0005_audit_log_order` gives every filter an index that ends in `created_at`: `audit_log_type_created_idx (target_type, created_at)`, `audit_log_target_created_idx (target_type, target_id, created_at)` (replaces `audit_log_target_idx`) and `audit_log_actor_created_idx (actor_id, created_at)` (replaces `audit_log_actor_id_idx`; it still serves the `ON DELETE SET NULL` lookup). Before it, the type and actor filters sorted every matching row on every page. The query-plan test checks each UI filter shape (and combinations), with and without a cursor, for its index and for no `USE TEMP B-TREE FOR ORDER BY` (`FOR LAST TERM`, the id tie, is allowed). A `targetId` without `targetType` is `BAD_REQUEST` (M3: the index cannot be sought by the id alone). `from`/`to` are inclusive epoch milliseconds; the page's date inputs are Brussels calendar days (M9: `dayRange` in `@smog/i18n`, the zone `formatDate` shows; a DST day has 23 or 25 hours; a reversed range is swapped). Pages are "Older actions" / "Newest actions" with the cursor in the URL; keyset pages have no page numbers, so the kit `Pagination` is not used. While a new filter loads, the previous rows stay, dimmed and `aria-busy` (M8).
- **2026-09-30 · Admin queries are user-scoped and refetched after writes.** `@smog/admin/client` keys every query with `userScopedKey` (so `PurgeOtherUsers` drops them when another account signs in on the browser) and gives them a 30 s stale time. `useInvalidateAfterAdminWrite()` invalidates every `admin.*` and public `gestures.*` query; the area hooks call it on success.
- **2026-09-30 · The admin layout.** The site header stays; below it, a 240 px left rail (`md` and up) and a fluid main area (`AdminPage`: no `max-w-content`, 16/24/32 px gutters). Below `md` the rail is a left Sheet behind "Open the admin menu" (a `PanelLeft` icon, so it is not a second hamburger under the site's, M7), closed on navigation. The rail's active link is TanStack Router's `data-status="active"` (with `aria-current="page"`), styled with the primary-subtle tokens. The user menu shows "Admin" to admins only; the gate and the procedures are the real check. The audit Sheet's data is a focusable, labelled scroll region (M6), and its target links are typed routes (M5). `admin/gestures/new` is a placeholder too (M10), so the dashboard's "New gesture" link needs no change in Task 4.
