- **2026-09-30 · [Fix round 1, I4] The site's unit tests pin their env; e2e passes `SITE_URL` without `.dev.vars`.** `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` builds the Worker's bindings with wrangler, which merges a local `.dev.vars` (or, without one, `.env*`, and with `CLOUDFLARE_INCLUDE_PROCESS_ENV` the process env) over `env.dev.vars`; only keys in `miniflare.bindings` win. So a `SITE_URL` for another e2e port broke 7 site tests, and real secrets in `.dev.vars` would have reached them (the old "`.dev.vars` is not read in tests" comment was wrong). `apps/site/vitest.config.ts` now sets `CLOUDFLARE_LOAD_DEV_VARS_FROM_DOT_ENV=false`, unsets `CLOUDFLARE_INCLUDE_PROCESS_ENV`, and binds every var of `env.dev.vars` (`unstable_readConfig`) and every `workerSecretsSchema` key as `""` (off), then the test auth secret. `test/env-isolation.test.ts` asserts it; it failed with a poisoned `.dev.vars` under the old config and passes under the new one. For e2e on another port, Playwright's `webServer` passes `SMOG_DEV_SITE_URL=http://localhost:<E2E_PORT>`, and `vite.config.ts` (dev only) sets `SITE_URL` from it through the Cloudflare plugin's `config` customizer. A `SITE_URL` in `.dev.vars` still wins over it, so leave it out. Alternatives: `CLOUDFLARE_INCLUDE_PROCESS_ENV=true` for the dev server (it binds the whole process env) or a `.dev.vars.e2e` (wrangler picks `.dev.vars.<env>` by the environment name, which is `dev`).

## Phase 4 fix wave B: load-robust tests, the guest consent, legal additions (2026-09-30)

- **2026-09-30 · A guest never inherits a mirrored consent (review I7).** Signed in, the account's decision is mirrored to the device with `mirroredFrom = <user id>`, and the copy stays after sign-out. `useConsent` now reads that copy as undecided for a guest (`analytics: null`, `needsDecision: true`), so the next person on a shared device is asked and nothing is sent under someone else's yes. The guest's own choice (`set`) writes a plain device choice and drops `mirroredFrom`. The copy itself is kept, not cleared at sign-out: the same account signing in again matches it (no rewrite), and N1 (a copy is never carried to another account, `importGuestConsent` and the carry both skip it) is unchanged. The privacy text says so (LEGAL-SIGNOFF P31). Alternatives: clear the copy on sign-out (same effect for the guest, one more write path in the sign-out flows of both apps), or device-level consent like a cookie banner (the owner ruled it out).
- **2026-09-30 · Tests wait on conditions, not on the clock (review I4).**
  - Mobile: the harness requires every route module in a `beforeAll` (120 s hook timeout), so the cold load of expo-router and the routes is never charged to a test. RNTL's `asyncUtilTimeout` is 5 s (`jest.setup-after-env.ts`) and Jest's `testTimeout` 30 s: a screen test has several `findBy*` waits, and one run of the whole turbo suite timed a two-wait test out at 10 s. The query-persist poll has a 5 s deadline.
  - Hook suites (happy-dom, ui-web): Testing Library's `asyncUtilTimeout` is 5 s, and each `bun test` script passes `--timeout=20000`, so a test outlives its own `waitFor`. Per script, not in the shared root `bunfig.toml` (Bun documents the flag, not a bunfig key).
  - Server races use a deferred gate the test resolves (lists reorder rollback, the gestures search placeholder), as favorites already did. No `delay(100)`.
  - Seeds: `@smog/db/testing` `insertGestures(db, rows)` inserts any number of gestures in one D1 batch with `makeGesture`'s defaults (they share `gestureValues`). The account and lists helpers use it, so the 499-row limits tests make one round trip.
  - Workers-pool Vitest configs (api, auth, db, email, rpc, account, favorites, gestures, lists) set `testTimeout: 30_000`; the site's already had 60 s. Admin is left to phase 5.
  - `packages/auth` no longer asserts a whole sign-up under 1500 ms (logged only); the relative native-scrypt check proves the CPU budget.
  - `lists.test.ts` dates the lists back a minute instead of sleeping 5 ms for `updated_at` to differ.
- **2026-09-30 · Turbo runs at most 3 tasks at once (`turbo.json` `concurrency: "3"`).** On a 4-core machine ten parallel tasks (each Vitest spawning workerd isolates, Jest two workers) made a render take seconds. In `turbo.json` rather than the `test` script, so `bunx turbo run test` gets it too; it also caps `build` and `check-types`, which is fine at this size. It must stay above the number of persistent `dev` tasks (2: site and mobile). Alternative: `--concurrency=3` on the root script only (the review's suggestion), which a direct `turbo run` bypasses.
- **2026-09-30 · Playwright: 2 workers locally, 1 in CI, `expect` waits 15 s, and a warm-up.** `workers` is `E2E_WORKERS`, else 1 with `CI` and 2 without (local runs that set `CI=1` get 1). `expect.timeout` is 15 s, so the specs no longer pass `{ timeout: 15_000 }` to `toBeVisible` (account, legal links; the explicit poll timeouts stay). `e2e/global-setup.ts` opens `/`, `/gestures`, `/gestures/hond`, `/sign-in` and `/account` in Chromium once the dev server is up (Playwright starts `webServer` before global setup), so the route compiles and Vite's dependency optimisation, which follows the browser's module requests, land before the first test. A warm-up failure is logged, not fatal.
- **2026-09-30 · Legal additions (review I6).** The Google photo is described as fetched from Google by the browser each time it is shown, and "of Apple" is gone (Apple sends no photo; the app shows initials). The Google recipient bullet says Google then sees the IP address and browser details. The cookies bullet lists the admin maintenance cookie (`smog_mx`), the local storage bullet the shared-device rule. `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` is not bumped: what analytics measures did not change. Whether to copy the photo to R2 instead is open decision 4 in LEGAL-SIGNOFF; Turnstile in the app is for the store labels (P32, phase 8).

## Catalogue admin API (phase 5 task 2)

- **2026-09-30 · Ruling 8 (catalogue rules) as implemented.** `admin.gestures.*` and `admin.categories.*` (`packages/features/admin/src/{schema/catalog,contract/{gestures,categories},server/{gestures,categories,catalog-writes}}.ts`). Gesture names are 1..120 characters after trimming, descriptions ≤ 2000, keywords ≤ 30 of 1..60 characters de-duplicated by `normalizeText` equality (the first spelling and the order kept: `Café`, `cafe` → `Café`), 1..20 categories (unpublished ones allowed, unknown ones `VALIDATION`); category names 1..60. The limits are in the Zod schemas only (no DB CHECK; that would rebuild the tables). Slugs come from `slugify` at create (`gesture` / `category` when the name has no letters), get `-2`, `-3`, … on a collision (the taken ones read with a range on the unique index, `slug = base OR slug > 'base-' AND slug < 'base.'`), are retried up to three times when a concurrent write takes the same one (the unique index), and never change on rename. A duplicate gesture name is only `checkName`'s warning; a `normalizeText`-equal category name is `CONFLICT` `duplicateName` (compared in the Worker, since SQLite cannot strip accents; the categories are tens of rows).
- **2026-09-30 · One batch per write, with in-batch guards.** Every catalogue write is one `db.batch([guards…, changes…, gesture_fts rebuild…, audit…])`, then `deps.bumpCatalogVersion(kv)`, whose failure is logged (`[admin] Failed to bump the catalog version after …`) and swallowed (ruling 8: D1 is written; only the typo tier and the public categories snapshot stay stale). Every insert and rename sets `sort_name = gestureSortName(name)`. The first read decides the typed answer (`CONFLICT` `stale`, `INVALID_STATE`, …), but another admin can write between that read and the batch, so the batch repeats the check: `failWhen(db, name, condition)` is a `SELECT CASE WHEN <condition> THEN json_extract('{}', 'smog-guard:<name>') END` that fails the whole batch (a D1 batch is one transaction) with an error naming the guard; SQLite has no `RAISE()` outside triggers. `runCatalogBatch` turns it into `GuardFailedError`. Guards: `expectedUpdatedAt` still current (update, saveMany, category rename), the state not already set (setPublished), no gesture left without a category (bulkUpdate, checked after its changes), still unpublished and unsponsored (gesture delete), still unused (category delete), still the exact set (reorder). Alternatives: conditional `UPDATE … WHERE updated_at = ?` (the keyword, category and audit statements of the same batch would still run), or read-check-write without guards (a lost update under concurrent saves).
- **2026-09-30 · `CONFLICT` data and optimistic locking.** The catalogue procedures declare `CONFLICT` with `data: { reason: "stale" | "duplicateName" | "published" | "sponsored" | "inUse", ids? }` (`CATALOG_ERRORS`, overriding the shared map's data-less `CONFLICT` per procedure). `stale` carries the ids that changed since they were read, and nothing is written; `saveMany` (≤ 50 rows) is all or nothing. A write's new `updated_at` is `max(now, expectedUpdatedAt + 1)`, so two saves in one millisecond still conflict. A category reorder leaves `updated_at` alone (the order is not what a rename dialog holds). The hooks surface `stale` as `staleIds` (`useAdminGestureMutations`, `useAdminCategoryMutations`) and refetch the admin queries, so the table editor can show the other admin's values.
- **2026-09-30 · `setPublished` has a one-of audit kind; a no-op is `INVALID_STATE`.** A procedure kind can name several actions (`{ audit: ["gesture.publish", "gesture.unpublish"] }`): `AuditWrite` in `contract/audit-map.ts`, the guard (each built entry must be one of them), the coverage check and `expectAudit` (`action` required for such a kind) accept it. Task 6's `maintenance.set` can use it too. Publishing a published gesture (or category) is `INVALID_STATE` with no write and no entry, since ruling 5 audits only what happened; the UI refetches. Alternatives: separate `publish` / `unpublish` procedures (the brief names `setPublished`), or auditing the no-op.
- **2026-09-30 · Set actions write one entry with `target_id` NULL.** `gesture.bulk_update` (`{ ids, patch }`, at most 100 ids) and `category.reorder` (`{ ids }`) are one entry each with `target_type` `gesture` / `category` and `target_id` NULL, so the audit list's target-type filter finds them. Every other catalogue action writes one entry per target: `saveMany` writes one `gesture.update` per row. `gesture.update` data is `{ name, slug, fields, previousName? }` (the fields the patch set); `gesture.delete` and `category.delete` keep `{ name, slug }` (the row is gone).
- **2026-09-30 · FTS set forms in `@smog/db`.** `rebuildGesturesFtsSql(ids)` and `rebuildCategoryGesturesFtsSql(categoryId)` share one `rebuild(ftsRows, gestureRows)` with `rebuildGestureFtsSql`, so the three cannot drift; ids are inlined (drizzle 0.45's D1 `batch()` cannot bind raw statements) after the id pattern check. A category rename, publish or unpublish rebuilds every gesture of the category in its batch (the `categories` column holds published names only); publishing a gesture rebuilds its row too, for uniformity (FTS indexes unpublished gestures; search filters on `published_at`). A category delete is refused while any gesture has it, and a guard repeats that in the batch, so no gesture ever loses the category by the delete's cascade; the "read the ids, reindex after the delete" step of the brief is therefore never needed and is not written.
- **2026-09-30 · The admin list and migration `0006_admin_gesture_order`.** `admin.gestures.list` reads every gesture (published or not) in `sort_name, id` order with the seekable keyset predicate of the public list, on the new full index `gesture_sort_name_idx (sort_name, id)` (the partial `gesture_published_sort_name_idx` only covers published rows); a query-plan test asserts `SCAN … USING INDEX gesture_sort_name_idx` without a cursor and `SEARCH … (sort_name>?)` with one, and no sort of the outer query. The migration is one `CREATE INDEX`, safe on existing data. `q` is a `normalizeText` substring of `sort_name` (`instr`) or of a keyword; keywords are stored as typed and SQLite cannot strip accents, so a `q` query first reads `gesture_keyword` (gesture id and keyword, a few thousand short rows at SMOG's size) and matches in the Worker, then passes the matching ids as one `json_each` parameter. If the catalogue grows far beyond that, add a normalised keyword column. `counts` apply the `q` and `category` filters but not `status` (they are the status tabs' numbers). `category` filters on any of the given ids, published or not. The categories list reads D1 (`sort_order`, name), at most 100, with `gestureCount` and `publishedGestureCount`, and `updatedAt` for the rename's `expectedUpdatedAt`.
- **2026-09-30 · Correlated subqueries name both sides.** Drizzle renders columns unqualified in a single-table statement, so a subquery column such as `"id"` binds to the inner table (`json_each` has an `id` column too, and `sponsorship."gesture_id" = "id"` compared the sponsorship with itself). The admin queries use `ref(alias, column)` for every correlated reference, as `@smog/gestures` does; a test caught the sponsorship case.
- **2026-09-30 · Gesture delete (ruling 8).** `confirmName` must equal the stored name (trimmed; else `VALIDATION`). A published gesture is `CONFLICT` `published`, one with any sponsorship `CONFLICT` `sponsored` (the FK is RESTRICT; the records are the sponsor's). Favorites, list items, keywords and category links cascade; the FTS row is deleted in the same batch.

- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, I-1] D1's 100 bound parameters per statement.** D1 refuses a statement with more than 100 bound parameters, also inside a batch, so no admin statement binds a list one parameter per item: `inList(column, values)` (`catalog-writes.ts`) is `column IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?))` with the list as one JSON parameter, used for every id list (bulk update and its category removal, the row and category lookups, the leftover-category check). The bulk update with 100 ids had 102 parameters before. The bounded multi-row inserts stay as they are (30 keywords × 3 = 90, 20 categories × 2 = 40). `test/limits.test.ts` runs every catalogue procedure at its contract maximums (100 ids, 20 categories added and removed, a 50-row save with 30 keywords and 20 categories each, 100 categories reordered) over a D1 binding that records each statement's bound parameters, and fails above 100; miniflare refuses more too, but only on the paths a test runs.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-1] A one-of kind writes one action per call.** The guard fails a call of a one-of kind that built entries with two different actions of its set. Which action matches the input (publish for `published: true`) is enforced by the procedures' tests (`expectAudit` with `action`, both directions), not by the guard: a mapping function per procedure would duplicate the handler.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-2] The guard marker is SQLite's error text.** A fired `failWhen` is recognised by `smog-guard:<name>` inside SQLite's "bad JSON path" message as D1 relays it. There is no structured channel out of a failed batch (no `RAISE` outside triggers, and a CHECK-constraint table would need a migration only for this), so a change in D1's error text would turn the typed `CONFLICT` / `INVALID_STATE` into `INTERNAL`, still atomic. Workerd verifies it; a phase 8 staging smoke carry (PROGRESS) checks a stale save on real D1.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-5] At most 100 categories.** `categories.list` and the `reorder` input stop at 100, so `categories.create` refuses a 101st (`INVALID_STATE`, checked before and by an in-batch guard), and reorder always gets the exact set. SMOG has tens.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-6] `ref(alias, column)` lives in `@smog/db`.** The gestures queries, the lists service and the admin catalogue import it from `@smog/db` (`src/sql.ts`) instead of each defining it.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-4] Filtered admin list plans.** The plan test pins each filter shape: `published` uses the partial index and `unpublished` / `all` / `q` the full one, all in index order with no sort. A category filter reads its members by primary key and sorts them (a category is a small part of the catalogue), which the planner prefers and the test records.

## Mux admin integration (phase 5 task 3)

- **2026-10-02 · `@smog/video` is a thin `fetch` client, not `@mux/mux-node`.** The admin needs four Mux calls (create upload, get upload, get asset, list assets) and the webhook HMAC. `src/client.ts` is a typed `fetch` client (basic auth, `{ data }` parsed with Zod, `MuxApiError` on a non-2xx, 404 as `null`), and verification is Web Crypto. Alternatives: `@mux/mux-node` 15.5 (generated, fetch-based, would likely run on workerd, but it is a large dependency for four endpoints, its webhook helper and shims would need their own workerd check, and the fake would have to imitate it). Phase 7 extends the client (master access, the render upload). The main entry is Worker only; `@smog/video/schema` holds the states for client code, and `@smog/site`'s build fails if `MUX_TOKEN`, `MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET` or `@mux/mux-node` appears in `dist/client` (`scripts/deploy-guard.ts --bundle`, run by the site's `build` script and by the deploy).
- **2026-10-02 · Ruling 4 as implemented.** `createDirectUpload` sends `cors_origin` (the `SITE_URL` origin), `new_asset_settings: { passthrough, playback_policies: ["public"], static_renditions: [{ resolution: "highest" }] }`, `timeout: 3600` and, in dev only, top-level `test: true`. `admin.mux.createUpload` is `{ exempt }` in `ADMIN_PROCEDURES`. The server refuses an upload URL that is not https on a `*.mux.com` host (`isAllowedUploadUrl`), unless `MUX_API_URL` points at a fake and the URL is on its origin. Mux moved direct-upload URLs from storage.googleapis.com to `*.mux.com` subdomains in 2025 (Mux changelog), so the existing `connect-src https://*.mux.com` covers the PUT and no host was added (`test/csp-mux.test.ts`).
- **2026-10-02 · Webhook verification.** `verifyMuxWebhook(rawBody, headers, secret, now)` parses `t=…,v1=…` (several `v1` accepted, for secret rotation), refuses a timestamp more than 300 s from now in either direction, and compares with `crypto.subtle.verify("HMAC", …)` (constant time, every candidate checked); the body is parsed only after the signature matched. Failure reasons (`missing`, `malformed`, `expired`, `mismatch`, `invalid-event`, `no-secret`) are logged as `[video] Rejected a Mux webhook: <reason>`, never with the body.
- **2026-10-02 · The webhook is rate-limited, against the brief (only rejected deliveries; fix round 1, M5).** The plan's brief says "not rate-limited"; the dispatch asked for a sensible limit. A delivery that fails (a body over 1 MiB, a missing, malformed, wrong or expired signature) counts against `RL_API` per IP (300/60 s outside dev) and answers 429 past it; a verified event is never counted, so genuine Mux traffic is never throttled as render events grow in phase 7. The body is read (capped at 1 MiB) before the limit is consulted, which costs at most one 1 MiB HMAC per forged request.
- **2026-10-02 · Idempotency and ordering.** The handler marks each event id in KV (`mux:event:<id>`, 24 h) after applying it and skips a seen id. The upload record (`mux:upload:<uploadId>`, 24 h) is merged, not overwritten: a ready or errored asset never goes back to preparing when `video.upload.asset_created` arrives late. The upload id comes from the upload event's `data.id` or the asset's `upload_id`; events without one, other passthroughs and other types are a 200 and ignored. A missing `MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is a 503 (Mux retries), so a missing secret shows in the logs and Mux keeps the events. KV is eventually consistent across locations, so a duplicate delivered within seconds to another colo may be applied twice; the merge makes that harmless.
- **2026-10-02 · `uploadStatus`: KV first, API while not final.** A final KV record (upload failed, or asset ready/errored) answers without calling Mux; otherwise the Mux API decides (the webhook may not be configured, as on staging), falling back to the KV record when Mux is unreachable. An upload whose passthrough is not `gesture-upload:` is `NOT_FOUND`, so an id from another flow reveals nothing.
- **2026-10-02 · The Mux fake.** `@smog/video/testing` `createFakeMux()` is an in-memory implementation of the Mux REST endpoints the client calls plus the browser's PUT (with the upload's CORS), as a `fetch`; it records every API request, so the ruling 4 body is asserted on what the client sent. The admin tests inject it through `AdminDeps.muxFetch` (unset in production). `@smog/video/testing/server` serves the same handler with Bun for the e2e: it readies an uploaded asset after 1.5 s (with the dev seed's public sample playback id) and POSTs signed webhooks to the dev server. Playwright starts it (`E2E_MUX_PORT`, 4010) and passes its URL and token to the dev server as dev-only vars (`SMOG_DEV_MUX_*`, vite.config.ts); the dev CSP adds the fake's origin to `connect-src` (`devConnectSources`, dev only, never for the real API).
- **2026-10-02 · `MUX_*` env.** `MUX_TOKEN_ID`, `MUX_TOKEN_SECRET` and `MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET` are optional secrets everywhere (staging has none yet: the admin shows "video uploads not configured" and offers the playback id only), and `MUX_API_URL` a var defaulting to `https://api.mux.com`. Phase 8 makes them required in production (PROGRESS carry). The site tests pin `MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (the only secret besides the auth one) so the webhook route is tested end to end.
- **2026-10-02 · The upload UI.** `MuxUpload` is a small state machine (`createMuxUploadController` in `@smog/admin/client`, wrapped by `useMuxUpload`): creating → uploading (one XHR PUT with progress; no UpChunk, clips are short) → processing (polls every 2 s; a failed poll is retried) → ready, or failed with a reason (`create`, `transfer`, `processing`, `cancelled`, `slow` after 10 minutes, whose retry resumes polling the same upload). `reset()` aborts the PUT and drops late answers. The upload tab stays mounted while another tab is open, so switching tabs never drops a running upload. `VideoField` (`{ value: { playbackId, muxAssetId? } | null; onChange }`) previews the chosen video and offers Upload / Choose existing / Playback id; the pasted id is validated with the catalogue's `playbackIdSchema`. Until Task 4's editor uses it, `/admin/dev/video-field` (dev and staging only, compiled out of production like `/dev/ui`) hosts it for the e2e and the screenshots.
- **2026-10-02 · `muxStreamUrl` / `muxThumbnailUrl` stay in `@smog/utils`.** Both UI kits and the gesture route import them from there (the brief moves them only if nothing else does).

- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, I1] The deploy guard covers every dev-only page.** `checkDevTools` takes the list of dev-only pages with a marker each: the `/dev/ui` gallery (`data-theme-column`) and the `/admin/dev/video-field` preview (`data-dev-preview`). A production build must contain none, a staging build each.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, I2] Upload progress is announced in milestones.** One `role="status"` region, mounted for the whole upload flow and never inside an `aria-busy` subtree, says the phase (preparing, uploading, processing, ready) and while uploading only the last 25 % milestone passed (25/50/75/100). The visible line under the ProgressBar shows every percent with `aria-live="off"`; the bar keeps `aria-valuenow`. Failures are announced by their `role="alert"`.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, I3] Only a ready asset can be chosen.** The picker still lists preparing and errored assets (with their status badge), but their "Choose" button is disabled and described by "Can be chosen once Mux has finished it.", so a gesture never points at a video that will not play. Task 4's save should still refuse a `muxAssetId` that is not ready.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M1, M3, M7] Webhook and client hardening.** The HMAC covers the raw bytes received (`<t>.` + body bytes, no decode and re-encode); the body is decoded with a strict UTF-8 decoder after the signature matched. A verified event that is not about a gesture upload touches no KV at all (no dedupe marker: it changes nothing). A 2xx body that is not JSON is a `MuxApiError`, and `listAssets` skips (with a `[video]` warning) an asset it cannot read, such as a new status, instead of failing the page.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M4] `MUX_API_URL` is the real API outside dev.** The env schema refuses another value unless `ENVIRONMENT` is `dev`, `createMux` ignores it outside dev (defence in depth: the Basic token only goes to api.mux.com), and `isAllowedUploadUrl` accepts a fake's origin only with `allowFake`, which the admin passes in dev only.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M8, M10, M13, M14] Small admin choices.** `uploadStatus` answers for any `gesture-upload:` upload in the Mux environment, not only the caller's: it is admin-only, read-only, and `createUpload` stores nothing to bind it to (ruling 4). A Mux 429 is `RATE_LIMITED` (not `INTERNAL`) on every procedure; no per-user limit on `createUpload` was added, since an unused upload costs nothing and `RL_API` already bounds it. Polling stops at once on an answer a retry cannot change (`NOT_FOUND`, `INVALID_STATE`, `UNAUTHORIZED`, `FORBIDDEN`, `BAD_REQUEST`) and keeps retrying network and server errors until the 10-minute deadline. A failed `admin.mux.status` shows an ErrorState with a retry (not "not configured"), with the playback id still usable.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M9, M11] Picker and ready state.** The upload tab's ready state shows only the success line and "Upload another"; the field's preview plays the video once. The picker pages with previous / next and "Page N" (Mux reports no total), and shows its EmptyState only when page 1 is empty and nothing follows.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M12] How code passes `t` around (Tasks 4–6 follow this).** `@smog/i18n` exports `Translate` (`ReturnType<typeof createI18n>["t"]`, defined once in `setup-web.ts`) and `TranslationKey`. Code that takes `t` as a parameter uses `Translate`; never `ReturnType<typeof useTranslation>["t"]`, which resolves the generic hook at its constraints and makes TypeScript instantiate the whole key union (TS2589 once the catalogue is large), and never a local copy. `packages/i18n/src/translate-type.test.ts` scans `apps/` and `packages/` and fails on either. Keys chosen from an enum come from a map `{ … } as const satisfies Record<Enum, TranslationKey>` (`ACTION_LABEL_KEYS` in `audit-data.tsx`, `STATUS_LABEL_KEYS` in `mux-picker.tsx`), never a template literal cast to one literal key. Later options, not done: an `admin` namespace loaded only by the admin routes, and i18next's selector API.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M2, M15] Notes.** The KV upload record is a read-modify-write; two events of one upload at once can leave `preparing` over `ready`, which is harmless because a non-final record makes `uploadStatus` ask the API (comment in `webhook-handler.ts`). The spec's §8.2 line now points here instead of `@mux/mux-node` `webhooks.unwrap`.

## Catalogue admin screens (phase 5 task 4)

- **2026-10-02 · `/admin/gestures` pages hold 100 rows.** The list asks `admin.gestures.list` for `ADMIN_GESTURE_PAGE_MAX` (100) rows per keyset page, which is also `BULK_UPDATE_MAX`, so "select every gesture on this page" is always one valid `bulkUpdate`. The filters in the URL are `q` (debounced 300 ms), `status` (`published` · `unpublished`, absent = all), `category` (one id) and `cursor`; the counts above the table come from the page's `counts` (the filters' totals, whatever the status). The selection is kept per page and cleared on any filter or page change. The name links to the editor; rows are not clickable as a whole (they hold a checkbox, a switch and a menu). Alternative: 50 per page with a size selector (a select-all would then need a cross-page selection, which the contract cannot take).
- **2026-10-02 · `AdminGestureRow` carries the description.** The table editor edits the description inline (A-20), so the list rows include it (at most 2000 characters, so a 100-row page stays small). `AdminGestureDetail` is the row plus `createdAt`. Alternative: fetch every buffered row's detail before editing (one call per row).
- **2026-10-02 · The table editor buffers against the row as first read.** `table-edits.ts` keeps, per gesture, the row as it was when its first cell changed and the changed fields; a value set back to the original (trimmed; categories as a set) drops the field, and a row without changes leaves the buffer. Refetches never move that base, so its `updatedAt` is what the one `saveMany` sends. Invalid cells (the contract's own field schemas) are marked with their reason (`aria-describedby`) and block the save; more than `SAVE_MANY_MAX` (50) changed rows also block it with a message (one call, all or nothing). The name and description cells have `maxLength` (120, 2000) and a counter once changed; the description is a textarea, so its line breaks survive. A typed playback id sends `muxAssetId: null` too (the old asset is no longer this gesture's). Categories and keywords open a small dialog with the same picker and keyword input as the editor. Escape restores a cell. [Fix round 1] The buffer lives in `GestureList`, not in the editor, so a refetch or an empty page never drops it; while it holds edits the filters and the pager are disabled, and a navigation or reload asks first (TanStack Router `useBlocker`, `LeaveGuard`). "Discard (N)" asks first for more than one row. Focus returns to the editor's heading after a save, a discard or a merge. Publishing stays immediate and outside the buffer (the list's switch and menu), so edit mode has no published column (the old `AdminTable` had one; M8).
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, C1] A stale save is a three-way merge; nothing is overwritten silently.** This replaces the first version's "Keep editing", which kept the whole form and so wrote the other admin's changed fields back with their old values (the review's probe: B changed the description and keywords, A's next save reverted them). Now, on `CONFLICT` `stale`, the editor refetches the gesture and compares, per field (`mergeDrafts` in `gesture-draft.ts`; the video, name, description, keywords, categories and the published state), the version the form was based on, the form, and theirs: a field only they changed is taken from theirs; one only I changed stays mine; one we both changed to different values is a conflict. Without conflicts the merge is saved at once against their `updatedAt` and a toast says so ("Merged with a newer version and saved"); so a field they unpublished is never republished. With conflicts a dialog shows those fields with their version and mine side by side and when theirs was saved: "Use their version" (the focused default) takes theirs for those fields and keeps my other edits unsaved; "Overwrite with mine" asks a second time, naming the fields ("This replaces the other admin's name with yours"), then saves; Cancel or Escape changes nothing, so the next save meets the conflict again. The table editor follows the same rule (`rebaseEdits`, `resolveConflicts`): stale rows are rebased on their newer version, my fields stay buffered, their other fields stay theirs, and rows where both changed a field are highlighted under a banner with the same two choices. Who saved is not shown: `gesture` has no `updated_by`, and the audit log would be a second read for every conflict. Alternatives: field-by-field choices in the dialog (more clicks for the common one-field case), or always reloading (loses work).
- **2026-10-02 · One `SortableList`, and one QR dialog.** The lists page's dnd-kit reorder (pointer and keyboard sensors, announcements, optimistic order with rollback and a toast) moved to `apps/site/src/components/sortable-list.tsx`, generic over `{ id, name }` items with a `renderRow` that gets the drag handle and `moveUp` / `moveDown` (null at the ends). Each caller passes its announcement keys as a key map (`REORDER_KEYS[event]`), not template-literal keys. The categories page has a published and a hidden section, each a `SortableList`; a move saves the whole order (published, then hidden) with one `reorder`. The gesture QR dialog moved from `learning/gesture-actions.tsx` to `components/gesture-qr-dialog.tsx` (`GestureQrDialog`, `qrFileName`, `gesturePageUrl`); the public detail menu, the admin row menu and the editor open it. Both stay in `apps/site` (not `@smog/ui-web`): they need the router, the site URL and the toast wiring.
- **2026-10-02 · The `/admin/dev/video-field` preview is gone.** The gesture editor mounts `VideoField`, so the preview route and `video-field-preview.tsx` are deleted and the deploy guard no longer requires (or refuses) the `data-dev-preview` marker; `/dev/ui` is again the only dev-only page. The video e2e now drives `/admin/gestures/new` and checks that a created gesture keeps the uploaded asset id.
- **2026-10-02 · Kit and test additions.** `DataTable` takes `selectedRowIds` (rows get `data-state="selected"`), `rowProps(row)` (a class and `data-*` attributes, for the stale highlight) and a column `className` (header and cells; the list pins its last column, the published switch and the row menu, to the right so a phone keeps them in view). `useDebouncedValue` is exported from `@smog/gestures/client` (the site's search already used it) for the name check and the list search. The site's component tests can answer a procedure with `rpcError(code, status, data)` (`src/test/render.tsx`), and `@testing-library/user-event` 14.6.7 (newest) is a site devDependency for the Radix menus. [Fix round 1, M10] The e2e answers `stream.mux.com` with an empty valid HLS playlist (`stubMuxStream`), so the player's error dialog never opens and axe runs on the whole page, the player included; the first version's `mux-player` exclusion is gone.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, I6] The editor's save is two calls that rebase after each step.** `admin.gestures.update` cannot also publish: the audit guard requires a one-of kind to write one action per call, and `update` plus `publish` would be two (task 6's `{ audit, noop }` is about no-op calls, not several actions). So the editor keeps `update` for the fields, then `setPublished`, and sets its base to each step's answer as soon as that step succeeds. A failed publish after a saved rename leaves only the publish to do; the next save never conflicts with this admin's own first step (tested).
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, I7] E2e fixtures go through the running Worker.** `bunx wrangler d1 execute --local` started a second workerd on the SQLite file the dev server holds open and failed with `SQLITE_BUSY` while other specs wrote. `POST /dev/e2e-seed` (`src/server/e2e-seed.ts`) takes a fixed list of operations with bound values (set a role, a `legacy` audit entry, a legacy id), never SQL; it is same-origin only, refused outside `ENVIRONMENT=dev`, and compiled into dev builds only (`__SMOG_E2E_SEED__`, unlike `/dev/ui`, staging does not get it). The deploy guard fails a staging or production build that contains its marker (`checkNoE2eSeed`, tested; staging and production builds checked by hand: no marker). The catalogue spec runs in its own Playwright project after the public specs (`catalog`, one worker; the maintenance project now follows it), because its bulk smoke publishes 100 gestures for a moment. It touches only rows it creates: every name starts with a per-run prefix, its gestures sit in its own hidden categories (deleted after each test), and the keyboard reorder moves two hidden categories of its own, so the public order never changes. The reorder test is back to the default 60 s.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1] Smaller review fixes.** The list no longer sorts its 100-row page on the client (M6): the order is the server's (`sort_name`), and each page is a URL with its cursor, so Back returns to the previous page. A hidden gesture's QR dialog says the link only works once it is published (M7). Removing a keyword moves the focus to the next keyword or the input (M9). The categories' "In use: unpublish instead" is an enabled item that explains itself, so keyboard users reach it (M2); a menu move is announced like a drop, through `SortableList`'s own live region (M3, also on `/lists`). Key maps are typed `Record<…, TranslationKey>` (M4), and no copy is built by concatenation: `admin.categories.hiddenName`, `countWithPublished`, `a11y.dragHandleFor`, and lists through `formatList` (`Intl.ListFormat`, M5). One `setPublished` mutation serves every row of the list (M11). On a phone the bulk bar is one "Actions" menu. A changed table cell has the primary border, a dot and "changed" for screen readers, distinct from the focus ring.

## Users and roles (phase 5 task 5)

- **2026-10-02 · Ruling 6 (Better Auth's admin HTTP endpoints off) as implemented.** `ADMIN_DISABLED_PATHS` (`packages/auth/src/server.ts`) lists all 15 `/admin/*` endpoints of the admin plugin (set-role, ban/unban, remove, create/update/get/list user, list/revoke sessions, set-user-password, impersonate, stop-impersonating, has-permission) and goes into `disabledPaths`. `packages/auth/test/admin-paths.test.ts` enumerates the configured instance's `/admin/*` endpoints and requires the list to equal them (a new endpoint in a Better Auth update fails it), asserts each answers 404 over HTTP even with an admin cookie, and that `auth.api` still sets roles, bans, unbans, lists and removes. No impersonation anywhere; `user.impersonate` stays unused.
- **2026-10-02 · Rulings 5 and 7 for users, as implemented.** `admin.users.setRole`, `ban`, `unban` and `delete` read the target from D1, run `userActionRefusal` (a pure function: `self` → `unchanged` → `lastAdmin` → `adminTarget` → `alreadyBanned` / `notBanned`; `INVALID_STATE` with `data.reason`, declared per procedure as `USERS_ERRORS`), then call `auth.api.setRole` / `banUser` / `unbanUser` / `removeUser` with the request's headers (Better Auth checks the session's admin role again), then `writeAudit`. A failed audit write after the change is logged (`[admin] Failed to write the audit entry user.role_change for user:<id>`) and rethrown, so the admin sees an error although the change happened (tested with a D1 that refuses the `audit_log` insert). A no-op (the role it has, a second ban, an unban without a ban) is `INVALID_STATE`, as `setPublished` in Task 2. Audit data: `role_change { from, to }`, `ban { reason, expiresAt }` (the reason is the admin's own text), `unban {}`, `delete { hadSessions }`; never an email or a name.
  - [Fix round 1, I-1] The last-admin rule is atomic: migration `0007_user_keep_one_admin` adds a `BEFORE UPDATE OF role ON user` trigger that aborts with `last_admin` when an admin would be demoted and no other admin without a ban in force remains. The earlier text here was wrong: `auth.api.setRole` is a D1 `UPDATE user SET role` through the same adapter, so it can be guarded in D1. D1 runs one write at a time and the trigger runs inside the statement, so two admins demoting each other at once cannot both win; the trigger also covers `admin:grant` and raw SQL. `setRole` keeps its read guard as the fast path (it now counts only admins in good standing, other than the target) and answers the trigger's abort as `INVALID_STATE lastAdmin`. Tested: a `Promise.all` of two mutual demotions (exactly one wins; the loser is `INVALID_STATE lastAdmin`, or `FORBIDDEN` when Better Auth's own role check sees the winner's write first; one admin and one audit row remain), raw demotions, and a banned admin not counting. The migration creates a trigger only, so it is safe on existing data; checked against migrations 0000-0006 plus the dev seed (one admin) with SQLite, and on the local D1. `account.delete` refuses the last admin in good standing with `INVALID_STATE` (the client shows `account.errors.lastAdmin`) in app code, not with a delete trigger (that would block test cleanups that `DELETE FROM user`).
  - The delete needs `confirmEmail` equal to the email, case-insensitively and trimmed (`VALIDATION` otherwise), as the gesture delete's `confirmName`.
- **2026-10-02 · "Banned" means a ban in force.** `banned = 1 AND (ban_expires IS NULL OR ban_expires > now)`, as the dashboard counts it. The list's `banned` filter, `AdminUser.banned` and the unban guard use it; an expired ban reads as not banned (its reason and end are not shown), and Better Auth clears the flag at the next sign-in. A ban with no `expiresInDays` has no end. [Fix round 1, M-3] A banned account cannot be promoted (`INVALID_STATE targetBanned`; the panel disables "Make admin" and says to lift the ban first), and `admin:grant` lifts any ban when it grants the role (its `--dry-run` says so), so it stays the recovery path.
- **2026-10-02 · The users search is `instr`, not `LIKE` (deviation from the brief).** D1 limits `LIKE` patterns to 50 bytes (`LIKE or GLOB pattern too complex`), so a search on a whole email (the e2e accounts are 51 characters) failed with a 500. `instr(lower(email), q) > 0 OR instr(lower(name), q) > 0` with `q` lowercased is literal (`%` and `_` need no escaping), case-insensitive for ASCII as `LIKE` was, and has no length limit; the admin gesture list uses `instr` too. A test searches a 60-character email. The list stays a seek on `user_created_at_idx` with a `(created_at, id)` keyset (no new migration: the ties are sorted on the right part of the order, and the table is small). [Fix round 1, M-1] D1's `lower()` folds ASCII only (no ICU), so the name is also matched as typed: `Émile` finds `Émile`, but `émile` and `ÉMILE` do not (a test documents this). Real Unicode case folding needs a normalised search column kept by the user create and update hooks (`normalizeText`, as `gesture.sort_name`), a migration with a backfill; left for when it is asked for. The search is a scan that stops after `limit + 1` matches: fine at thousands of accounts; a normalised column plus FTS if it reaches six figures.
- **2026-10-02 · No admin create-user.** The brief has no create action, and adding one would need a `user.create` audit action (a CHECK change on `audit_log`, so a table rebuild migration). `/admin/create-user` is disabled over HTTP; `auth.api.createUser` still runs `databaseHooks.user.create.before` (a test proves a photo and a name over 80 are refused there), so a later task that adds it inherits the rule and can show `INVALID_NAME` / `INVALID_IMAGE`.
- **2026-10-02 · Helpers take `Translate` from `@smog/i18n`, with key maps (TS2589).** Once the catalogue grew by the users block, a helper typed with `ReturnType<typeof useTranslation>["t"]` failed type checking with TS2589 ("excessively deep"). As Task 3 rules (its DECISIONS entry), the one definition is `Translate = ReturnType<typeof createI18n>["t"]` in `@smog/i18n` (`setup-web.ts`), and `translate-type.test.ts` forbids local copies. The users screens pick keys from `as const satisfies Record<…, TranslationKey>` maps (roles, refusal reasons, sign-in methods), never template-literal keys. `setup-web.ts`, `index.ts`, the test, `head.ts`, `audit-data.tsx` and `email/src/templates/types.ts` are byte-identical to Task 3's, so the two merge cleanly.
- **2026-10-02 · `/admin/users` screens.** A DataTable (name and email, role, status, joined) with the search (debounced 300 ms, replacing the history entry), role and status in the URL, keyset pages as on the audit page, and `?user=<id>` opening the side panel (the audit log's link). The panel shows the methods and counts and the actions, each behind an AlertDialog: promote/demote, ban (reason with a counter, a length of 1, 7, 30, 90 or 365 days or none), lift the ban, delete (type the email; confirm stays disabled until it matches). The admin's own account has every action disabled with the reason; an admin target has ban and delete disabled until demoted. The server's refusal reasons are translated (`admin.users.refused.*`) in a toast; every action refetches the admin queries when it settles. Long emails are cut on narrow screens (the panel shows them whole) and the table scrolls sideways on a phone.

- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, I-2] Better Auth's refusals are typed.** `authCall` maps a failed `auth.api` admin call: the `last_admin` abort to `INVALID_STATE lastAdmin`, a 403 `APIError` (the actor lost the role meanwhile) to `FORBIDDEN`, a 404 (the target is gone) to `NOT_FOUND`, a 400 `YOU_CANNOT_*` to `INVALID_STATE self`, and another 400 to `BAD_REQUEST`; anything else stays a 500. The failure is logged first either way.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-4] The ban's entry comes from Better Auth's answer.** `user.ban`'s `expiresAt` is taken from `banUser`'s returned user, not a re-read, so the entry is written even when the account is deleted right after. Better Auth's ban is two writes (the ban, then the sessions); if the second fails, the call fails without an entry while the ban stands (the session survives until it expires, since `getSession` does not check `banned`). Accepted: rare, and a retry of the ban is refused as `alreadyBanned`, so the admin sees it.
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-5] Deleting an account drops its ban reasons from the audit log.** The ban reason is an admin's free text and may name people. `admin.users.delete` writes its `user.delete` entry and, in the same D1 batch, replaces `reason` with `reasonRemoved: true` in that account's `user.ban` entries (both or neither; the `user.ban` schema accepts either shape). This is the one change to existing audit rows, made for data minimisation (ruling 7). The end date and who banned stay. Alternatives: keep the reason (it outlives the account) or a length only (says nothing useful).
- **2026-10-02 · [Fix round 1, M-6, M-7, M-8] Users screen accessibility and phone layout.** The reason an action is off (own account, an admin target, a banned account) is one note tied to the affected buttons with `aria-describedby`. Long emails wrap anywhere in the panel and dialog titles. Below `md` the name cell shows a "Banned" badge, since the status column is scrolled out of view.

## Maintenance settings and email previews (phase 5 task 6)

- **2026-10-02 · Ruling 9 as implemented: one maintenance definition.** [Fix round 1, M-4: moved from `@smog/admin/schema` to `@smog/config/maintenance`.] `packages/config/src/maintenance.ts` holds `MAINTENANCE_KV_KEY`, `MAINTENANCE_MESSAGE_MAX`, `maintenanceSettingSchema` (`{ bypassVersion: int, enabled, message?, until? }`), `MAINTENANCE_OFF`, `parseMaintenanceSetting` (missing or malformed is `null`, read as off), `nextBypassVersion` and `BYPASS_VERSION_INITIAL`. It is a runtime-wide setting that the gate, the CLI and the admin all read, so a feature package was the wrong home; `@smog/admin/schema` re-exports it, which keeps the ruling's import path. The site's gate (`apps/site/src/worker/maintenance.ts`) and `bun run maintenance` import `@smog/config/maintenance` (the root already devDepends on `@smog/config`, so the round's root `@smog/admin` devDependency is gone); the gate's own parser and the script's version rule are gone. The cookie signing stays in the gate (`signBypassCookie` / `verifyBypassCookie`): only the Worker signs and checks cookies.
- **2026-10-02 · The first window keeps version 0.** `POST /api/maintenance/bypass` signs with `bypassVersion ?? 0` when nothing is stored, but the phase 4 script started the first window at the current second, so a cookie fetched before the very first window did not work in it. `nextBypassVersion(true, null)` is now `BYPASS_VERSION_INITIAL` (0), what the endpoint signs with; turning off still starts a new version above the old one (at least the current second). Tested in the admin package and end to end in `apps/site/test/admin-maintenance.test.ts`. [Fix round 1, M-5] It only validates v0 cookies issued while nothing was stored (admin-issued, at most 12 h old); every `off` writes `max(now, current + 1)`, never 0, so no revoked cookie reopens. Deleting the key does reset the version to 0 and revives v0 cookies from the last 12 h: turn maintenance off instead of deleting the key (noted at `BYPASS_VERSION_INITIAL`).
- **2026-10-02 · `admin.maintenance.set`: the change, then the entry (ruling 5).** It validates (`message` trimmed 1..280; `until` an ISO date in the future and at most 7 days ahead, checked when the input is parsed; `message` and `until` only with `enabled: true`), reads the stored setting, writes KV, then `writeAudit` (`maintenance.enable` or `maintenance.disable`, `target_type` `setting`, `target_id` `maintenance`, data `{ message, until }` with `null` for none: the new window's for an enable, the ended window's for a disable). A failed entry is logged, the value read before the change is put back (a delete when there was none; a failed restore is logged too), and the error rethrown (fix round 1, I-2: otherwise the toast said "not changed" over a change that stood unaudited, and the retry was a no-op that never audited it). A restored `off` brings back the old version's cookies, which is right, since the disable did not happen. Tested: the key removed again, a stored window put back exactly, the retry audited, and a failed restore logged. A changed note or end while on is a new `maintenance.enable` (the version stays).
- **2026-10-02 · A no-op mutation may skip its entry (`markUnchanged`), strictly.** The brief says setting the state maintenance already has is a no-op without an audit row, but `adminProcedure`'s guard failed any `{ audit }` mutation that built no entry. [Fix round 1, I-1, the strict form] A kind opts in with `{ audit, noop: "<reason>" }` (`maintenance.set` is the only one; the coverage test wants a reason). In mutation mode the guard now gives the handler a D1 and a KV that record every write (a statement that is not a `SELECT` or `WITH … SELECT` when it is prepared, `exec`, `withSession`, `kv.put`, `kv.delete`). `markUnchanged(db)` (now in `guard.ts`) fails the call on a kind without `noop` or when anything was written already, and then makes the rest of the call read-only, so a later write throws before it reaches D1 or KV; after the handler the guard checks again that nothing was built or written. Reads use the same gate, read-only from the start. Tested: a write after the mark (KV put, D1 update, an audit insert) is refused and nothing changed, a mark after a KV put fails, and a mark on a kind without `noop` fails. Alternatives: audit the no-op with `{ unchanged: true }` (breaks the brief's "no row") or `INVALID_STATE` as the users and catalogue no-ops (two admins pressing "Disable" at once must both succeed).
- **2026-10-02 · Enabling from the UI: the cookie first, in the same action.** "Enable" calls `POST /api/maintenance/bypass` (same-origin `fetch`, so the HttpOnly cookie lands in this browser) and only when it succeeds calls `admin.maintenance.set({ enabled: true })`; a refused cookie leaves maintenance off and says so. The set response itself sets no cookie: the rpc handler has no response-header plumbing, and the bypass endpoint already owns the signing, the rate limit and the CSRF rule. The page also has the bypass card ("Bypass for this browser (12 h)", with the expiry from `getMaintenanceBypassStatus`, a server function that checks the request's `smog_mx` cookie with the gate's verifier past the isolate cache, since the cookie is HttpOnly; [fix round 1, M-3] for an admin session only, and a malformed cookie costs no KV read). `/api/maintenance/bypass` was already exempt from the 503 (phase 4). [Fix round 1, I-4] The bypass endpoint answers the version it signed (`{ expiresAt, bypassVersion }`). After `set` answers, the card compares it with the stored `bypassVersion`; on a mismatch (a stale KV read: a change made elsewhere moments before) it asks for a cookie once more, and if the versions still differ it shows a warning ("your bypass cookie is for an older window; press the bypass button in a minute") instead of success. `/sign-in` and the bypass endpoint stay open during a window, so the admin can always recover. [M-8] A 429 from the endpoint says "too many attempts" instead of "sign in again".
- **2026-10-02 · Propagation: a note, not a write-through, and no "fresh" reads.** [Fix round 1, I-4, M-1: the wording was wrong.] Workers KV is eventually consistent: a write is usually read back at once where it was made, a read with `cacheTtl` may be that many seconds old at its location, and other locations see a write within about a minute. There is no uncached read. `admin.maintenance.get` reads past any isolate cache with `cacheTtl: 30` (KV's shortest; the default is 60), and the gate's cookie checks skip its 30 s isolate cache (`skipIsolateCache`) but not KV's. So a change reaches every visitor within about 2 minutes (isolate cache, edge cache, propagation); the settings page, the contract, the CLI and AGENTS.md now say "up to about 2 minutes". A write-through is not possible from the admin service (the cache lives in the gate's module, and other isolates and locations would still lag). The e2e polls for the guest's 503 and 200.
- **2026-10-02 · Email previews.** `@smog/email/samples` exports `EMAIL_SAMPLES` (one prop set per template, typed as a complete mapped type over `EmailTemplateProps`, so `check-types` fails for a template without a sample) and `EMAIL_TEMPLATE_IDS` (its keys). It is pure data with no renderer, so the admin contract validates `template` with `z.enum(EMAIL_TEMPLATE_IDS)` (an unknown id is `VALIDATION` over HTTP) and the site validates its URL with it. Samples use the RFC 2606 host `smog.example`, the code `482913` and the name "Alex"; no real address (a test fails on `@` or another host). `admin.emails.list` renders each template's subject in nl/en/fr; `admin.emails.preview` renders one with `renderEmail`. Both are reads; nothing is sent. The site shows the HTML in `<iframe sandbox="" srcdoc>` (no scripts, forms, popups or navigation) and puts a strict CSP (`default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src data:; …`) first in the document's head, on top of the page CSP a `srcdoc` document inherits; the plain text is React text in a `<pre>`. Template names come from a key map typed `Record<EmailTemplateId, TranslationKey>`, so phase 6's templates need a label too.
- **2026-10-02 · The maintenance e2e is isolated.** [Fix round 1, I-3] `e2e/admin-settings.spec.ts` is its own Playwright project, `maintenance`, with `dependencies: ["chromium"]` and `workers: 1`, so it runs after every other spec and nothing else runs while the site is in maintenance (alone: `bunx playwright test --project maintenance --no-deps`). The email previews never touch the flag and moved to `e2e/admin-emails.spec.ts` in the normal project. Every window is closed in `finally` and the file's `afterAll` (turn off, then wait until a cookieless visitor gets 200), and `globalSetup` turns maintenance off before a run, so an aborted run cannot poison the next one. The shared helpers (`e2e/maintenance.ts`) use no `expect`, so `globalSetup` can use them. One caveat stays: `reuseExistingServer` can attach to another checkout's server on the same port; use `E2E_PORT` when several checkouts run e2e.
- **2026-10-02 · A demoted admin keeps the gate bypass until it expires.** [Fix round 1, M-6] The gate checks a cookie's signature and version, never the session (phase 4), so an admin demoted during a window keeps the public site for up to 12 h; admin pages and procedures refuse them at once. To revoke it, turn maintenance off and on again (a new version). Not worth a D1 read per request.
- **2026-10-02 · Email preview details.** [Fix round 1, M-2, M-7] The comment now names the real walls: the empty `sandbox` blocks scripts, forms, popups and top navigation, but a frame can navigate itself, so the preview also gets `<base target="_blank">` (a click becomes a popup, which the sandbox blocks), and the page CSP's `frame-src` would stop a link loading. The template list is 14rem wide, so the 780 px frame fits at 1280; the frame is 40rem tall; without a width in the URL a phone-sized screen starts at the 390 px width.

## Admin hardening and parity (phase 5 task 7)

- **2026-10-02 · The kit returns the focus to a dialog's opener.** Radix Dialog and AlertDialog focus their `Trigger` on close and prevent FocusScope's own return. Every admin dialog and sheet is controlled (opened from a button, a table row or a menu item, with no `Trigger`), so closing one left the focus on `<body>`: the keyboard runs found it for the category form, the table editor's review, the audit entry sheet and the users panel. `useReturnFocus` (`@smog/ui-web` `lib/return-focus.ts`) remembers `document.activeElement` when the content mounts (before Radix moves the focus in) and refocuses it on close while it is still in the document; otherwise Radix's behaviour stands (a menu item that unmounted: the menu has already focused its trigger). `DialogContent`, `SheetContent` and `AlertDialog` use it, so the fix holds for the site and every later screen; a caller's own `onCloseAutoFocus` runs first and may `preventDefault()`. Alternative: a `Trigger` in each screen, which a row or a menu item cannot be.
- **2026-10-02 · Admin a11y and screenshot specs.** `e2e/admin-a11y.spec.ts` runs axe (`serious`/`critical` only, as the other specs) on every admin screen and its overlays (the audit sheet, the row menu's QR dialog, the bulk bar, the table editor, the editor's picker, the category form, the users panel, the email text view, the phone rail sheet) in light and dark at 390 and 1280 px, and keyboard-only runs of the rail, the row sheets, the QR dialog, the category form and a table editor save, each checking focus return. The category reorder's keyboard run stays in `admin-catalog.spec.ts`. `e2e/admin-screenshots.spec.ts` replaces the per-task screenshot blocks of the shell, catalogue, users, video and emails specs (one harness in `e2e/admin.ts`: the review browser, a themed context per theme and width, `ADMIN_PAGES`); the settings spec keeps its maintenance-on shots on the same harness, in its own project. `csp.spec.ts` adds the admin pages, a direct upload to a `*.mux.com` URL (routed, and counted, so the PUT provably passed `connect-src`) and the email preview frame. On the emails screen axe skips the sandboxed preview frame (the template's own document; with no scripts allowed, axe could hang entering it), while the frame's scroller is still checked.
- **2026-10-02 · The email preview's scroller takes the focus.** At 390 px the 390/780 px frame scrolls sideways inside its box, which axe flags (`scrollable-region-focusable`): it is a labelled `<section tabIndex={0}>`, as the audit entry's data box.
- **2026-10-02 · Parity: the admin gesture search matches names and keywords, not descriptions.** The old library filtered name, description ("info") and concepts on the client. `admin.gestures.list` `q` matches a `normalizeText` substring of the name or a keyword (phase 5 task 2); matching descriptions too would read every description (up to 2000 characters each) per debounced keystroke, since SQLite cannot strip accents. The admin looks gestures up by name; the public search (FTS5) still ranks descriptions. Revisit with a normalised description column if admins ask.
- **2026-10-02 · Parity: the table editor offers hidden categories, marked.** The old `CategoriesCell` offered active categories only. The category picker (editor and table editor) lists every category in catalogue order with hidden ones marked (an eye-off icon and "(hidden)"), so an admin can file gestures in a category before publishing it (hidden category names are not indexed or shown publicly).
- **2026-10-02 · No `updated_by` column yet.** The gesture conflict dialog (phase 5 task 4) says when the other admin saved, not who: the row has no `updated_by`. Not worth a migration now. Who saved is already in `audit_log` (the newest `gesture.update` / `gesture.publish` / `gesture.unpublish` entry for the target, with its actor), so if admins ask, the editor can read that entry on a `CONFLICT` (one indexed read, `audit_log_target_created_idx`) instead of adding a column that every write would have to keep. A column would also be fine (`ALTER TABLE gesture ADD COLUMN updated_by TEXT REFERENCES user(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` needs no rebuild), but it duplicates the log.
- **2026-10-02 · The boundaries check covers the root package.** `scripts/check-boundaries.ts` now checks the root package (`smog`: `scripts/**` and its top-level files) next to the workspaces, under `ROOT_PACKAGE` in `@smog/config/boundaries`: scripts may import `@smog/config` and the features' `./schema` subpaths, and must declare what they import. `__fixtures__` stays skipped; the fixture roots are named `smog`. This closes the phase 5 task 6 carry.
- **2026-10-02 · The admin test helpers are no longer a knip entry.** Every area uses `test/helpers.ts` now, so knip reports an unused helper export again; `adminRouter` and `mappedActions` became module-private (task 1 review, M11).

## Phase 5 fix wave

- **2026-10-02 · The guard counts the audit inserts D1 ran (review M1).** Until now a `{ audit }` mutation passed when it had *built* an entry with `auditStatement`, even if that statement never ran. The guard's D1 proxy now hands out wrapped statements. When a statement's SQL is an `INSERT INTO audit_log` (quoted or not), the wrapper counts it once its `run`, `all`, `raw` or `first` resolves. A `batch` gets D1's own statements back (workerd's `batch` wants its own objects) and counts the audit inserts in it once the batch resolves. After the handler, `{ audit }` needs the run count to equal the number of entries built: fewer means an entry was left out of the batch that ran, and more means an `audit_log` insert skipped the writer and its schema. `{ exempt }` needs a count of 0. Tested in `guard.test.ts` with an entry that was built but not run, a raw extra insert, and an exempt kind that runs a raw insert. Alternative: a branded statement whose execution flips a flag (review option b). That would miss raw inserts, and Drizzle's batch path would need its own hook.
- **2026-10-02 · A read procedure gets Better Auth's read methods only (review M2).** Better Auth writes D1 through its own adapter, which the D1 gate never sees, and a clean wrap would mean rebuilding Better Auth on the gated D1 per call (the instance is memoised per isolate). So, in read mode, the guard puts a proxy in `context.auth`. Its `api` answers `AUTH_READ_METHODS` (`getSession`, `getUser`, `listSessions`, `listUserAccounts`, `listUserSessions`, `listUsers`, `userHasPermission`). Any other method throws `AdminGuardError` when called, before it runs. Reading anything else on `auth` (`handler`, `$context`, `options`) throws too. `getSession` may still refresh the caller's own session row, as on every request; that is session housekeeping, not admin state. Tested: `banUser` from a read is refused and the account stays unbanned, `auth.handler` is refused, `getSession` works, and every allowlisted name is a function on the real instance. No admin read uses `auth` today.
- **2026-10-02 · Account self-deletion's last-admin check stays a read, and the race is accepted (review M3).** `account.delete` checks `isLastAdmin` and then calls Better Auth's `deleteUser`. Better Auth runs its own deletes (sessions, accounts, then the user row) through its adapter, so our code cannot put a conditional `DELETE … WHERE EXISTS (another unbanned admin)` in the same batch. Making it atomic would need one of these:
  - a `BEFORE DELETE` trigger on `user`, which 0007's header rules out because it blocks the test cleanups that `DELETE FROM user`;
  - our own delete path in place of Better Auth's, which would lose its password and fresh-session checks.
  
  Two admins who are the only ones in good standing and delete their own accounts at the same moment can both pass the check and leave no admin. Recovery: `bun run admin:grant --env <env> <email>`, which also lifts a ban. The window is a few milliseconds, and it needs the last two admins to delete themselves together.
- **2026-10-02 · `inList` and `jsonList` live in `@smog/db`, and biome refuses `inArray` (review M6).** The D1-safe list idiom moved from `@smog/admin` to `packages/db/src/sql.ts`, next to `ref`: any list is one `json_each(?)` parameter, so D1's 100-parameter limit never applies. It is used by the admin slices, `gestures` (`inCategories`, `gesturesByIdsQuery`), `account` (the import's published check) and `lists` (`touchListsWithNewItemsStmt`). The `json_each(...)` table sources in `favorites` and `lists` use `jsonList` too. The guard is a biome override on `packages/**/src/**` and `apps/site/src/**`: `noRestrictedImports` forbids `inArray` and `notInArray` from `drizzle-orm`, with a message that points to the helpers. Tests may still use them (lists and favorites test helpers do). The one subquery use (`itemRowsQuery`) is now `` sql`${listItem.listId} IN ${subquery}` ``, which renders the same SQL. A lint rule covers more than a grep test would (aliases, multi-line imports), and it shows in the editor. A spread `IN (${sql.join(...)})` over values is not caught; the helper's doc comment and the review checklist cover it.

## Infrastructure: payments, jobs, bindings and provisioning (phase 6 task 1)

- **2026-10-02 · `@smog/payments` is a thin `fetch` client, no `@mollie/api-client` [dep].** Four calls (create, get, cancel; refunds are read from the payment's `amountRefunded`) plus `Idempotency-Key`, which SDK 4.x cannot send; the `@smog/video` precedent. `createMollie(env)` is `null` without `MOLLIE_API_KEY` and talks only to `https://api.mollie.com` outside dev. Every answer is parsed by Zod into `MolliePayment` with integer cents (`mollieValueToCents`, `^\d+\.\d{2}$`, never a float; a test scans the package for `parseFloat(`/`Number(`). Errors are one class, `MollieApiError { status, retryable }`: 5xx, 429 (`MollieRateLimitError`) and network failures (`status` 0) are retryable, so the webhook can answer 503 for exactly those; 4xx and an unparseable payment are not. A create without `_links.checkout.href` is an error. `MolliePayment` also carries `dashboardUrl` (`_links.dashboard.href`) when Mollie sends it, for the admin's link (ruling 4). The fake (`./testing`) answers like Mollie (HAL errors, 422 on an uncancelable cancel, the same payment for a repeated `Idempotency-Key`) and records each request; `./testing/server` adds the hosted checkout page, whose buttons await the form webhook before the 303 to `redirectUrl`, so the e2e is deterministic.
- **2026-10-02 · The env refines of rulings 2, 10 and 12.** `MOLLIE_API_KEY` must match `^(test|live)_\w{20,}$`; production takes `live_` only, dev and staging `test_` only. `MOLLIE_API_URL` (default `https://api.mollie.com`) may differ in dev only. The R2 tokens come as a pair and then need `R2_ACCOUNT_ID` and `MEDIA_BUCKET` too. `MEDIA_BUCKET` and `R2_ACCOUNT_ID` are optional vars (`""` is unset): `wrangler.jsonc` names the bucket in every env, and production gets `"R2_ACCOUNT_ID": ""` and `"TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY": ""` placeholders so `REQUIRED_WORKER_CONFIG.production.vars` exist in the file (empty is what production already had; phase 8 checks the values). `RENDER_MODE` is set per env: `fake` in dev and staging, `container` in production.
- **2026-10-02 · `failWhen`, `GuardFailedError` and `toGuardFailure` live in `@smog/db` (`src/sql.ts`).** Moved from `@smog/admin`'s `catalog-writes.ts` with the behaviour unchanged; `toGuardFailure(error)` replaces the private `guardName`, and the message prefix is now `[db]`. `Statement` (a D1 batch item) moved with them. The admin's `runCatalogBatch` and its tests stay; `inList`/`jsonList` were already there (phase 5 fix wave).
- **2026-10-02 · The D1 retention purges (`packages/db/src/retention.ts`).** Five chunked deletes (`rowid IN (SELECT rowid … LIMIT 500)`, at most 20 chunks each per run): `audit_log` older than 3 × 365 days (strictly older: an entry exactly 3 years old stays), expired `session` and `verification`, and `sponsorship_token` expired, or used, more than 30 days ago. Each chunk read seeks an index, except the used-token one: `used_at` has no index and migration 0008 adds only the two the brief names; the table holds a few rows per sponsorship, so the scan stays small. A failing purge is logged and the others still run; the error is rethrown at the end. Task 5 calls `runRetentionPurges` from `runRetentionPurge`.
- **2026-10-02 · Migration 0008 adds no CHECK on `refunded_cents`.** A table-level `check()` makes drizzle-kit rebuild `payment`, which ruling 4 rules out; the column is only ever written from `mollieValueToCents`, which is never negative.
- **2026-10-02 · Queues, DLQs and crons in `wrangler.jsonc`, in every env (dev too).** `EMAIL_QUEUE` → `smog-<env>-email` (`max_retries` 5) and `EVENTS_QUEUE` → `smog-<env>-sponsorship-events` (`max_retries` 10), both `max_batch_size` 10, `retry_delay` 30 and `dead_letter_queue` `<queue>-dlq` (no consumer); `MEDIA` → `smog-<env>-media`; `triggers.crons` the four `CRON` schedules. `queue()` dispatches by the queue name's suffix (`worker/queues.ts`), so one Worker serves every env; a DLQ or unknown queue is logged and acked. `scheduled()` dispatches on `controller.cron` against `CRON`; an unknown schedule is logged. The consumers and cron handlers are stubs that ack or log until tasks 2, 4 and 5. `max_batch_timeout` is task 2's (the email consumer).
- **2026-10-02 · `CRON` is the `@smog/jobs/cron` subpath, and the root may import it.** `release-config-check` compares `triggers.crons` with it and `bun run cron <name>` reads it, so the root package's boundaries entry gains `@smog/jobs/cron` (the only subpath; `cron.ts` is pure data). `bun run cron` calls `<dev server>/cdn-cgi/local/scheduled?cron=…`, the path `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` 1.62 serves (it rewrites the older `/cdn-cgi/handler/*` and `/cdn-cgi/mf/scheduled`), and refuses a non-local URL.
- **2026-10-02 · The producers swallow by default; the webhook and the outbox throw.** `enqueueEmail`/`enqueueEvent` validate (an invalid or over-128 KB message always throws: a bug), retry 3 times at 100/400/1600 ms, then log `[jobs] Failed to enqueue <template|type>` and return `null`/`false`, as ruling 8 says for admin actions and crons. `{ onFailure: "throw" }` rethrows, for the Mollie webhook's 503. `QueueEmailOutbox` throws by default, so Better Auth fails the request and the user can ask for the code again rather than wait for an email that was never queued; `{ onFailure: "log" }` swallows. `pendingRenderStarter(mode)` is a factory, since its log names the mode.
- **2026-10-02 · `scripts/ensure-cloudflare-resources.ts` looks resources up by name** (amended by fix round 1 below: `r2 bucket list` replaces `r2 bucket info`, and a CORS drift now fails). It reads `env.<env>` of `wrangler.jsonc` (producers, consumers, DLQs, `r2_buckets`, `SITE_URL` for the CORS origin) and asks `wrangler queues info <name>` (the API filters by name; parsing `queues list` would mean a paginated box table), `wrangler r2 bucket info <name> --json` and `wrangler r2 bucket cors list <name>`. "Missing" is only the documented not-found answers (`Queue "…" does not exist`, R2 code 10006, "There is no CORS configuration" or 10059); any other lookup failure stops the run before anything is created. `--create` runs `queues create`, `r2 bucket create … --no-update-config` (wrangler must not offer to edit the config) and `r2 bucket cors set … --force` with `{ rules: [{ allowed: { origins: [SITE_URL origin], methods: ["PUT"], headers: ["content-type"] }, maxAgeSeconds: 3600 }] }`, the last only on a bucket that has no CORS. An existing CORS that does not allow the PUT is reported, never overwritten; nothing is ever deleted or updated (a test checks every command's verbs). `--check` creates nothing and exits 1 with the exact commands; `--dry-run` calls nothing. Production `--create` needs `SMOG_PROVISION_PRODUCTION=1` in the script too, on top of `deploy.yml` choosing `--check` unless `vars.SMOG_PROVISION_PRODUCTION` is `1`. The deploy job runs it before the D1 migrations; `release-config-check` asserts the step, its order, both modes and the variable, the naming, the DLQs, the consumer settings, the bucket, `MEDIA_BUCKET`, `RENDER_MODE`, the crons, and `REQUIRED_WORKER_CONFIG` against the schemas, `.dev.vars.example` (secrets, commented `# KEY=` lines count) and each env's vars.
- **2026-10-02 · The eight transactional templates are registered as stubs with their final props.** Ids `transactional/<kebab-name>` (the `auth/*` pattern), copy under `email.transactional.<camelName>.{subject,body}` and labels under `admin.emails.template.<camelName>`. The props are fixed now so tasks 3–5 can build email plans against them; dates are ISO 8601 strings (queue messages are JSON) and amounts integer cents. Task 2 designs the bodies and may add `email.common.*` keys. The samples test now allows addresses on `smog.example` only (the admin email's contact and invoice address) instead of no `@` at all. `@smog/email` gains `EmailOutbox` and `OutboxEmail` (a union over the template ids) as types only; `DirectEmailOutbox` is task 2's.
- **2026-10-02 · The deploy guard's browser markers.** `dist/client` must not contain `MOLLIE_API_KEY`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `aws4fetch` or `AWS4-HMAC-SHA256` (the presigner's algorithm string, which survives minification) besides the Mux ones; `checkClientHasNoMuxSecrets` is now `checkClientHasNoSecrets`.
- **2026-10-02 · `@smog/render/contract`.** `renderInputSchema` v1 is a plain `z.object`, so a v1 reader ignores the optional fields phase 7 may add; `RENDER_OVERLAY_LAYOUT` holds the fractions of ruling 7.7 and the spec's timing (the last 5 s, a 1 s fade, a 30 px slide), with the colour from `@smog/styles` tokens.

- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I1] The account and token prerequisites.** The resources step needs the Workers Paid plan (Queues), R2 enabled on the account, and a token with Account › Workers R2 Storage: Edit (also needed by `wrangler deploy` once `r2_buckets` is bound) and Account › Workers Scripts: Edit or Queues: Edit (List/Create Queue accept either). A refused lookup or create appends a `[provision]` hint: `[code: 10042]`/"enable R2" → enable R2; "Workers Paid"/"paid plan" → the plan; `[code: 10000]`/`10001`/"Authentication error"/`(403)`/"permission" → the token permissions. Recorded in AGENTS.md and PROGRESS "Owner actions".
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I2, M4, M10] A CORS drift fails, and is still never overwritten.** `r2 bucket cors list` is parsed into rules (`allowed_origins`, `allowed_methods`, `allowed_headers` lines); a bucket whose rules do not allow `PUT` with `content-type` from the `SITE_URL` origin is `cors-mismatch:<bucket>`, which fails both `--check` and `--create` (exit 1) and prints the exact `wrangler r2 bucket cors set … --file cors.json --force` command with the JSON. An empty `SITE_URL` with a bucket planned refuses, and production `--create` refuses while `SITE_URL` is `https://smog-site-production.workers.dev` (`PRODUCTION_SITE_URL_PLACEHOLDER`). Alternative: overwrite on drift (rejected: the script never changes what exists).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, M1–M3] Exact lookups, without the analytics query.** A queue exists only on the exact line `Queue Name: <name>` and is missing only on `Queue "<name>" does not exist`; anything else stops the run. Buckets come from one `r2 bucket list` (the `name:` lines, matched exactly) instead of `r2 bucket info`, which also runs a GraphQL analytics query needing another permission. If the list misses a bucket that exists (the API may page), `r2 bucket create` answers `[code: 10004]` and the bucket is kept as existing and its CORS looked up; the CORS "none" answer is the exact wrangler line or `[code: 10059]`. The log prefix is now `[provision]`.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, M5] Bucket location.** Staging is created with the account default. A bucket's location and jurisdiction cannot change after creation, so production waits for the owner's choice between `--location weur` and `--jurisdiction eu` (the latter changes the S3 endpoint to `<account>.eu.r2.cloudflarestorage.com` and needs `"jurisdiction": "eu"` on the `r2_buckets` binding); PROGRESS "Owner actions" lists it, and production provisioning is gated anyway (`SMOG_PROVISION_PRODUCTION`, the `SITE_URL` placeholder).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, M6, M7] Queue message limits.** `idempotencyKey` is at most 480 characters (`EMAIL_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_MAX`), so `email:sent:<key>` stays under KV's 512-byte key limit. The producers send with `contentType: "json"`, so the 128 KB guard (JSON in UTF-8) measures exactly what the queue stores; v8 serialisation would count two-byte strings differently.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, M8] A producer merges with or after its consumer (a rule, not a guard).** The task 1 consumers ack and drop. A runtime guard would have to know per message whether its consumer is real, which is more code than the risk; the rule (PROGRESS) is that nothing enqueues on `EMAIL_QUEUE` before task 2's consumer and nothing on `EVENTS_QUEUE` before task 4's.

## Emails and the email queue (phase 6 task 2)

- **2026-10-03 · Ruling 8 as implemented: one path from an outbox email to a sender.** `@smog/email` `deliverEmail(sender, { email, env })` renders an `OutboxEmail` in its locale and sends it with `From`/`Reply-To` from env (`EmailDeliveryEnv`: `EMAIL_FROM`, `EMAIL_REPLY_TO`, `SITE_URL`); the email consumer and `DirectEmailOutbox` both call it, and `sendEmail()` (`src/send.ts`, the phase 2 seam) is gone. `renderEmail` wraps every failure in `EmailRenderError`. Better Auth takes `createAuth({ outbox })`; the site passes `QueueEmailOutbox(EMAIL_QUEUE)`, or `DirectEmailOutbox` with a `[site]` warning if the binding is missing (every env binds it). `@smog/auth`'s env no longer reads `EMAIL_FROM`/`EMAIL_REPLY_TO`: the consumer adds them. `QueueProducer.send` returns `Promise<unknown>`, since the Workers `Queue.send` now answers a `QueueSendResponse`.
- **2026-10-03 · Every auth email goes through the queue, the code too; `max_batch_timeout: 0`.** [Fix round 1, I1, M5: corrected.] Verify, code, magic link and reset are queue messages without an idempotency key (each code is new). One path was kept for codes too: the hand-off is a single `Queue.send` of a few milliseconds, a transient Email Service error is retried (auth emails from 5 s, see below) instead of being lost, and two paths would mean two sets of logs and failure modes. The email consumer does not wait to fill a batch: `max_batch_timeout: 0` on every env's email consumer (wrangler 4.143 passes 0 through as `max_wait_time_ms: 0` and Miniflare accepts 0..60; `release-config-check` holds it), since messages are handled one at a time anyway. Cloudflare gives no latency guarantee; expect about 1–3 s from the request to the Email Service, more under a backlog. The first version said a synchronous send "never reported a failure to the user anyway": wrong, since the magic link and `/send-verification-email` await their send; see the next entry for how every endpoint now reports a failed hand-off.
