- **2026-10-03 · The queue keeps each env's sending exactly as it was.** The consumer sends through `createEmailSender` with the env's own bindings and vars, as `getAuth` did: dev → `DevEmailSender` (the KV mailbox at `/dev/mail`, nothing leaves the machine), staging → the `EMAIL` binding from `SMOG & Co <info@smog.zias.be>` (`allowed_sender_addresses: ["info@smog.zias.be"]`), production → the `EMAIL` binding from `noreply@smog.vlaanderen`. No `wrangler.jsonc` sender, var or binding changed. Recipients: the auth emails go to the same addresses as before. The welcome email is the one new email, and it only ever goes to an address that has just proven it is reachable by following our verification link or code (or a provider vouching for it), so staging mails nobody it had not mailed already. The transactional templates have no producer yet: tasks 4 and 5 add them, with the rule that admin emails go to verified admins only.
- **2026-10-03 · The consumer's decisions (`processEmailMessage` in `@smog/jobs`).** [Fix round 1, I2, I3, M3, M4: amended.] One message at a time. An invalid envelope (schema) is logged `[email] Dropped an invalid message <id>: <paths>` and acked. An auth email (`auth/*`) whose `props.minutes` have passed since the message was enqueued (`message.timestamp`) is acked as expired: its code or link no longer works, and a newer one may already have been sent. A key with a KV marker `email:sent:<key>` is acked as a duplicate. A message that cannot be rendered (`EmailRenderError`: a producer bug, or props from an older version during a gradual deployment) is retried like a failed send, so after the retries it lands in `smog-<env>-email-dlq` with its body, kept 4 days and replayable (the first version acked it, losing the email). A send the Email Service refuses for good (`isPermanentSendError`: `E_RECIPIENT_SUPPRESSED`, `E_RECIPIENT_NOT_ALLOWED`, `E_VALIDATION_ERROR`, `E_FIELD_MISSING`, `E_CONTENT_TOO_LARGE`, `E_TOO_MANY_*`, `E_HEADER_*`) is acked; a sender or domain error (`E_SENDER_NOT_VERIFIED`, `E_SENDER_DOMAIN_NOT_AVAILABLE`) is retried, so the emails wait in the DLQ until the configuration is fixed; anything else (rate limits, `E_DELIVERY_FAILED`, internal errors, a KV read failure) is retried. Delays: `min(5 × 2^(n − 1), 60)` s for auth emails, `min(30 × 2^(n − 1), 3600)` s for the others. With `max_retries` 5 a transactional message reaches the DLQ about 15.5 min after its first try (30 + 60 + 120 + 240 + 480 s), an auth message after about 2 min (the earlier "2 h" was wrong). After a send, the marker is written with a 7-day TTL; if that write fails the message is still acked and the failure logged. Each outcome is logged once, by the consumer: the template, the message id, the attempt and the error's name and `code`, never its message (a binding error may name the recipient), the address or the props. `CloudflareEmailSender`, `DevEmailSender` and `deliverEmail` no longer log a failure themselves.
- **2026-10-03 · The welcome email (E-01, ruling 8): verified once, from the hooks.** `databaseHooks.user.create.after` sends it when the new user is already verified (a code or magic-link sign-up, a social sign-up). `update.before` sees only the change, `update.after` only the new row, so `before` decides whether the update verifies an unverified address and records it under the endpoint context both calls share (a `WeakMap`); `after` sends it. The decision is: `emailVerified: true` without `email` (every Better Auth path that writes only the flag checks first that the address is unverified: the link, the magic link, the code reset, social sign-in over an unproven account, account linking), or with `email` when that address belongs to a user who is still unverified (a code verification writes the address too: a verified one is a repeat, and an address nobody has is an email change, not a first verification). An update outside an endpoint (our own code) never sends it. The key is `welcome:<userId>`. [Fix round 1, I4] The consumer's KV check is a best-effort filter, and delivery is at least once: KV caches a missing key for up to a minute and consumers run concurrently, so two messages with one key a few seconds apart can both be sent, and two concurrent clicks on the stateless verify link both pass Better Auth's check and both enqueue the welcome. A duplicate welcome is harmless; an atomic once-per-user claim in D1 is a PROGRESS carry, for after task 3's migration. Producers in tasks 4 and 5 must get correctness from D1 state and use the key only as a filter. The sign-in-only server never sends it, and rows inserted by SQL (the seed, the phase 8 import) run no hook. A failed hand-off is logged `[auth] Failed to queue the welcome email:` and never fails the sign-up or the verification. The locale is the account's, else the request's. Alternatives: a `welcomed_at` column now (deferred: a migration while task 3 adds one in parallel; it is the PROGRESS carry), or sending on every verified update and relying on the 7-day key alone (rejected: a re-verification a week later would welcome again). Pinned by `welcome.test.ts` on each Better Auth 1.7.6 path that verifies an existing account (the link, a code, the magic link, the reset by code, account linking) and by an update that matches no row (`updated?.emailVerified`, M1).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I1] A failed hand-off fails the auth request.** Better Auth 1.7.6 sends the code, the reset and the sign-up verification through `runInBackgroundOrAwait`, which catches and logs a failed send even without a `backgroundTasks` handler, so the endpoint answered 200 and the user waited for an email that was never queued. Now: `createAuth` has no `waitUntil` option and no `backgroundTasks` handler (every hand-off is awaited); `send` marks a failure in a Better Auth request state (`defineRequestState` from `@better-auth/core/context`, a new direct dependency at the same 1.7.6; one state spans the hooks and the endpoint), and an `after` hook turns it into `503 EMAIL_NOT_SENT`. The magic link and `/send-verification-email` await their send and fail on their own. `send-failure.test.ts` covers every sending endpoint. The welcome hook still catches its own failure: it never fails a verification. Timing trade-off: the response now waits for the hand-off (one `Queue.send`, a few ms; up to about 2.1 s when the queue is failing and the producer retries), so a request for an existing account and one for an unknown address can differ by that much. That difference is far smaller than the D1 work that already differs between those branches, and Turnstile plus `RL_AUTH` (5 per minute per IP outside dev) make a statistical probe impractical; an error the user can act on is worth more.
- **2026-10-03 · The email layout: the logo, the footer and the plain text.** [Fix round 1, M2, M6] The logo's alt text is white and in the brand size, for clients that block images (Outlook's default). The gestures of the admin email are one block each instead of `<br />` lines. The header shows the logo, which `@smog/brand` now renders as `apps/site/public/brand/email-logo.png` (369 × 80, the white logo on the brand green, opaque, shown 40 px tall), because email clients do not show SVG. It is loaded from the site (`renderEmail(…, { siteUrl })`); without a site URL the header keeps the text wordmark. The organisation's footer sits under the card: "© <year> SMOG & CO vzw · België" (fr "asbl · Belgique", en "Belgium") and the tagline "SMOG — Spreken Met Ondersteuning van Gebaren", which keeps the Dutch name in every language with a translation in brackets (inventory §4). The admin preview's policy now allows images from `<origin>/brand/` only (`emailPreviewCsp(origin)`), so the logo shows there too. Details rows (`DetailsBox`) are tables; html-to-text runs table cells together ("GestureHond"), so a label cell carries `data-email-label` and a custom formatter adds ": " (French: a no-break space before the colon) in the plain-text part.
- **2026-10-03 · Email copy (nl canonical).** The old Dutch subjects are kept where the old email existed: "Betaling bevestigd — bedankt!", "Je sponsoring is nu live!", "Je SMOG-sponsoring verloopt binnenkort", "We hebben je sponsoring ontvangen", "Nieuwe sponsoring van {name}" (the display name; "Sponsoring verlengd door {name}" for a renewal; [fix round 1, M6] French "Nouveau parrainage : {name}", so no elision is needed before a vowel, and the French reminder says "pour {gesture}", since "de le geste" is wrong). New ones: "Video maken mislukt voor {gesture}", "Terugbetaling nodig voor betaling {paymentId}", "Welkom bij SMOG & Co". A gesture in a sentence is a noun phrase, `email.common.gestureNamed` ("het gebaar ‘Hond’") or the fallback `email.common.yourGesture` ("je gebaar", bug 28); in a details row the value is the name or the capitalised fallback. Dates are long dates in Europe/Brussels (`formatDate`) and amounts `formatMoney` from integer cents; a non-integer amount or a non-ISO date is an `EmailRenderError`. The render-failed email shows at most 300 characters of the error, cut again in the template. The VAT number shows as "BE 0123.456.749". The invoice email falls back to the sponsor's email when the invoice has none. Keys chosen from an enum come from maps (`KIND_KEYS`, `REASON_KEYS`), never template literals.

## Sponsorships core (phase 6 task 3)

- **2026-10-03 · The state machine is a pure table plus one writer.** `server/transition-table.ts` holds `transition(status, event)` (spec §5.5 and ruling 6; every pair not listed is `null`) and is bun-tested pair by pair (10 statuses × 21 events, against an independent list of the allowed pairs). `server/transition.ts` is the only writer of `sponsorship.status`: `transitionStatements` returns `[failWhen(status ≠ from), update, sponsorship_event]` (guard `sponsorship-stale`, `isStaleTransition`), refuses a pair the table refuses (`InvalidTransitionError`), a patch with `status`/`updated_at`, and event data that fails `SPONSORSHIP_EVENT_DATA_SCHEMAS` (strict per type, as the audit writer: an unknown key is refused; dates are ISO strings). `eventStatement` writes only the trail-only events (`created`, `render_started`, `refund_needed`, `token_issued`; never `legacy`). The scan that keeps it so is `src/no-direct-status.test.ts`, a bun test, not `test/` as the plan wrote: the Workers pool cannot read the repository. It scans `apps`, `packages` and `scripts` (tests excluded: fixtures insert rows in any state) for a Drizzle `.update(sponsorship)` or raw `UPDATE sponsorship SET` that sets `status`, asserts it finds exactly the one in `transition.ts`, and also forbids `parseFloat(`/`Number(` in the package.
- **2026-10-03 · `settlePayment(db, { payment, now })` returns what to enqueue, and `settleFromMollie` re-fetches.** The brief's signature, plus `settleFromMollie(db, mollie, { mollieId, now })` (the webhook's and the status poll's path: `GET` then settle; `null` for a Mollie 404 or a payment that is not ours, found by `mollie_id` or, when that was never stored, by `metadata.paymentId`, which then fills `mollie_id`). The result carries `events` (`payment.settled` for `paid`, `already`, `late_revived`, and a `refund_needed` that revived some items, which still need their render) and `notify` (the `admin_refund_needed` emails, keyed `admin_refund_needed:<paymentId>:<adminId>`). Nothing is enqueued here, so this task merges no producer (task 1's rule). Every write is guarded by the payment status it read (`paymentGuard`, `payment-stale`); a lost race is read again once. Each re-fetch stores `amountRefunded` (`refunded_at` keeps the first time).
- **2026-10-03 · Late paid (D-LATEPAID): one batch per revived item, then the payment.** Each `cancelled` item is revived (`revived`) in its own guarded batch, so the partial unique index decides per item (`isGestureTaken`: D1's `UNIQUE constraint failed: sponsorship.gesture_id`) and one taken gesture does not undo the others. The final batch sets the payment `paid` (all revived: `late_revived`) or `refund_needed` (any not: a `refund_needed` event on those items and the admin email), guarded by the payment's old status; a re-run counts items already back in a blocking status as revived, so it is idempotent. A renewal paid late still renews while the sponsorship is `live`/`expiring` (`late_revived`); an ended one is `refund_needed`.
- **2026-10-03 · An amount mismatch also releases the gestures.** Ruling 6 says `refund_needed` plus the admin email; on an `open` initial payment the items are also `cancelled` (`{ reason: "mismatch" }`) in the same batch. Otherwise they would stay `awaiting_payment` forever: the stale sweep only looks at `open` payments. Alternative: leave them (rejected: a blocked gesture nobody can buy).
- **2026-10-03 · "Paid twice" is read from the trail.** Mollie `paid` for a payment that is already `paid` is `refund_needed` (`double`) when its items carry a `marked_paid_manually` (or `renewed` with `manual: true`) event for that payment; otherwise it is `already`. A payment marked paid by hand keeps its `paid_at`. No column was added.
- **2026-10-03 · Availability is one read with a constant status list and no ORDER BY.** `json_each(?)` joined to `gesture` by primary key (published only) and to `sponsorship` with `status IN (<the blocking statuses as literals>)`, so SQLite can match the partial unique index; the plan test asserts no scan and no temp B-tree (the ids are put back in the order asked in TS). A fourth state, `unavailable`, covers unknown and unpublished gestures (bug 39), which ruling 13's three states do not name; the CTA hides itself for it. Outputs use epoch milliseconds, as the admin's do.
- **2026-10-03 · The guards are declared per procedure and enforced in one middleware.** Each contract slice exports its guards (`{ rateLimit: "RL_API" | "RL_SPONSOR", turnstile }`), `SPONSORSHIP_PROCEDURE_GUARDS` merges them (`satisfies` the whole contract, so a procedure without guards fails `check-types`), and `sponsorshipProcedure` runs one guard middleware that fails closed on an unknown path, the `adminGuard` pattern. `RL_SPONSOR` is keyed `<ip>:<path>` as `rateLimit`, then Turnstile as `requireTurnstile` (skipped without `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`). The router test checks every procedure both ways. The typed errors are `SPONSORSHIP_ERRORS`: `GESTURE_UNAVAILABLE { gestureIds }`, `INVALID_STATE { reason }` (`INVALID_STATE_REASONS`, which includes the admin's `stale`, `noVideo`, `paid`, `notRefunded`, `tooMany` so task 6 reuses it) and `TOKEN_EXPIRED { expiresAt }`; copy under `sponsorship.errors.*`.
- **2026-10-03 · The Mollie description lives in `sponsorship.mollie.*`.** Ruling 2 names `sponsor.mollie.description`, but `sponsor.*` is task 8's block; keeping it in this task's block avoids a shared edit. A renewal gets its own line (`sponsorship.mollie.renewal`). The ruling's "info" tone for `awaiting_payment` is the kit Badge's `primary` (the kit has no info variant).
- **2026-10-03 · The render seam's switch lives with the services.** `renderStarterFor(mode, { db })` is `fakeRenderStarter` (calls `completeRender` with the job input's `sourcePlaybackId` and no asset) for `fake`, else `pendingRenderStarter(mode)`; task 4's `worker/render.ts` only reads `RENDER_MODE` and calls it, and phase 7 replaces that one function. Job creation is guarded in the batch (`sponsorship-stale`, `render-active`), so two creators make one job (`createRenderJob` answers `null` for the loser); `completeRender`/`failRender` are guarded by `render-final` and are no-ops on a final job. A job that finishes after its sponsorship left `rendering` is recorded without a transition (logged).
- **2026-10-03 · The admin builders read, check and build; the Mollie calls stay with the admin service.** Each builder (`approveStatements`, `rejectStatements`, `requestChangesStatements`, `regenerateTokenStatements`, `markPaidStatements`, `cancelPaymentStatements`, `forceExpireStatements`, `recordRefundStatements`) reads the row, throws `SponsorshipActionError` (`notFound`, or an `INVALID_STATE` reason: `stale` when the status does not allow it, `noVideo`, `notRefunded`, `notRenewable`) and returns `{ statements, after }` for one batch the admin appends its audit entry to. The re-fetch and cancel at Mollie before mark paid and cancel (ruling 14) are task 6's service, around these. Request changes writes `changes_requested` and `token_issued`; regenerating needs `changes_requested` (re-edit, 7 days) or `expiring` (renewal, until `ends_at`). Mark paid on a renewal renews (`renewed`, `manual: true`). Force expire returns the sponsored `muxAssetId` to delete (never the gesture's own).
- **2026-10-03 · The empty client slice files are knip-ignored until task 8.** `use-checkout`, `use-payment-status`, `use-reedit`, `use-renewal` and `use-logo-upload` exist (so tasks 4–8 edit disjoint files) but export nothing yet; `knip.json` ignores those five paths, and task 8 removes the entry when it fills them.
- **2026-10-03 · No migration.** Migration 0000's partial unique index, `(gesture_id, status)` and the `render_job`/`sponsorship_event` indexes serve every read here. (Superseded for chargebacks by fix round 1, I-3: migration 0009.)
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-1] Every settle re-derives what to enqueue.** A `refund_needed` payment (first run or any retry) answers `refund_needed` with `events` = `payment.settled` while any item is past payment (`rendering` … `expiring`) and `notify` = the `admin_refund_needed` emails, rebuilt from the stored `refund_needed` events (the latest reason; for `late` the flagged items' `payment_item` share, Minor 4; otherwise Mollie's amount). "Paid twice" now flags every item, so its reason is stored too. `failRender` on a job that already failed answers `failed` with the same keyed emails and the stored error. So a caller that fails after the commit and is retried loses nothing; the idempotency keys make resends safe. Task 4's webhook therefore enqueues every event and email of the result whatever the outcome (503 on a failed enqueue), and its `payment.settled` consumer acts only on items past payment (PROGRESS). A late payment's revivals and final batch can each lose a race, so `settlePayment` reads again up to 3 attempts in all, and logs the last error (Minor 7).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-2] The status scan is structural, with an allowlist.** `src/no-direct-status.test.ts` flags any update or upsert of `sponsorship` outside `WRITERS` (a map of path → reason): Drizzle `.update(sponsorship)` (aliased via `sponsorship as x`, or namespaced), `onConflictDo(Update|Nothing)` after `.insert(sponsorship)`, ``sql`UPDATE ${sponsorship}` ``, and raw `UPDATE` / `INSERT OR REPLACE` / `REPLACE INTO` / `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` on the table (quoted, aliased or schema-qualified). Only `server/transition.ts` may write `status`; any other allowlisted file (task 5's purge clearing `logo_key`) is checked column by column. The reviewer's shapes are detector tests.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-3] Chargebacks are recorded and told, never acted on.** `@smog/payments` parses `amountChargedBack` (`amountChargedBackCents`; the fake gains `chargeback()`). Migration 0009 adds `payment.charged_back_cents` (default 0) and `charged_back_at` with `ADD COLUMN` only, safe on staging's rows. A settle that sees more charged back than stored writes it (guarded, so concurrent settles record it once), notes it on each item as `refund_needed` `{ reason: "chargeback" }` (the trail is the audit record: it is not an admin action, and `audit_log`/`sponsorship_event` types are CHECKed, so no new type without a table rebuild), and every settle while one exists emails the admins with the refund-needed template and reason `chargeback` (key `admin_chargeback:<paymentId>:<cents>:<adminId>`, so each new amount is told once). Reusing the template adds one value to its `reason` union, less churn for task 2 than a ninth template. The payment stays `paid` and the sponsorship is not cancelled: the admin decides through the existing force-expire (or reject) path; cancel takes open payments only (corrected in task 6 fix round 1, M7). Task 6's admin shows the chargeback (PROGRESS).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, Minors]** The renewal year is added in SQL (`coalesce(ends_at, now) + 365 d`), so two renewals settling together add two years (1). Request changes on a `rejected` sponsorship whose gesture was sponsored again is `INVALID_STATE gestureTaken` (pre-checked; callers map `isGestureTaken` for a race) (2). `recordRefundStatements` refuses (`stale`) a Mollie payment that is not the payment's own (3). A renewal link is never regenerated once `ends_at` has passed (`notRenewable`) (5). `completeRender` answers `noop` when it only closed the job (6). The display name refuses control characters and line separators (8). `TokenPlan`'s raw token and URL are for the copy dialog only; a test checks the statements, the trail, the token rows and the logs never contain it, and task 6 must not put it in audit data (9). `settlePayment` has `Promise.all` race tests: paid ∥ paid, paid ∥ admin cancel, late ∥ late with a taken gesture, two renewals (10).

## The money path (phase 6 task 4)

- **2026-10-03 · Procedures reach the queues and R2 through `RpcEnv`.** `RpcEnv` gains optional `EMAIL_QUEUE`, `EVENTS_QUEUE` and `MEDIA` (`RpcBindings` in `@smog/rpc`), which the site's `createRpcContext` fills from `cloudflare:workers`. The checkout verifies the logo in `MEDIA`, and `paymentStatus` enqueues a settle's outputs; tasks 5 and 6 (re-edit, renewal, mark paid) need the same. Rejected: a bindings resolver in `SponsorshipsDeps` or `AdminDeps` (fix round 1, I-4, corrected reason: `@smog/api` *can* read the Worker env lazily, as task 6 showed, but a resolver adds a second way in, ties the api package to `cloudflare:workers`, and tests would have to mock that module instead of passing bindings in the context) and a per-request `appRouter` factory (every transport and the SSR client would change). `context.env` is the single source of bindings for every procedure; a missing one is logged and answered cleanly.
- **2026-10-03 · [dep] `aws4fetch` 1.0.20 for the presigned logo PUT.** Web Crypto only, runs on workerd; the deploy guard already keeps it out of `dist/client`. It never signs `content-type` or `content-length` unless asked, so the presign passes `aws: { signQuery: true, allHeaders: true }` with both headers: `X-Amz-SignedHeaders=content-length;content-type;host`, and R2 refuses another type or another size than the one declared to `uploadLogo` (at most 2 MiB) with a 403 (ruling 10; fix round 1, I-2a). The browser sets `Content-Length` from the file itself, so the returned `headers` stay `{ content-type }`; check the size binding against R2 on staging once its tokens exist (PROGRESS). Region `auto`, service `s3`, `X-Amz-Expires` 300.
- **2026-10-03 · The signed fallback upload.** The path holds the uuid of `logos/<uuid>` (one segment: `PUT /api/logos/upload/<uuid>?exp=&sig=`). The HMAC (key: HKDF-SHA-256 of `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, empty salt, `info: smog-logo-upload`) covers the key, the `Content-Type` and `exp`, so the fallback enforces the signed type exactly as the presigned URL does, with no type parameter in the URL. The route also checks the magic bytes, so a renamed GIF is refused at once; the checkout checks them again because a presigned PUT never passes through the Worker. The route is limited by `RL_SPONSOR` per IP and takes one upload per URL (a `head` first, then `put` with `onlyIf: { etagDoesNotMatch: "*" }`; a second PUT is a 409), so a URL cannot be replayed into repeated 2 MiB writes (fix round 1, M-5). The admin read adds `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, `private, no-store` and `nosniff`, and echoes only one of the three image types (anything else is `application/octet-stream` with `content-disposition: attachment`) (M-4).
- **2026-10-03 · The checkout's order.** No key, then the replay (a repeat of the `checkoutId` answers before anything else, so a double click never fails on a total or a logo that the first call already used), then the price, the publish check, the logo, the batch and Mollie. The payment row is the batch's first statement, so a concurrent repeat fails on `payment.id` and is answered as a replay (a repeat that loses on the gesture index first is read the same way). A replay of an open payment without a checkout URL (the first call is still at Mollie) creates again with the same `Idempotency-Key`, which Mollie answers with the same payment. A replay must ask for what is stored: the same gestures (sorted) and the same total, else `INVALID_STATE alreadySettled` (fix round 1, M-3; the wizard makes a new id when the selection or the logo changes; no new reason, so no new copy). The publish check is also an in-batch guard (`gesture-unpublished`: the count of published gestures among the ids), so an unpublish between the read and the batch fails the whole checkout as `GESTURE_UNAVAILABLE` (M-2).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-2b] The checkout copies the verified logo to a fresh key.** `claimLogo` reads the uploaded object once (at most 2 MiB), checks its declared type and its magic bytes on exactly those bytes, and writes them to a new `logos/<uuid>` that no client has a URL for; that key is the one stored on the sponsorships. The upload key is deleted once the batch committed, and the copy is deleted when the batch fails (so a retry with another gesture can use the same upload). A still-valid upload URL (presigned or fallback) can then only write an orphan that the purge removes, never the bytes the render reads, and two checkouts can no longer share one object. An invalid upload is deleted at once.
- **2026-10-03 · The `paymentStatus` throttle.** KV `sponsorships:status-refetch:<paymentId>` holds the time of the last re-fetch with KV's minimum TTL (60 s); a poll within 5 s of it does not call Mollie. It is best effort (a KV get then put: neither atomic nor consistent across locations, so concurrent polls from different edges may each re-fetch; fix round 1, M-7), and it only spares Mollie: D1 decides everything. A Mollie failure answers the stored status, and an enqueue failure is logged, not thrown: the webhook re-derives the outputs.
- **2026-10-03 · The webhook's answers.** The id is read first, so a bad body is a 400 even without a key. A body over 4 KiB is a 413 and counts like a 400. Every output of the settle is enqueued with `onFailure: "throw"`, so a queue that stays down (or is not bound) is a 503 and Mollie's retry resends; a settle that throws is a 500. Mollie's 404 (counted) and "not ours" (not counted) are both 200. A 2xx has an empty body, with the outcome in `x-smog-webhook` (fix round 1, M-8); an error answers `{ code }`. A verified id is never limited, so its payer could replay it at will: an `already` outcome re-enqueues at most once a minute per payment (KV `mollie:fanout:<paymentId>`, 60 s, written only after a successful enqueue, so a 503's retry always resends) (M-1).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-4] One way to the queues: `context.env` and `@smog/jobs`' `enqueueOutputs`.** `enqueueOutputs` and the queue types (`JobQueues`, `Outputs`) live in `@smog/jobs` (ruling 1: it produces the messages), not in `@smog/sponsorships`. Every feature puts a service's `events` and `notify` on the queues with it: `{ email: context.env.EMAIL_QUEUE, events: context.env.EVENTS_QUEUE }` in procedures, the Worker bindings in the webhook and the consumers. `onFailure: "throw"` where the caller can be retried (the webhook, the consumer), the default `log` elsewhere (admin actions, polls, crons; ruling 8). Task 6 drops its `AdminDeps.queues`, `enqueueAfter` and `@smog/api`'s `queues.ts` for this when it merges. A swallowed enqueue (mark paid, a poll, a re-edit) is recovered by task 5's reconciliation in the hourly stale sweep (PROGRESS).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-1] The `payment.settled` emails have a 6-day window.** A refund or chargeback keeps Mollie's status `paid`, so its webhook settles `already` and fans out again, possibly weeks later, when the email consumer's 7-day KV markers are gone. `handlePaymentSettled` therefore builds the emails only while `now − paid_at` is under 6 days (the 7-day marker minus a day); the render jobs and `render.requested` are re-checked whatever the age. Every real retry falls inside the window (the events DLQ after about 10 h, Mollie's retries for about a day). A column recording "sent" was rejected (a migration, and a crash between the claim and the enqueue would lose the emails). For task 3's owner: `settlePayment` could also stop returning `payment.settled` for `already` once `paid_at` is old.
- **2026-10-03 · The `payment.settled` consumer is derived from D1 every time.** It acts on a `paid` or `refund_needed` payment's items past payment, minus the items flagged `late`/`mismatch` for it; `double` and `chargeback` flags keep their fan-out (those sponsorships are real). Render jobs: `createRenderJob`'s in-batch guards make one job however many messages run; `render.requested` goes out again for every item's `queued` job, so a message lost after the job was written is recovered by the next `payment.settled` (the start acts on a queued job only). Emails: re-derived with their keys on every run; the email consumer's KV marker filters repeats (at least once, ruling 8). A D1 claim for "emails sent" was rejected: it needs a migration, and a crash between claim and enqueue would lose the emails. `admin_new_sponsorship` lists only the items that count, with their total. A renewal sends only `payment_confirmed` (the brief), although the admin template has a renewal variant.
- **2026-10-03 · `readCappedBody` in `@smog/utils`.** The webhook (4 KiB) and the logo upload (2 MiB) read bodies through one capped reader (`content-length` checked first, the stream counted and cancelled past the cap, a dropped client a 400). `@smog/video` and `@smog/analytics` keep their own copies for now (PROGRESS carry), outside this task's files.

## Lifecycle jobs and token flows (phase 6 task 5)

## Admin sponsorships API and export (phase 6 task 6)

- **2026-10-03 · The sponsorship operations reach the admin through `AdminDeps`, built in `@smog/api/src/admin-deps.ts`.** `AdminDeps.sponsorships` (`AdminSponsorshipServices`) is the task 3 builders (`approve`, `reject`, `requestChanges`, `regenerateToken`, `markPaid`, `cancelPayment`, `forceExpire`, `recordRefund`), `settle` (`settlePayment`) and `refusalOf`, which maps `SponsorshipActionError` to its reason, a fired batch guard (`isStaleTransition`, `isStalePayment`) to `stale` and the partial unique index (`isGestureTaken`) to `gestureTaken`. The admin types them structurally and never imports `@smog/sponsorships/server` (ruling 1). The Mollie "client factory" is `AdminDeps.mollieFetch` (as `muxFetch`): `@smog/api` may not import `@smog/payments`, so the admin builds the client with `createMollie(context.env, { fetch })`. The admin tests import `admin-deps.ts` by relative path, so they run the exact production wiring.
- **2026-10-03 · The queue bindings come in as `AdminDeps.queues()`.** The rpc context carries no queue bindings, so `@smog/api/src/queues.ts` reads `EMAIL_QUEUE` and `EVENTS_QUEUE` from `cloudflare:workers` `env` when an action enqueues; the tests record the messages instead. A missing binding is logged and the committed change stands; an enqueue failure is retried by the producer, then logged and swallowed (ruling 8). Alternative: add the queues to `RpcContext` (rejected: `@smog/rpc` is shared with tasks 4 and 5 in this wave). **Merge order:** this task produces `payment.settled` (consumer: task 4's events queue) and `sponsorship_live` (consumer: task 2, merged), so it merges after task 4.
- **2026-10-03 · One batch per action; payment actions write one entry per sponsorship.** Every action appends its `auditStatement`s to the builder's statements and commits once, so the transition, its event and the entries land together or not at all (the `adminProcedure` guard also counts the `audit_log` inserts D1 ran). Mark paid and cancel write one entry per sponsorship of the payment (`{ paymentId }`); record refund targets the payment; the export is a `system` entry. A link's raw token is never in an entry or a log: request changes and regenerate store only the expiry (and the purpose). Approve's entry has the dates computed from the same `now` the builder got, with `SPONSORSHIP_DURATION_DAYS`.
- **2026-10-03 · Mark paid when Mollie already has the money: settled, then audited after.** Ruling 14 says such a payment "settles normally": `settlePayment` runs its own batches, so the `sponsorship.mark_paid` entries (`source: "mollie"`) are written in a batch right after it, not inside it. The settlement is the webhook's idempotent one, not an admin change, so an entry that failed after it would lose only the record of the click. The trail then shows `payment_paid`, not `marked_paid_manually`, so a later webhook is not "paid twice". Otherwise the payment is cancelled at Mollie while `isCancelable` (a failure is `INVALID_STATE paymentProvider`, nothing written) and marked paid by hand. Without a Mollie key or a Mollie id the action is local only. Cancel settles a payment Mollie reports `paid` and answers `INVALID_STATE paid`.
- **2026-10-03 · Force expire deletes the asset with a plain `DELETE` in the admin.** `@smog/video` has no asset delete yet and task 5 (the expiry sweep) adds one in the same wave; to keep `packages/video` theirs, the admin sends `DELETE /video/v1/assets/<id>` with the Mux client's credentials (204 or 404 is done; anything else is logged, never thrown). PROGRESS carries the switch to `@smog/video`'s function once both are merged.
- **2026-10-03 · "Needs the admin" is one SQL rule for the dashboard and the list.** `paymentNeedsAdmin`: a `refund_needed` payment with no refund recorded yet (`refunded_cents = 0`), or a chargeback (`charged_back_cents > 0`) while one of its sponsorships still has a blocking status. A recorded refund (partial for a `late` payment, whose refund is only the flagged share) or a cancelled / expired sponsorship ends it. Alternative: `refunded_cents < amount_cents` (rejected: a correct partial refund would stay flagged for ever). The list filter is `refundNeeded: boolean` and each row carries `refundNeeded`, for task 7's "Refund needed" tab; the detail shows the chargeback fields and the `refund_needed` trail entries.
- **2026-10-03 · The list: one new index, the counts in the same batch.** Migration `0010_sponsorship_admin_order` adds `sponsorship_created_id_idx (created_at, id)` (CREATE INDEX only, safe on existing rows; the plan's name `0009_…` was taken by task 3's chargebacks). The query-plan test asserts a scan or seek of that index with no sort; status filters are applied while it scans (statuses are a large share of a small table; a `(status, created_at)` index cannot serve a multi-status page without a sort). `counts` group by status under every filter but `status`. Joined selects inside a D1 batch alias their same-named columns (`aliased()`): Drizzle's D1 batch reads rows as objects keyed by column name, so `sponsorship.id` and `gesture.id` collapse and every later field shifts.
- **2026-10-03 · The CSV export.** The 18 old columns in order; "Payment amount (€)", "Mollie payment ID" and "Has logo" come from the checkout's (`initial`) payment item (`payment_item.includes_logo` survives the logo purge); "Duration (years)" is the rounded span of the stored dates. Every field is quoted, CRLF rows, and the formula guard prefixes `'`. It reads in keyset pages of 500, oldest first, serialising each page before the next ("stream it": at most one page of rows is held), counts first and refuses over 50,000 rows (`tooMany`), and has no 10,000 cap. It stays an oRPC procedure returning `{ csv, filename, rows }` (the plan's interface): an HTTP stream would need a non-oRPC route and could not report `rows` to the audit entry before the body ends. No BOM is added (the old export had none); task 7 may prepend one at download.
- **2026-10-03 · Smaller choices.** `logoUrl` is `/api/logos/<uuid>` (the key without `logos/`, one path segment for the `$key` route; `sponsorLogoUrl`). `mollieDashboardUrl` is `https://my.mollie.com/dashboard/payments/<tr_id>` (Mollie redirects it to the organisation's page; we do not store `_links.dashboard`). Input refinements (a reversed date range, a repeated status) answer oRPC's `BAD_REQUEST` like the other admin inputs; a foreign cursor is `VALIDATION`. The user panel lists at most 100 sponsorships, by the account-export rule (a verified email, compared case-insensitively).

- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I1] Settle paths write the admin's entries in the settlement's own batch.** `SettleInput` gains `extra?: Statement[]` (task 3's `settle.ts`, backward compatible): every path writes them with its final batch, alone when nothing else changes (`noop`, `already`), and a lost-race retry reuses them (the guard counts an insert only when its batch resolved). Mark paid on a payment Mollie reports `paid` passes its `sponsorship.mark_paid` `{ source: "mollie", note? }` entries; cancel on such a payment passes `sponsorship.cancel` `{ paymentId, refused: "paid" }`, then answers `INVALID_STATE paid` (the one handler that writes and then throws; the guard's comment says so). Mark paid answers the real outcome: `result: "refund_needed"` when the settlement flagged the payment. This replaces the earlier "settled, then audited after" entry above.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I2] Commit locally, then cancel at Mollie (best effort).** Mark paid and cancel re-fetch Mollie first (a `paid` payment is settled instead), commit their batch, and only then cancel the Mollie payment while `isCancelable`; a failure is logged (`[admin] Failed to cancel the Mollie payment …`), never thrown. Cancelling first let Mollie's `canceled` webhook win the race (or follow a failed batch) and cancel a bank-transferred payment the admin was marking paid. Now that webhook finds a final payment and changes nothing; a customer who pays in between is ruling 6's "paid twice" (`refund_needed`) or late paid (revived).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I3, M4] The CSV formula guard and the BOM.** A cell gets the leading `'` when it starts with a tab, CR or LF, or when its first character after any leading whitespace (every Unicode space, the BOM and the zero-width spaces) is `=`, `+`, `-`, `@` or U+2212, read after NFKC (which folds `＝ ＋ － ＠ ﹢`). Legacy rows (phase 8) never passed the trimming schemas, and LibreOffice and Sheets may trim on import. The CSV now starts with a UTF-8 BOM so Excel reads the euro sign and accents; `sponsorshipsCsvFile` (`@smog/admin/client`) keeps it byte for byte.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, M1, M2] Pinned plans.** The status filter is written `+status IN (…)`: the unary `+` keeps SQLite from picking `sponsorship_status_ends_at_idx` plus a sort on fresh statistics (the Review tab did), so every tab scans `sponsorship_created_id_idx` in order; the plan test covers the status, multi-status and combined filters. The user panel reads the account's address first and seeks a new `sponsor_email_lower_idx` (`lower(email)`), added to the still-unmerged migration 0010 (it was never applied anywhere, so this is not an edit of a merged migration).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, M3] The export keeps its cap and is held whole.** Streaming would need a non-oRPC route; the cap bounds it instead: at 50,000 rows about 20 MB of text, held as UTF-16 because of the `€`, plus the JSON answer's copy, well under the Worker's 128 MB, and sponsorships grow by at most one blocking row per gesture. Revisit with a streamed route (counting first, so the audit entry still has `rows`) if the cap is ever raised.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, M8, I4] Smaller fixes.** Mark paid writes `changed: false` on the entry of an item it did not move (one already past payment). The client hooks exist now: `useAdminSponsorships`, `useAdminSponsorship`, `useAdminSponsorshipActions` (every action refetches the admin and public gesture queries when it settles), `useAdminSponsorshipsExport`, `sponsorshipRefusalOf` and `sponsorshipsCsvFile`.

- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, after task 4] One way to the queues; mark paid fails loudly.** `AdminDeps.queues`, `AdminQueues` and `packages/api/src/queues.ts` are gone: the admin reads `EMAIL_QUEUE`/`EVENTS_QUEUE` from `context.env` (task 4's `RpcEnv`) and enqueues with `@smog/jobs`' `enqueueOutputs`, as every feature does. This supersedes the "queue bindings come in as `AdminDeps.queues()`" entry above. Mark paid (by hand, or settled because Mollie had the money) enqueues with `onFailure: "throw"`: when `payment.settled` cannot be enqueued the committed change stands, the call fails (logged) so the admin sees it, and task 5's reconciliation sweep re-sends `payment.settled` for a paid payment with an item in `rendering` and no job. Every other action logs a failed enqueue and still answers (ruling 8). The detail's `logoUrl` was checked against task 4's route: it serves `logos/<id>` for a 36-character id, and the stored key is the claimed `logos/<uuid>` copy, so `/api/logos/<uuid>` is right.

## Admin sponsorships screens (phase 6 task 7)

## Public sponsor screens and the gesture CTA (phase 6 task 8)

- **2026-10-03 · The wizard state is one tested reducer in `@smog/sponsorships/client` (`use-checkout.ts`).** `wizardReducer` holds the step, the selection (each gesture with its name, slug and playback id, so steps 2 and 3 need no reads), the details as typed, the logo file and the ids the checkout answered `GESTURE_UNAVAILABLE` for (`taken`, never selectable again in that run). Toggling past 10 leaves the selection as it is and sets `limitReached` (the explanation shows); preselect applies only to an empty selection and only `available`, untaken gestures, at most 10; unticking the logo keeps the file, and `checkoutInput` sends the key only when the box is ticked. `validateDetails` checks the details with the schema's own `displayNameSchema`, `contactEmailSchema` and `normalizeBelgianVat` and answers one error code per field; the site maps codes to copy (key maps, the `Translate` rule). The price shown anywhere comes from `priceSponsorship` (the logo line is the difference of two calls, so no constant is multiplied twice). Alternatives: a form library (more weight for three steps), wizard state in the URL (S-01 says memory, and a URL would carry the contact details).
- **2026-10-03 · `checkoutId` per attempt.** One v4 UUID per wizard run; a double click or a retry after a timeout reuses it (ruling 5). `GESTURE_UNAVAILABLE` keeps it (nothing was written); `INVALID_STATE paymentProvider` and `alreadySettled` take a new one, since that payment can no longer be answered. The renewal page does the same with its own id.
- **2026-10-03 · `sponsorship_checkout_started` is tracked by `useCheckout` in the client package**, after `sponsorships.checkout` answers and before `onRedirect` (the site's `location.assign`), through `useAnalytics()` and so through the consent gate. The renewal does not send it: the event (spec §12) describes the wizard's checkout, and a renewal is a different funnel; a `sponsorship_renewal_started` can be added if the owner wants it.
- **2026-10-03 · `?gesture=` takes several slugs, comma separated.** Ruling 13 names `?gesture=<slug>`; the success page's "Try again" keeps a whole selection, so the same parameter takes `a,b` (as `?category=` does on `/gestures`), and repeated or JSON-array values TanStack parses are accepted too. Slugs are checked (`[a-z0-9-]`), deduplicated and capped at 10; each is resolved through `gestures.bySlug`, then `sponsorships.availability`, then preselected once.
- **2026-10-03 · R-11 is one D1 read in the Worker's redirect step.** `gestureIdParam` recognises `?gestureId=` on `/sponsors` and `/sponsor` (case and trailing slash ignored); `loadGestureSlug` answers the slug of the published gesture whose id, legacy id or slug it is (`SELECT slug FROM gesture WHERE published_at IS NOT NULL AND (id = ?1 OR legacy_id = ?1 OR slug = ?1) LIMIT 1`, three unique indexes), and the redirect is one 301 to `/sponsor?gesture=<slug>` with the other params kept in place. An unknown, unpublished or malformed value (not `[\w-]{1,120}`) and a failed lookup (logged) drop the param, so the wizard opens empty. It lives next to `loadCategorySlugs`, the other D1-backed rename. The plain `/sponsors/<rest>` prefix rule is unchanged.
- **2026-10-03 · The gesture CTA reads availability on the client only.** The card is the last item of the text column and never in the loader, so the gesture page (and its SSR) never waits on it, and its late mount moves nothing above it. Until the read answers, and when it fails, the detail's own `sponsor` credit (from `gestures.bySlug`) still shows, so an availability outage loses no credit; otherwise the card hides. `available` with `checkoutEnabled: false` and `unavailable` hide it. The old `gesture.sponsoredBy` line is gone from both apps (the key is left for now; no code reads it).
- **2026-10-03 · Mobile CTA and `SPONSOR_LINK_IN_APP`.** `apps/mobile/src/config.ts` exports `SPONSOR_LINK_IN_APP = true`; with `false`, iOS drops the `available` card (the only one with a link), while the pending and sponsored cards stay. The link opens `https://<site>/sponsor?gesture=<slug>` with `Linking.openURL` (the system browser, not an in-app browser, so the purchase is clearly outside the app). The phase 8 store-submission carry decides the value for the first review.
- **2026-10-03 · The preview is a still, drawn from the render contract (ruling 7).** `SponsorOverlayPreview` places the logo box and the two text lines with `RENDER_OVERLAY_LAYOUT` (percentages, and `cqh` for the font size in a size container); the green is the contract's brand colour, applied inline because it is the video's fixed colour, not a theme role. The value card "5 sec" is `RENDER_OVERLAY_LAYOUT.overlaySeconds` (bug 24).
- **2026-10-03 · The payment poll counts answers, not timers.** `usePaymentStatus` refetches every 2 s while the answer is `open` and fewer than 15 answers arrived in this round (`dataUpdateCount` minus the round's start); "Check again" starts a new round of 15. An unknown payment (`NOT_FOUND`) is final; a network error is retried 3 times. Interval and count are options so the component test runs in milliseconds.
- **2026-10-03 · The e2e wiring for checkout.** `playwright.config.ts` starts the Mollie fake (`E2E_MOLLIE_PORT`, 4020) and gives the dev server `MOLLIE_API_URL` and the fake's test key through `SMOG_DEV_MOLLIE_*` (`vite.config.ts`, dev only), so every e2e run has checkout enabled. `/dev/e2e-seed` gains `resetSponsorships { slugs }` (payments with their items, then the sponsorships with their events, tokens and jobs, then orphaned sponsors) and `sponsorship { id: e2e-…, gestureSlug, status, displayName, endsAt?, token? }`, which inserts a row in a state with its own sponsor and the SHA-256 of a raw token the spec keeps; rows are inserted, never updated, so the status scan stays meaningful. The spec probes `sponsorships.paymentStatus` and `reedit.get`: until tasks 4 and 5 merge they answer the stub's 500 and the paid, failed and re-edit tests skip with the task named. The admin-queue assertion waits for task 6 (a TODO in the spec; availability `pending` stands in).

- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-1] Step 1 reaches every gesture.** With no query the grid is `gestures.list` (keyset pages of 50) with "Load more", so every published gesture can be browsed and chosen, as the old wizard's full list allowed; a query uses `gestures.search` (one page of 50) and says "Showing 50 of N matches, search more precisely" when it matched more. Availability is read in chunks of 100 ids (`AVAILABILITY_IDS_MAX`, one `json_each` parameter each). The result line says "N available gestures shown". Rejected: one unbounded read of the catalogue (the old way; it grows with the catalogue) and only a "showing 50 of N" note (browsing would still miss gestures). Also recorded: step 2 no longer lists the chosen gestures as removable chips (the old wizard did); they are named on step 3 and changed by going back.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-2] Any edit after an attempt takes a new `checkoutId`.** "Continue to payment" dispatches `attempt` with the next id; the first toggle, details or logo change (or a `GESTURE_UNAVAILABLE` answer) after it switches to that id. A plain retry or a double click keeps the id (ruling 5's idempotency), but a repeat can no longer carry a different selection under an id whose open payment the server would answer with the old amount. Task 4 is asked (coordinator) to also compare a repeat's gestures and total with the stored payment.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-3] Page-level states are the page's h1.** The kit's `EmptyState` and `ErrorState` take `level` 1–4 (level 1 is sized `title-1`; tests); every page-level state passes 1: the paused wizard, the success page's missing and error states, every link guard, the re-edit "Sent!", and the root `NotFound`. The loading states render their h1 ("Checking your payment…", "Checking your link…") above the skeleton, so the page always has one. The e2e's `blockingViolations` now also blocks axe's moderate `page-has-heading-one`.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-4] One live region holds the success page's headline.** It always renders (`role="status"`, `aria-atomic`) and holds the current h1: "Checking…", "Processing…", then the outcome; the per-attempt counter is not live. A final outcome (paid, failed, refund_needed, the timeout) also moves the focus to its h1, and so does the re-edit's "Sent!".
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-5] A gesture card is named by the gesture and described by its badge** (`aria-labelledby` the name, `aria-describedby` the "Sponsored" / "In progress" / "Just taken" badge), so a disabled card says why. A chosen card that became blocked stays enabled until it is deselected (Minor 2). The selection bar alone announces the count (Minor 6).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-6, I-7] The e2e cannot stay skipped for good.** `apps/site/e2e/phase6.ts` holds `PHASE6_PENDING` (`checkout`, `reedit`, `adminQueue`). A flagged test skips only while its probe answers exactly the task 3 stub (`INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` "not implemented"); an implemented procedure fails the test until the merging task removes its flag, and any other 500 runs and fails it. `packages/features/sponsorships/src/phase6-pending.test.ts` fails once PROGRESS says "Phase 6: done" while a `notImplemented(` call or the flags remain (task 9 removes them). The paid test waits for both sponsorships to reach `in_review` through a dev-only seed read (`sponsorshipStatus`), which proves the webhook, the fan-out and the fake render; the admin-queue assertion is task 6's, and the spec throws once `adminQueue` is off without it. The spec resets its rows after the run (Minor 10) and runs axe on the paused, success, link and CTA states in light and dark (Minor 9).
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-8] The token pages send no Referer, and Workers Logs keep their URLs.** `/sponsor/edit` and `/sponsor/renew` answer `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (route `headers`) and carry `<meta name="referrer" content="no-referrer">` for a client navigation; a Workers test pins both. The site-wide `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` already kept the token from other origins; now same-site requests do not repeat it either. Accepted: `observability.enabled` makes Workers Logs record each request URL, raw token included. The token is single use, hashed at rest, and expires (7 days, or at `ends_at`), and the logs are visible to account members only. Workers Logs cannot drop query strings; `observability.logs.invocation_logs: false` in `wrangler.jsonc` would stop recording request URLs, but it drops every invocation's metadata (status, duration) too, so it is not set. The other way, moving the token out of the URL into a fragment the page reads, would change the email links tasks 2 and 5 already issue; noted for phase 9.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, I-9] Pay cannot run twice for one attempt.** `useCheckout` and `useRenewalCheckout` set `redirecting` when the checkout answers; Pay stays busy and the action is a no-op until the page leaves, or comes back from the bfcache (`pageshow` with `persisted`), so `sponsorship_checkout_started` fires once per attempt.
- **2026-10-03 · [Fix round 1, Minors]** The display name has `autocomplete="off"` (3). The logo error sits under the dropzone, the guidelines follow the field, and the Browse / "Choose another logo" button carries the field's description (4, 5). The timeline's done step reads "(completed)" (7). The price summary is an h2 (8). On iOS with `SPONSOR_LINK_IN_APP` off, the sponsored card drops "available again from {date}" too, a pointer to a later purchase (13). The renewal still sends no event; whether to add `sponsorship_renewal_started` is the owner's call before launch (PROGRESS) (1).

## Hardening, staging smoke and parity (phase 6 task 9)
